282 — Static Program Verifier

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a deductive static program verifier (a Dafny / Boogie / ESC-class tool): an annotated imperative source language with preconditions, postconditions, loop invariants, and assertions; a type checker; a Boogie-like intermediate verification language (IVL) of guarded commands; weakest-precondition verification-condition generation in passive (DSA) form; an SMT encoding (SMT-LIB) discharged by a pluggable SMT solver; and counterexample reporting mapped back to source locations. Prove an annotated program correct, or report a failing assertion with a concrete counterexample. It is a deductive verifier — not an SMT core (#278), a model checker (#279/#281), or a theorem prover (#280).

Project Number: 282

Project Name: Static Program Verifier

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal: Build a from-scratch deductive program verifier in Rust — spv — that parses an annotated imperative language, type-checks it, lowers it to a Boogie-like guarded-command IVL, generates a weakest-precondition VC in passive (DSA) form, discharges it via an SMT solver, and reconstructs source-level counterexamples; soundness is cardinal — never report "verified" for a buggy program.

1.1 Engineering definition (what is built)

A Dafny/Boogie/ESC-class deductive verifier as a 7-crate Rust workspace + the spv binary. Six-stage pipeline, each stage a separate crate, immutable-after-construction IR shared by ownership:

# Stage Crate Input → Output Locked core type
1 Parse spv-syntax source.spv → surface Program AST Program, Decl, Method, Function, Stmt, Expr, Spec, Type
2 Resolve + typecheck + spec well-formedness spv-ast Program → typed/resolved AST Type, TypeError
3 Desugar to guarded commands spv-ivl typed AST → IvlCmd IvlCmd, Reason, desugar_method
4 VC generation (wp) + passive form spv-vc IvlCmd → VcFormula VcFormula, wp, passify
5 SMT encode + emit spv-smt VcFormula → SmtTerm → SMT-LIB 2 SmtSort, SmtTerm, SmtSolver, SmtResult, Model
6 Discharge + map back spv-verify check(NOT VC) → VerifyResult VerifyResult, VError, VErrorKind, Counterexample
  • Source language (LOCKED surface): methods with requires/ensures/modifies/decreases; local VarDecl; Assign; If/While; method Call; Assert/Assume/Return; pure Functions; Quant (quantifiers + Triggers); Old() two-state expressions; Axiom.
  • Central equivalence (LOCKED): program correct iff its VC is valid iff (NOT VC) is UNSAT in SMT. verify = ask solver to refute negated VC. Unsat ⇒ Verified; Sat(Model) ⇒ VError + reconstructed Counterexample; Unknown ⇒ surfaced explicitly (timeout/incompleteness), never silently as verified.
  • wp calculus (LOCKED five rules): wp(assume p, Q)=p⇒Q; wp(assert p, Q)=p∧Q; wp(havoc x, Q)=∀x.Q; wp(x:=e, Q)=Q[e/x]; wp(c1;c2, Q)=wp(c1,wp(c2,Q)); wp(c1[]c2, Q)=wp(c1,Q)∧wp(c2,Q). Method VC = wp(desugar_method(m), true) after pre/post wrapping.
  • Loops not unrolled — invariant cut: prove invariant I on entry (assert I), preserved by one arbitrary iteration (havoc targets; assume I∧guard; body; assert I), I∧¬guard ⇒ post. Termination via decreases (well-founded variant) for total correctness.
  • Modular verification: a Call is verified against the callee contract, never its body — assert(callee.requires); havoc(modifies); assume(callee.ensures). This is what makes verification scale.
  • Passive form (DSA): naive wp over Assign+Choice is exponential (Q[e/x] duplicates Q). passify ⇒ dynamic single assignment (one assignment per version; assignments → assume of equalities), keeping VC linear-ish (Flanagan-Saxe 2001).

1.2 System boundaries

  • In: lexing, parsing, name resolution, type/spec checking, IVL desugaring, wp VC generation, DSA passification, VC simplification, SMT-LIB 2 encoding + trigger annotation, solver dispatch behind SmtSolver, Unsat/Sat/Unknown handling, counterexample reconstruction, VErrorKind classification, the spv CLI.
  • Out (hard boundary): the SMT solver's internals; program execution; codegen; concurrency/separation logic; a real language's full semantics; persistence; networking; a GUI.
  • Trust boundary: input source.spv is untrusted; the verifier must be memory-safe and never crash on malformed/adversarial annotated input — degrade to a precise ParseError/TypeError/VError, not a panic.

1.3 NON-goals — sibling portfolio projects out of scope

Concern Owned by spv stance
Competitive SMT solver internals #278 spv-smt talks to the SmtSolver trait + SMT-LIB only; reuses #278 or Z3 — never re-implements DPLL(T)/theory solvers
Explicit-state model checking #279 spv is deductive (VC over wp), not state-space exploration
Symbolic / bounded model checking #281 same — invariant cut, no path/state enumeration
General interactive theorem prover #280 spv discharges first-order VCs automatically via SMT, not tactic-driven proofs
Full separation logic, concurrency, real-language semantics, compilation/execution, GUI — explicitly excluded; heap is a Map model (§ Phase 12), single-threaded, no I/O

1.4 Deployment model & users

  • Form: embeddable no_std + alloc library crates (spv-syntax/spv-ast/spv-ivl/spv-vc/spv-smt/spv-verify) + one std binary spv.
  • spv subcommands (LOCKED): verify, print-ast, print-ivl, print-vc, print-smt — each surfaces one pipeline stage for inspection/regression.
  • Audiences: verification engineers (CI gate: methods must verify); PL/formal-methods researchers (a hackable wp/IVL/encoding testbed); students learning Hoare logic / weakest preconditions (observe a triple become a guarded command become a VC become an SMT query).

1.5 Phase-0 deliverable — the seven spv-* crates

  1. spv-syntax — Lexer, Token, Parser, surface AST (Program/Decl/Method/Function/Stmt/Expr/Spec/Type), ParseError/ParseErrorKind. no_std + alloc.
  2. spv-ast — name resolution + TypeChecker + spec well-formedness; owns Type, TypeError, the typed AST. Depends on spv-syntax.
  3. spv-ivl — IvlCmd (Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign/Seq/Choice) + Reason + the Desugarer (desugar_method). Depends on spv-ast.
  4. spv-vc — wp calculus, passify (DSA), VcFormula (first-order logic), VC simplification. Depends on spv-ivl.
  5. spv-smt — SmtSort/SmtTerm, SMT-LIB 2 emission, SmtSolver trait + SmtResult/Model, trigger annotation. Depends on spv-vc.
  6. spv-verify — driver: per-method build + discharge, Unsat⇒Verified/Sat⇒VError mapping, counterexample reconstruction, VErrorKind. Depends on all above.
  7. spv-cli — the spv binary (verify/print-ast/print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt). std.

Phase-0 smoke test (the gate): a method with assert true ⇒ Verified; assert false ⇒ Failed([VError{ kind: AssertionMightNotHold, .. }]). End-to-end pipeline alive on type shells.

1.6 What later phases unlock (Phase 1 → 13, doc ## 13..## 25)

Phase Heading Unlocks
1 ## 13 Lexer + recursive-descent Parser → full Program AST (all Stmt/Expr/Spec clauses, Old(), Quant+triggers)
2 ## 14 Resolution + TypeChecker + spec well-formedness (Bool specs; Old() only in ensures; modifies well-formed; arity) → precise TypeError
3 ## 15 Logical expression language: source Expr → VcFormula atoms/terms; arithmetic/bool/eq/quantifiers+triggers/Old() two-state/pure-fn semantics
4 ## 16 IVL core + straight-line desugar (Assign/Assert/Assume/If→Choice) with Reason tags
5 ## 17 Procedure contracts + modular verification (wrap pre/post; lower Call) + framing
6 ## 18 Loops via invariant cut + decreases termination (total correctness)
7 ## 19 wp VC generation (five rules) → VcFormula per method
8 ## 20 Passive form (DSA) passify + VC simplification (anti-exponential)
9 ## 21 SMT encoding → SmtTerm over Int/Bool/uninterpreted/Array; triggers; SMT-LIB 2; SmtSolver trait
10 ## 22 Backend integration: check(NOT VC); Unsat/Sat/Unknown; push/pop incremental; wire #278 or Z3
11 ## 23 Counterexample reconstruction: Model → source vars + program point + VErrorKind
12 ## 24 Richer features: heap/Map memory model, arrays/sequences + axioms, user pure functions (uninterpreted + axioms, well-founded recursion with fuel), ADTs
13 ## 25 Validation: PASS/FAIL benchmark suite (soundness gate), differential vs Boogie/Dafny subset, fuzzing, perf vs targets — the done gate

1.8 Timeline (estimate)

  • Phase 0: 6–14 weeks (workspace, 7 crate shells, all IR type shells, error types, CI/Makefile/Dockerfile, smoke test green).
  • Phase 1–13 (full verifier): several months; effort concentrates in wp correctness (Phase 7), DSA non-blowup (Phase 8), SMT encoding fidelity + triggers (Phase 9), counterexample mapping (Phase 11), and the soundness benchmark gate (Phase 13).

1.9 Recommended language — Rust 2021, MSRV 1.78

Driver Why Rust
Multi-stage compiler-like pipeline enum + exhaustive match model the AST, the guarded-command IvlCmd, and the first-order VcFormula as zero-cost tagged unions with no null
Immutable shared IR ownership/borrowing make the immutable-after-construction IR safe to share across stages with no aliasing bugs
Untrusted input + soundness memory safety: a verifier that crashes/UBs on adversarial annotated input is worse than useless; the soundness-critical VCGen must be robust
Performance fast VC/SmtTerm term construction at scale (large VCs, many quantifiers) without GC pauses

Rejected: OCaml/F# (sound semantically, weaker systems story + tooling here); C/C++ (memory-unsafe IR graphs); Python (too slow for VC/SMT term construction, no sum types). Reference tools are C#/OCaml (Boogie/Dafny, Why3) but their architecture maps cleanly to Rust.

1.10 Required prerequisites (reader & implementer)

  • Hoare logic: triples {P} c {Q}; partial vs total correctness.
  • Weakest preconditions: Dijkstra wp/guarded commands; the five locked rules.
  • First-order logic + SMT: satisfiability vs validity; Model; uninterpreted functions; quantifiers, triggers, axioms, fuel; theories Int/Bool/Array.
  • Compiler/IR construction: lexing, recursive-descent parsing, name resolution, type checking, lowering passes, DSA.
  • Host-language semantics: the source language's Old() two-state expressions, modifies/frame, decreases/variant, and Choice nondeterminism.
  • Reference reading: Boogie ("This is Boogie 2", Barnett et al.); Dafny (Leino, Program Proofs); Why3 (Filliâtre-Paskevich); ESC/Java + ESC/Modula-3 (Detlefs-Leino-Nelson-Saxe); Flanagan & Saxe 2001 (passive form); Dijkstra (guarded commands, wp); Hoare logic; Z3 (de Moura-Bjørner).

2. Why Build This

A deductive program verifier is the canonical "compile a program into a logical formula and prove it" artifact. It forces every layer of the program-meaning stack — concrete syntax, types, an operational/axiomatic semantics, a logical IR, a predicate calculus, an SMT theory encoding — into one coherent, soundness-critical pipeline. Boogie/Dafny/Why3/ESC are the reference systems; this rebuilds their architecture from scratch in Rust. Below: the unique knowledge it teaches, the historical + architectural stakes, the engineering muscles, portfolio cross-links, and why it is a high-signal calibration project.

2.1 Unique systems knowledge this teaches

Concept What you can only learn by building it Where it lives
Program → logic A program is a formula; "is it correct?" is "is this VC valid?". The semantic gap is bridged mechanically, not by intuition. spv-ivl → spv-vc
Guarded-command IVL Why a tiny 6-constructor language (Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign/Seq/Choice) is expressive enough to encode if/while/call/return and clean enough to define wp on. Boogie's central insight. IvlCmd in spv-ivl
wp calculus Backward predicate transformer: 5 rules (wp(assume p,Q)=p⇒Q, wp(assert p,Q)=p∧Q, wp(havoc x,Q)=∀x.Q, wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x], wp(c1;c2,Q)=wp(c1,wp(c2,Q)), wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q)∧wp(c2,Q)) compose to one VC. Dijkstra's predicate transformers made executable. wp in spv-vc
Modular verification A call is checked against the callee contract, never its body: assert(pre); havoc(modifies); assume(post). This — not cleverness — is what makes verification scale past toy size; frame/modifies is the load-bearing piece. Phase 5, spv-ivl
Loop invariants ≠ unrolling An invariant cut (assert I on entry; havoc targets; assume I; one symbolic iteration; assert I) verifies an unbounded loop in finite logic. Teaches why a weak invariant produces a false counterexample to your annotation, not the program — the #1 usability pitfall in all of Dafny. Phase 6
Passive form (DSA) Naive wp over assignment + Choice is exponential (Q[e/x] duplicates Q; nested Choice multiplies). DSA (each var assigned once, fresh versions, assignments → assumed equalities) makes the VC near-linear. Flanagan–Saxe 2001 — the difference between a verifier that scales and one that hangs at 50 lines. passify, Phase 8
SMT interface A verifier is a client of a decision procedure: encode VcFormula → SmtTerm over sorts Int/Bool/Uninterp/Array, annotate triggers for quantifier instantiation, emit SMT-LIB 2, ask check(¬VC). Validity ⇔ Unsat. Teaches the theory boundary (decidable fragments, where quantifiers go Unknown). spv-smt, Phase 9–10
Counterexample as inverse A Sat Model is run backwards through DSA/skolem renaming to recover source variables + the exact failing program point + VErrorKind. Verification is only usable if failure is legible. Phase 11, spv-verify
Soundness vs completeness The defining tension of the whole field, made concrete and testable (§2.2). every phase; Phase 13 gate

2.2 Soundness vs completeness — the cardinal lesson

  • Soundness = the verifier must NEVER report Verified for a buggy program. A dropped Assert, a mis-framed Havoc, an off-by-one in Q[e/x], or one mis-encoded operator → false "verified" → the gravest possible failure. This is a safety property of the tool itself.
  • Completeness = verify all correct programs. Strictly secondary, and provably bounded by SMT: quantifiers are undecidable, so Unknown/timeouts are expected and must be surfaced as Unknown, never silently as Verified.
  • Asymmetry is the design north star: when in doubt, fail to verify, never falsely verify. Phase 13's done-gate enforces it — every expected-FAIL fixture must report a VError; a single false Verified is a release blocker.
  • Few projects teach this asymmetry so viscerally: here a subtle bug isn't a wrong answer, it's an unsound tool that lies about correctness.

2.3 Why deductive verification matters — historical + architectural

  • Hoare logic (1969) + Dijkstra guarded commands / wp (1975) are the theory; this project turns them from blackboard calculus into a working VCGen.
  • ESC/Modula-3 & ESC/Java (Detlefs–Leino–Nelson–Saxe): proved automated extended static checking of real annotated code is feasible — the lineage this tool sits in.
  • Boogie (Leino, "This is Boogie 2"): the pivotal architectural idea — a reusable intermediate verification language + VC backend that many front ends target. spv-ivl is exactly that decoupling: front-end semantics live above the IVL; wp/SMT live below it. Learn why an IVL is the right seam.
  • Dafny (Leino, "Program Proofs"): shows the stack shipping in industry (used at AWS, Microsoft for protocol/crypto/cloud-control-plane proofs). This is a from-scratch slice of that pipeline.
  • Why3 (Filliâtre–Paskevich): alternative multi-prover backend — motivates the SmtSolver trait abstraction over one hard-wired solver.
  • Flanagan & Saxe 2001: the passive-form result that makes any of it tractable.
  • Architecturally the takeaway is durable: isolate the IVL, define wp once, treat the solver as a pluggable oracle. That layering outlives any one source language or solver.

2.4 Engineering muscles built

  • Multi-stage compiler-style pipeline as a stack of total, well-typed passes: parse → typecheck → desugar → passify → wp → encode → solve → reconstruct, each a pure function over an immutable IR.
  • Sum-type-driven IR design: Expr, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm as Rust enums with exhaustive match — zero-cost tagged unions, no null, compiler-checked case coverage on every transform (a soundness asset).
  • Term-rewriting at scale: substitution Q[e/x], DSA renaming, VC simplification (constant folding, implication flattening), trigger annotation — all hash-consing-friendly, allocation-aware.
  • Two-state reasoning: old() / pre-vs-post snapshots, framing, the modifies set — the mechanics that make procedure contracts compose.
  • Reasoning under undecidability: quantifier triggers, fuel for recursive pure functions, explicit Unknown handling — engineering around a logic that can't always answer.
  • Soundness discipline as a process: differential testing vs a Boogie/Dafny subset, fuzzing (parser, desugar, wp, SMT emission), expected-PASS/expected-FAIL corpora — treating a proof tool as a safety-critical system under test.
  • Precise error engineering: mapping an opaque SMT Model back to VError{kind, span} + Counterexample — inverse-transforming through every prior pass.

2.5 Cross-links to the portfolio

Project Relationship
#278 SMT solver spv-smt is a client: it talks to an SmtSolver trait + SMT-LIB 2 and reuses #278 (or Z3). This project consumes a decision procedure; #278 is one. Re-implementing a competitive solver is an explicit non-goal here.
#279 explicit-state model checker Contrast: #279 explores a reachable state space. This verifier deduces correctness via VCs — no state enumeration, unbounded loops handled by an invariant cut, not exploration.
#281 symbolic model checker Closest cousin: also symbolic, also SMT-backed — but bounded/state-space-oriented vs deductive, contract-modular, unbounded. Sharpens the model-checking-vs-deductive-verification distinction.
#280 theorem prover A general prover is a non-goal; this is automated verification of annotated imperative code via VCs + SMT, not interactive proof of arbitrary math.
Parser / compiler projects spv-syntax (Lexer/Parser → AST) and spv-ivl (desugaring to a small core) reuse standard front-end + lowering muscle; the novelty is the target is logic, not machine code.

2.6 Calibration value

  • Breadth × depth in one binary: front-end (lex/parse/type), formal semantics, a logical IR, a predicate-transformer calculus, an SMT theory encoding, and counterexample reconstruction — staff/principal-level integration across compiler, PL-theory, and solver domains.
  • The bug class is unforgiving and instructive: correctness here = soundness of a soundness tool. It rewards engineers who think adversarially about their own VCGen ("what program would this wrongly verify?").
  • Scale realism: target VC sizes, solver-time budgets, and the DSA-vs-exponential blowup make the difference between a demo and a tool tangible and measured (quantified in Phase 8/13).
  • Industry-relevant: the exact stack (Boogie/Dafny) shipping in AWS/Microsoft formal-methods practice — high transfer to real verification work, and a strong signal of ability to build correct tools that other people trust.

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

After completing this project end-to-end, the engineer possesses these specific competencies:

  • Predicate-transformer calculus applied to real code (Phases 6–7, §§18–19): can derive wp(c, Q) for any guarded-command program by hand, identify which rule a given transformation violates, and explain why assert p → p ∧ Q (not p ⇒ Q) is the critical polarity that makes obligations provable rather than assumable.
  • DSA / passive-form reasoning (Phase 8, §20): can explain step-by-step how passify eliminates the exponential blowup in naive wp, trace a five-branch Choice tree through version numbering, and identify a missing join-equality that would allow a DSA merge to mask a counterexample.
  • SMT-as-oracle interface design (Phases 9–10, §§21–22): can write SMT-LIB 2 for a VcFormula, annotate quantifiers with loop-free triggers, distinguish the three SmtResult cases and explain why Unknown ≠ Verified at the type level, and wire a new SMT backend behind the SmtSolver trait without touching the VCGen.
  • Soundness-first engineering discipline (Phase 13, §25 and throughout): can construct a mutation-testing harness that kills all dropped-assert mutants, write differential tests against Boogie/Dafny, and articulate the asymmetry between a false Verified (catastrophic) and an Unknown (honest incompleteness) at a code-review level.
  • Modular verification architecture (Phase 5, §17): can design a multi-method verification system where call-site cost is O(callee contract size) rather than O(callee body size), enforce this contract-only boundary at the API level so it cannot be accidentally violated, and explain the framing/modifies mechanics that make it sound.
  • Counterexample reconstruction as an inverse transform (Phase 11, §23): can trace a Z3 model through the DSA version map back to source variable names, explain the role of Reason{kind, span} tags in assigning each VError its correct VErrorKind, and diagnose a missing DSA inversion that produces an unmapped model variable.
  • Loop invariant methodology (Phase 6, §18): can apply the invariant-cut encoding to an unbounded loop, distinguish the two failure modes (LoopInvariantOnEntry vs LoopInvariantNotMaintained), and explain why a counterexample to a weak invariant is a counterexample to the user's annotation, not the program — the most important usability pitfall in the Dafny/Boogie ecosystem.

3. Architectural Overview

A single, strictly-ordered, non-cyclic compiler pipeline: annotated source enters at spv-cli, flows down through six transformation crates, and exits as a verdict (Verified) or a classified VError with a source-level Counterexample. No stage skips, no stage feeds backward; each lowers one IR to the next and is independently inspectable (print-ast/print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt). The only external dependency is the SMT solver, reached through one trait (SmtSolver) over SMT-LIB 2 text.

3.1 Canonical pipeline diagram

Front end — trust boundary through IVL lowering:

                          +-----------------------------------------------+
                          |  spv-cli  (binary "spv")                      |
                          |  verify | print-ast | print-ivl |             |
                          |  print-vc | print-smt                         |
                          +-----------------------------------------------+
                                  |
          source.spv  (annotated imperative program)   <== TRUST BOUNDARY
          requires/ensures/modifies/decreases, assert/   untrusted input;
          assume, if/while/call, old(), quantifiers      every byte adversarial
                                  |
                                  v
  +-----------+   tokens   +---------------------------------------------------+
  | spv-syntax|---------->| spv-syntax: recursive-descent Parser               |
  | Lexer ->  |           | -> Program AST (Decl/Method/Stmt/Expr/Spec/Type);  |
  | Vec<Token>|           | ParseError on bad input; spans on every node       |
  +-----------+           +---------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                          Program (surface AST, unresolved)
                                  |
                                  v
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | spv-ast   resolve identifiers; TypeChecker (Int/Bool/Ref/Map/Seq);    |
  |           spec well-formedness: specs Bool, old() only in ensures,    |
  |           modifies well-formed, arity checks. Emits TypeError.        |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                          typed + resolved AST
                                  |
                                  v
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | spv-ivl   Desugarer -> IvlCmd{Assume,Assert,Havoc,Assign,Seq,Choice} |
  |           if -> Choice[assume g;.. [] assume !g;..];                  |
  |           while -> [assert I; havoc targets; assume I; body; assert I]|
  |           call -> assert(pre); havoc(modifies); assume(post);         |
  |           method -> assume(requires); body; assert(ensures).          |
  |           Each Assert carries Reason{kind: VErrorKind, span}.         |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                          IvlCmd  (guarded commands)

Back end — VC generation through verdict:

                          IvlCmd  (guarded commands)
                                  |
                                  v
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | spv-vc    passify(c) -> DSA IvlCmd (each Var assigned once);         |
  |           wp(c, post) -> VcFormula by 6 rules:                       |
  |             wp(assume p,Q) = p=>Q                                    |
  |             wp(assert p,Q) = p AND Q                                 |
  |             wp(havoc x,Q)  = forall x.Q                              |
  |             wp(x:=e, Q)    = Q[e/x]                                  |
  |             wp(c1;c2, Q)   = wp(c1, wp(c2, Q))                      |
  |             wp(c1[]c2,Q)   = wp(c1,Q) AND wp(c2,Q)                  |
  |           VC = wp(desugar(method), True).  Valid iff program correct. |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                          VcFormula  (first-order logic)
                                  |
                                  v
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | spv-smt   encode VcFormula -> SmtTerm; trigger-annotate quantifiers; |
  |           emit SMT-LIB 2; SmtSolver.check(&[NOT VC]).                |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                    SmtResult: Unsat | Sat(Model) | Unknown
                                  |
                                  v
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | spv-verify  Unsat => Verified.                                       |
  |             Sat(Model) => VError{kind, span, Counterexample}.        |
  |             Unknown => reported, NEVER Verified.                     |
  +----------------------------------------------------------------------+
                                  |
                                  v
                  VerifyResult { Verified | Failed(Vec<VError>) }

  Trust: source.spv untrusted; IRs trusted-by-construction; a buggy solver
  returning spurious Unsat = unsound (soundness depends on trusted solver).
  Concurrency: none per method; methods may be verified in parallel jobs.
  Persistence: none — no cache, no DB; IRs live in memory only.

Direction of data flow: source text -> Token -> Program -> typed AST -> IvlCmd -> VcFormula -> SmtTerm/SMT-LIB -> SmtResult -> VerifyResult. Every arrow is a total lowering of a smaller, more-explicit language; soundness is the conjunction of each arrow preserving meaning (Sec 3.8).

3.2 spv-syntax — source front end

Owns Lexer, Token, the recursive-descent Parser, and the entire surface AST (Program, Decl, Method, Function, Stmt, Expr, Spec, Type). Input: untrusted UTF-8 source bytes (the trust boundary). Output: a Program AST or ParseError{kind: ParseErrorKind} (UnexpectedToken/BadSpec/BadType). no_std + alloc — pure, allocation-only, no I/O, deterministic. Key invariant: the AST is a faithful, lossless tree of the source (spans on every node for later error mapping); the parser never silently repairs or drops a clause — malformed input is a ParseError, never a partial tree that could later verify. Spec clauses (requires/ensures/modifies/decreases), old(), assert/assume, and Quant with Triggers are first-class grammar productions, not afterthoughts. Built in Phase 0 (type shells) and fully in Phase 1.

3.3 spv-ast — name resolution, type checking, spec well-formedness

Owns Type, TypeError, and the resolved/typed AST. Input: surface Program from spv-syntax; output: the same AST with every Var/Call/Field resolved to a declaration and every Expr assigned a Type, or a precise TypeError (Undeclared, Mismatch{expected,got}, NonBoolSpec, OldOutsideEnsures, BadModifies, ArityMismatch). The TypeChecker infers/checks Int/Bool/Ref/Map/Seq/Named and enforces spec well-formedness: every requires/ensures/invariant expression is Bool; Old() appears only inside ensures (else OldOutsideEnsures); modifies entries name framable locations (else BadModifies); call/function arities match. Key invariant: every node downstream stages touch is resolved and well-typed, so spv-ivl/spv-vc never face an undeclared name or a type ambiguity and can lower without runtime type queries; an ill-formed spec is rejected here, before it could be silently treated as a tautology and let a buggy program verify. Built in Phase 2 (consuming the logical-expression semantics fixed in Phase 3).

3.4 spv-ivl — intermediate verification language + desugaring

Owns IvlCmd (Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign/Seq/Choice), Reason{kind: VErrorKind, span}, and the Desugarer (desugar_method). Input: typed AST; output: one IvlCmd per method. The Boogie-like guarded-command core erases source control flow into nondeterminism: if -> Choice(Seq[Assume(g), then], Seq[Assume(Not g), else]); assignment/return -> Assign; assert/assume -> Assert/Assume; a method body is wrapped Seq[Assume(requires), body..., Assert(ensures)]; a Call -> Seq[Assert(callee.requires), Havoc(modifies), Assume(callee.ensures)] (modular, Sec 3.7); a While -> the invariant cut Seq[Assert(inv), Havoc(targets), Assume(inv), Choice(inductive-step, exit)] with decreases for termination (Sec 3.7, Phase 6). Key invariant — soundness of lowering: the IvlCmd must over-approximate nothing the source guarantees and under-approximate nothing it must prove — every reachable source assertion becomes a reachable Assert, and every Havoc exactly covers the frame that may change. Dropping an Assert, narrowing a Havoc, or mis-encoding a guard is a soundness bug (a buggy program would verify). Each Assert carries a Reason linking it back to a source VErrorKind + Span so spv-verify can name the failure. Built in Phases 4 (straight-line: assign/assert/assume/if), 5 (contracts/calls/framing), and 6 (loops/termination).

3.5 spv-vc — weakest-precondition VC generation + passive form

Owns VcFormula (first-order logic: True/False/And/Or/Not/Imp/Forall/Exists/Atom(Pred)), wp, and passify. Input: an IvlCmd; output: a VcFormula that is valid iff the method meets its spec. Two stages: passify(c) rewrites to dynamic single assignment (DSA) — each Var written once, fresh versions per assignment, Assign becoming an Assume of an equality, Choice arms merged with version reconciliation — so that wp(x:=e, Q)=Q[e/x] does not duplicate Q and explode (the Flanagan-Saxe passive-form trick keeps VC size near-linear instead of exponential); then wp(c, True) applies the six rules (Sec 3.1) bottom-up to the passive command. A final simplification pass (constant folding, Imp/And flattening, dead-True elimination) shrinks the formula before SMT. Key invariant — soundness of VCGen, the cardinal property: wp must compute the weakest precondition exactly — never weaker (would let buggy programs verify) — and passify must be a meaning-preserving renaming. This crate is where an off-by-one in a wp rule or a botched DSA merge most directly causes false "verified", so it is the most heavily fuzzed and differentially tested stage (Phase 13). Built in Phases 7 (wp) and 8 (DSA + simplification).

3.6 spv-smt — SMT encoding + solver interface

Owns SmtSort (Int/Bool/Uninterp(Atom)/Array(_,_)), SmtTerm, the SmtSolver trait, SmtResult (Unsat/Sat(Model)/Unknown), and Model. Input: a VcFormula; output: SmtTerms + emitted SMT-LIB 2, and the solver's SmtResult. Maps the FOL VcFormula onto sorted SMT terms — Int/Bool directly, pure functions to Uninterp, heaps/Map/Seq to Array with select/store axioms — and annotates each Forall with Triggers to control quantifier instantiation (untriggered quantifiers => Unknown/timeout/matching loops). The verifier asks for refutation: SmtSolver.check(&[NOT VC]). Key invariant — soundness of encoding: the SMT translation must be a conservative, faithful model of VcFormula semantics — every SMT-LIB axiom and sort must agree with the logical meaning, because a mis-encoded operator that makes NOT VC spuriously Unsat reports a false "verified". The SmtSolver trait is the sole boundary to the external (trusted) solver — #278 or Z3 — over SMT-LIB text; a buggy solver is an explicit soundness assumption, not this crate's fault, but Unknown is surfaced, never swallowed. Built in Phases 9 (encode/emit/trait/sorts) and 10 (backend integration, push/pop, Unknown handling).

3.7 spv-verify — driver, verdict mapping, counterexample reconstruction

Owns VerifyResult (Verified/Failed(Vec<VError>)), VError{kind: VErrorKind, span, counterexample}, VErrorKind (the eight classes: AssertionMightNotHold, PostconditionMightNotHold, PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, DivisionByZero, ModifiesViolation, MightNotTerminate), and Counterexample{bindings, at}. Input: the typed Program; orchestrates per-method desugar -> passify -> wp -> encode -> check. Verdict mapping (LOCKED): Unsat => Verified; Sat(Model) => VError; Unknown => reported as such, NEVER Verified. On Sat, it decodes the Model, reverses the DSA/skolem renaming back to source Atoms, locates the failing program point via the triggering Assert's Reason{kind, span}, classifies the VErrorKind, and renders a readable Counterexample (bindings: Vec<(Atom, Value)>, at: Span). Key invariant: the driver upholds end-to-end soundness — it must treat anything other than a clean Unsat as "not verified", so timeouts/incompleteness can never masquerade as success; and the counterexample must point at the actual failing source location, not a DSA artifact. Modular verification lives here: each method is verified solely against callee contracts (never bodies), so methods are independent verification jobs — the basis for safe parallelism (Sec 3.1 concurrency boundary). Built in Phases 0 (smoke verdict), 10 (discharge), 11 (counterexample), 12 (richer features wiring).

3.8 spv-cli — driver binary

The spv binary (std): subcommands verify, print-ast, print-ivl, print-vc, print-smt. Input: a source.spv path + flags; output: a VerifyResult rendered to stdout with a process exit code (0 = Verified, nonzero = Failed/error), or the chosen IR dump for inspection. It is the only crate doing I/O and the only std crate; all logic crates above are pure (spv-syntax is no_std + alloc). Key invariant: the CLI is a thin shell — it adds no verification logic, only argument parsing, file reading, solver process wiring, and rendering, so the trusted computing base for soundness stays in the lower crates. The print-* subcommands expose each pipeline stage's IR verbatim, making the lowering chain (Sec 3.1) auditable end to end. Built in Phase 0 and extended per phase as each IR becomes printable.

3.9 Cross-cutting properties

Property Where enforced Statement
Soundness (cardinal) spv-ivl, spv-vc, spv-smt, spv-verify A buggy program MUST NEVER report Verified. Every lowering over-/under-approximates correctly; Unknown is never Verified.
Completeness (secondary) spv-smt + spv-verify Verify all correct programs as far as SMT allows; bounded by quantifiers/Unknown/timeouts — tracked separately from soundness in Phase 13.
Determinism all crates Same source + same solver => same VerifyResult. No clocks, no RNG, no iteration-order dependence in IR construction.
Auditability spv-cli print-* Every IR (Program/IvlCmd/VcFormula/SMT-LIB) is dumpable; no hidden stage.
Trust boundary spv-syntax entry; SmtSolver exit Only source.spv is untrusted; the solver is a trusted soundness assumption reached over SMT-LIB.
Modularity spv-verify Each Call checked against callee Spec only; methods verify independently => scalable + parallelizable.

4. System Constraints

Hard, quantified envelope for the spv verifier. All numbers are budgets enforced/asserted by Phase 13 (## 25) validation; violating one is a defect, not a tuning preference. Targets assume a single annotated source.spv per spv verify invocation on commodity x86-64 (8 cores, 16 GB), release build, MSRV 1.78.

4.1 Quantified envelope

# Constraint Target / Bound Mechanism / Crate Failure if violated
C1 Program scale (per method) ≤ 2000 statements, ≤ 16 nested control levels, ≤ 64 locals/method verifies in-budget spv-ivl desugar, spv-vc wp timeout escalation → Unknown (C8)
C2 Program scale (per file) ≤ 500 methods, ≤ 50k AST nodes total; methods discharged independently (modular) spv-verify per-method loop linear slowdown only; no blowup
C3 Per-method solver-time budget p50 < 1 s, p95 < 10 s, p99 < 30 s wall-clock for SmtSolver::check spv-smt backend exceeds → timeout (C8)
C4 Configurable timeout --timeout-ms default 30000, range [100, 600000]; per-method, not global spv-cli → spv-verify hit → SmtResult::Unknown (C8)
C5 VC size VcFormula node count linear-ish: ≤ k·N, k ≤ 8, N = IVL command count, after passify. Without DSA: exponential (2^d, d = Choice depth) — FORBIDDEN spv-vc passify then wp exponential trap → OOM/timeout
C6 Passive-form mandate wp runs ONLY on passify(c) output; Choice/Assign duplication of post Q is eliminated (Flanagan-Saxe) spv-vc passify C5 broken; gravest perf bug
C7 SMT call budget 1 check(NOT VC) per method nominal; ≤ 3 with push/pop refinement; total ≤ 3·(#methods) spv-smt SmtSolver, spv-verify retry storm → wall-clock blowout
C8 Unknown handling SmtResult::Unknown (timeout/incompleteness/quantifier) reported as explicit non-verdict, NEVER silently treated as Verified spv-verify Unsat/Sat/Unknown match soundness compromise if mis-mapped
C9 AST IR memory Program ≤ 64 MB for max file (C2); ~1.3 kB/AST node ceiling spv-syntax/spv-ast OOM; reject oversize input
C10 IVL IR memory IvlCmd tree ≤ 96 MB/method peak (post-desugar, pre-passify) spv-ivl OOM
C11 VC + SMT IR memory VcFormula + SmtTerm ≤ 128 MB/method peak spv-vc/spv-smt OOM
C12 RSS ceiling total process RSS ≤ 2 GB excluding external solver subprocess whole pipeline OOM-kill; surface as error
C13 Determinism same source.spv + same solver build + fixed --seed ⇒ byte-identical SMT-LIB emission AND identical VerifyResult all crates; no HashMap iteration in output paths (use sorted / BTreeMap/IndexMap) flaky verdicts; CI nondeterminism
C14 Startup zero global init; no lazy_static/once-cell heavy ctor; cold spv verify overhead (excl. solver spawn + check) < 50 ms for ≤ 100-method file spv-cli latency regression
C15 Recovery — parse/type ParseError/TypeError are typed, non-panicking; malformed input ⇒ diagnostic + nonzero exit, NEVER crash/UB spv-syntax/spv-ast panic = defect
C16 Recovery — solver solver crash/timeout/Unknown ⇒ caught, mapped to Unknown verdict; verifier process survives, continues to next method spv-verify/spv-smt cascade failure
C17 Soundness (cardinal) NEVER emit Verified for a program whose VC is not valid; on any doubt (Unknown, encoding gap) ⇒ withhold Verified spv-verify, spv-vc gravest failure (see C8, §note)
C18 Quantifier instantiation every Forall emitted with ≥1 Trigger; triggerless quantifier ⇒ rejected or flagged (matching-loop / nontermination risk) spv-smt trigger annotation solver Unknown/divergence
C19 Counterexample fidelity every Sat(Model) ⇒ a Counterexample{bindings, at} with the failing Span + ≥ the asserting variables resolved to source Atoms spv-verify reconstruction unactionable error report
C20 Numeric model integers are mathematical (unbounded Int), NOT machine words — no overflow wraparound; DivisionByZero modeled as explicit VErrorKind, not solver UB spv-vc/spv-smt unsound arithmetic

4.2 Topology & dependency assumptions (LOCKED)

  • Single process, single machine. One spv invocation = one OS process; the seven spv-* crates link into the spv binary. No distribution, no IPC fabric, no daemon. The only out-of-process actor is the SMT solver.
  • SMT-solver dependency is mandatory and external. Verification cannot complete without a backend behind the SmtSolver trait. Two supported wirings: (a) project #278 in-process/linked, (b) Z3 via SMT-LIB 2 over stdin/stdout subprocess. Absence ⇒ spv verify errors out (clear "no solver" diagnostic); print-ast/print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt still work solver-free.
  • Solver is a trusted oracle. A wrong Unsat from the backend ⇒ false Verified upstream; trust boundary documented in Threat Model. spv-verify does not re-check solver answers (no proof reconstruction in scope).
  • No persistence / no network. No DB, no cache server, no telemetry egress. Optional on-disk SMT-LIB dump is for print-smt only. Solver subprocess is local.
  • Untrusted input = the .spv source. Adversarial/malformed annotated programs must not crash the verifier (C15); they may legitimately be rejected (ParseError/TypeError) or yield Failed/Unknown.

4.3 Concurrency & I/O

  • Per-method VC build + discharge is embarrassingly parallel (C2 modularity); MAY use a bounded worker pool ≤ #cores. Determinism (C13) is preserved: results collected and emitted in source order regardless of completion order; no shared mutable VCGen state.
  • Solver subprocess I/O is line-buffered SMT-LIB; check/push/pop are synchronous per worker. Incremental solving (C7) uses push/pop, not fresh process per query.
  • No async runtime required; std threads sufficient.

4.4 Non-budgeted (explicit non-goals, restating §identity)

  • No competitive SMT solver (that is #278), no model checker (#279/#281), no general theorem prover (#280), no separation logic / concurrency, no real-language full semantics, no compilation/execution of verified programs, no GUI. These carry no performance budget here.

4.5 Notes & rationale

  • C5/C6 — the exponential trap. Naive wp(x := e, Q) = Q[e/x] and wp(c1 [] c2, Q) = wp(c1,Q) ∧ wp(c2,Q) duplicate Q; over nested Choice/Seq the VcFormula grows as 2^d. passify (DSA: each Var assigned once → fresh versioned Var, assignment becomes Assume(x_k = e)) makes VC size linear-ish in IVL size. This is the single most load-bearing performance decision; skipping it silently reintroduces blowup → C12 OOM. Phase 8 (## 20) is the gate.
  • C8/C17 — Unknown ≠ Verified. The verdict mapping is exactly: Unsat ⇒ Verified; Sat(model) ⇒ Failed(VError…); Unknown ⇒ Unknown (a distinct, surfaced outcome — timeout, quantifier incompleteness, or encoding limit). Any code path that collapses Unknown into Verified is the gravest soundness defect and is a Phase 13 hard-fail fixture.
  • C18 — triggers bound quantifier cost. Triggerless Forall invites matching loops and unpredictable solver time (→ Unknown, nondeterministic latency); the encoder must annotate or refuse. fuel (Phase 12, ## 24) similarly bounds recursive pure-function unfolding.
  • C13 — determinism sources. Eliminate HashMap iteration order from any output-affecting path (SMT-LIB term emission, error ordering); pass --seed through to the solver where supported; pin solver version in CI for differential tests. Same program + same solver build + same seed ⇒ same verdict is a tested invariant.
  • C3/C4 — budget vs. correctness. Time budgets govern liveness, never soundness: exceeding the budget yields Unknown, never a fabricated Verified. A correct-but-slow program hitting --timeout-ms is reported honestly as Unknown, not failed and not verified.

5. Required Reading

Curated, non-optional. Each entry: what to extract for this verifier, mapped to the crate/phase that consumes it. Read in the order within each subsection; depth markers — [skim] = orient, [study] = work the proofs/code, [ref] = keep open while coding. Do not read for breadth; read for the exact rule, term, or invariant named.

5.1 Books

# Work (author, ed./year) Exact chapters Extract → consumer Depth
B1 Leino, Program Proofs (MIT Press, 2023) Pt I (Hoare logic, wp); Pt II (methods, requires/ensures/modifies); loops + invariants + decreases; functions + old(); framing The specification surface semantics this tool emulates 1:1 — Spec{requires,ensures,modifies,decreases}, two-state Old, invariant cut, well-founded variant. Worked PASS/FAIL examples seed Phase 13's benchmark suite. → spv-ast, spv-ivl, spv-verify (Ph 1,2,5,6,13) [study]
B2 Bradley & Manna, The Calculus of Computation (Springer, 2007) Ch 1–2 (propositional/FOL); Ch 3 (first-order theories: equality, ints, arrays); Ch 5 (quantifier elim); Ch 7 (decision procedures, combination); Ch 12 (program correctness, wp, invariant generation) First-order theory underneath VcFormula/SmtTerm; the theory-of-arrays axioms for the Map/heap encoding; why quantifiers are the completeness boundary. → spv-vc, spv-smt (Ph 3,9,12) [study]
B3 Kroening & Strichman, Decision Procedures (Springer, 2nd ed. 2016) Ch 1 (SAT/SMT intro); Ch 3 (equality + uninterpreted functions, EUF); Ch 6 (theory combination, Nelson–Oppen); Ch 7 (arrays); Ch 9 (quantifiers, triggers/E-matching); Ch 11 (SMT-LIB + solver interaction) What the SmtSolver does behind the SmtSolver trait — EUF for uninterpreted pure functions, array theory for heaps/seqs, trigger/E-matching for Quant/Forall instantiation. Explains Unknown. → spv-smt, spv-verify (Ph 9,10,12) [study]
B4 Winskel, The Formal Semantics of Programming Languages (MIT Press, 1993) Ch 2–3 (operational semantics of IMP); Ch 6–7 (Hoare logic, soundness & relative completeness); Ch 4 (well-founded induction) The soundness argument: why {P} c {Q} + wp is sound, and the relative-completeness ceiling. Grounds the cardinal claim "VCGen must NEVER let a buggy program verify". → spv-vc, spv-ivl (Ph 6,7) [study]
B5 Nielson & Nielson, Semantics with Applications: An Appetizer (Springer, 2007) Ch 6 (axiomatic semantics); Ch 7 (predicate transformers / wp); annotated programs + verification conditions Cleanest derivation of the five wp rules (assume, assert, havoc/nondeterminism, assignment-substitution, sequencing) and VC extraction from annotated loops. Cross-check against the spine's wp table. → spv-vc (Ph 7,8) [study]
B6 Klabnik & Nichols, The Rust Programming Language (2nd ed., 2023) + The Rustonomicon (online) TRPL Ch 6 (enums + exhaustive match), Ch 10 (generics/traits — for SmtSolver), Ch 15 (Box/recursive types — for Box<Expr>/Box<VcFormula>); Nomicon: Drop/recursion only if stack-deep IR Idioms for modeling AST/IvlCmd/VcFormula/SmtTerm as sum types with no null; trait-object backend dispatch; arena/Box recursion for deep trees. → all crates (Ph 0–13) [ref]

Reading discipline: B1 first (the what), then B4/B5 (the why it's sound), then B2/B3 (the how the solver decides it). B6 is [ref], opened per-phase.

5.2 Papers

# Paper (author, venue, year) Core contribution Implementation relevance → consumer Depth
P1 Flanagan & Saxe, "Avoiding Exponential Explosion: Generating Compact VCs", POPL 2001 Passive form (dynamic single assignment): convert assign+Choice to assumes-of-equalities so each var is assigned once → VC size linear-ish, not exponential The literal algorithm passify(&IvlCmd) -> IvlCmd implements. Read before Phase 8 — naive wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x] duplicates Q across Choice. The whole reason DSA exists here. → spv-vc (Ph 8) [study]
P2 Leino, "This is Boogie 2" (MSR tech report, 2008) The Boogie IVL reference: guarded commands, assume/assert/havoc, procedure contracts, the loop/invariant encoding, where-clauses Normative model for IvlCmd{Assume,Assert,Havoc,Assign,Seq,Choice} and the desugaring of call→assert-pre/havoc-modifies/assume-post. Match our Reason-tagged asserts to Boogie's. → spv-ivl, spv-vc (Ph 4,5,6,7) [ref]
P3 Barnett, Chang, DeLine, Jacobs, Leino, "Boogie: A Modular Reusable Verifier for OO Programs", FMCO 2005 Architecture of a VCGen as a pipeline front end → IVL → VC → SMT; modular (per-method) verification against contracts Justifies the seven-crate split and per-method discharge in spv-verify; framing of unmodified heap. Read for system shape, not algorithm. → spv-verify (Ph 5,10) [skim]
P4 Detlefs, Leino, Nelson, Saxe, "Extended Static Checking for Java" (a.k.a. ESC/Java; SRC research report 159, 1998) + Leino–Nelson–Saxe ESC/Modula-3 Original wp-to-prover checker; assume/assert/modifies; soundness vs. completeness trade (intentional unsoundness sources to avoid) Defines the bug-finding bar and where unsoundness sneaks in (dropped assert, mis-framed havoc) — exactly what Phase 13's soundness gate forbids. → spv-verify, spv-vc (Ph 11,13) [study]
P5 Dijkstra, "Guarded Commands, Nondeterminacy and Formal Derivation of Programs", CACM 1975 Guarded-command language; nondeterministic choice []; wp predicate transformer; healthiness conditions Origin of Choice and the wp rule wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q) AND wp(c2,Q). Eight pages; read fully. → spv-ivl, spv-vc (Ph 4,7) [study]
P6 Filliâtre & Paskevich, "Why3 — Where Programs Meet Provers", ESOP 2013 A VC-generating front end over multiple back-end provers via a common logic + driver layer Validates the SmtSolver trait + SMT-LIB abstraction (pluggable #278/Z3); driver/encoding separation = our spv-smt/spv-verify boundary. → spv-smt, spv-verify (Ph 9,10) [skim]
P7 Leino, "Efficient Weakest Preconditions" (Information Processing Letters, 2005) A two-formula wp variant (N/W predicates) avoiding the blow-up of plain wp on passive programs Optional optimization for Phase 8 after P1 — alternative to pure substitution; benchmark VC size against it. → spv-vc (Ph 8) [study]
P8 Barnett & Leino, "Weakest-Precondition of Unstructured Programs", PASTE 2005 wp over an unstructured (CFG/block) IVL, not just structured stmts Reference if loops/early-Return push us past the structured Seq/Choice tree; informs how Return is modeled. → spv-ivl, spv-vc (Ph 6,7) [ref]
P9 de Moura & Bjørner, "Z3: An Efficient SMT Solver", TACAS 2008 Z3 internals at a high level: combined theories, E-matching with triggers, model construction Why trigger annotation (Phase 9) matters; how Sat(Model) is produced for counterexamples (Phase 11); the default backend behind the trait. → spv-smt, spv-verify (Ph 9,10,11) [skim]
P10 Leino & Pit-Claudel, "Trigger Selection Strategies to Stabilize Program Verifiers", CAV 2016 How bad/missing triggers cause Unknown, timeouts, instability; selection heuristics Direct input to spv-smt trigger annotation and to explaining Unknown to users; matrix-loop pitfall. → spv-smt (Ph 9,10) [study]

Reading order: P5 (origin) → P2 (the IVL we copy) → P1 (the passive-form must-read) → P4 (soundness bar) → then P3/P6 (system shape), P9/P10 (solver reality), P7/P8 (optimizations) as the relevant phase arrives.

5.3 Reference Implementations

Study architecture and algorithm, not lines. We are Rust + seven crates; these are C#/OCaml. Copy ideas, not code.

# Implementation What to study What NOT to copy → consumer
R1 Boogie (github.com/boogie-org/boogie, C#) VCGeneration/ — the VC generator; the passive-form / DSA pass; assume/assert/havoc lowering; SMT-LIB emission in Provers/ C# class hierarchy; its where-clause + polymorphic-map machinery (out of scope); the .NET solver-process plumbing spv-vc, spv-ivl, spv-smt (Ph 4–10)
R2 Dafny (github.com/dafny-lang/dafny, C#) The AST → Boogie translation (Translator.cs): how requires/ensures/modifies/decreases, old(), loops, calls, and pure functions lower to guarded commands; fuel for recursive functions The full Dafny surface language (datatypes/traits/refinement); resolver scale; we implement a subset of the source language only spv-ivl, spv-ast, spv-verify (Ph 2,5,6,12)
R3 Why3 (gitlab.inria.fr/why3, OCaml) The driver layer: one logic, many provers; transformation pipeline; printer to SMT-LIB OCaml module/functor structure; WhyML's full feature set; session DB spv-smt, spv-verify (Ph 9,10)
R4 Z3 SMT-LIB front end + the z3.rs/FFI crate (or #278's solver) The concrete SMT-LIB 2 request/response shape: declare-fun, assert, check-sat, get-model, push/pop, (! … :pattern …) triggers Z3's C++ internals; do not bind to the C API beyond what the SmtSolver trait needs — keep it text-SMT-LIB-first so #278 plugs in spv-smt, spv-verify (Ph 9,10)
R5 ESC/Java2 (Kind, Chalin et al.) or OpenJML A real wp-based checker on a real language: how counterexample labels map back to source assertions; warning classification Java-specific semantics; the bytecode/JML breadth spv-verify (Ph 11)
R6 Viper / Silver (ETH, Scala) — optional An intermediate verification language designed as a reuse target; inhale/exhale ≈ our assume/assert; framing Permission/separation logic (explicit NON-GOAL); Scala structure spv-ivl (Ph 4,5) [skim]

Hard rule: read these to learn the desugaring and the DSA transform, then implement clean-room in Rust. No license-laundering, no structural transliteration of C#/OCaml class graphs into Rust — model with enums + match per §[Core Types].

5.4 Specifications (Normative)

Treat as authoritative; deviations are bugs, not choices.

# Spec Authoritative for Normative use → consumer
S1 SMT-LIB 2.6 standard (Barrett, Fontaine, Tinelli — smt-lib.org) Sort/term syntax; declare-sort/declare-fun/define-fun; assert/check-sat/get-model; theories Core, Ints, ArraysEx; :pattern trigger annotation; push/pop; sat/unsat/unknown Exact target of SmtTerm → text emission and of the SmtSolver/SmtResult{Unsat,Sat,Unknown} parse. SmtSort{Int,Bool,Uninterp,Array} ↔ Core/Ints/ArraysEx. Conform to it, do not invent dialect. → spv-smt (Ph 9,10)
S2 The Boogie 2 language reference (P2 + the Boogie manual) IVL command set, contract/loop encoding, expression typing Normative shape for IvlCmd desugaring and the call/loop lowering; our IVL is a subset (no polymorphic maps, no where). → spv-ivl (Ph 4,5,6)
S3 Hoare-logic + wp rule set — the spine §[Key Algorithms] table, cross-validated against B5/P5 {P} c {Q}; partial vs total correctness; the five wp rules verbatim The single normative definition of wp(&IvlCmd, VcFormula) -> VcFormula. Method VC = wp(desugar_method(m), True) after pre/post wrap. Any code deviating from these rules is a soundness bug. → spv-vc (Ph 7)
S4 Rust Reference + Rust API Guidelines (doc.rust-lang.org/reference, rust-lang.github.io/api-guidelines) Edition-2021 semantics; match exhaustiveness; trait coherence; no_std + alloc rules (spv-syntax/-ast/-ivl/-vc/-smt are no_std; spv-cli is std) Conformance for the crate boundary, MSRV 1.78, and the SmtSolver trait design. → all crates (Ph 0)

Normative wp table (reproduced — bind every Phase-7 test to it):

wp(assume p, Q)   = p => Q
wp(assert p, Q)   = p AND Q
wp(havoc x, Q)    = forall x. Q
wp(x := e, Q)     = Q[e/x]
wp(c1 ; c2, Q)    = wp(c1, wp(c2, Q))
wp(c1 [] c2, Q)   = wp(c1, Q) AND wp(c2, Q)

Correctness identity (cite in Phases 7,10): program correct ⇔ VC valid ⇔ (NOT VC) Unsat. Sat(Model) ⇒ a concrete Counterexample; Unknown ⇒ incompleteness (timeout/quantifiers), never silently treated as Verified — that would breach soundness (P4, B4).

6. Design Principles

Twelve-plus non-negotiable principles for the Static Program Verifier (spv). Each is derived from a design tension in the spine and the KEY ALGORITHMS/FACTS. Format: title — rule (one/two lines) → tradeoff → operational implication → violation example. Later phases (## 12..## 25) reference these by name (e.g. "per P1 Soundness over completeness"). Priority ordering is total: when two principles conflict, the lower number wins (P1 > P2 > ... > P15).

# Principle One-line invariant
P1 Soundness over completeness Never report Verified for a program that can violate a spec.
P2 Lower to a tiny IVL All semantics flow through 6 IvlCmd constructors.
P3 wp is the calculus VC = wp(desugar_method(m), VcFormula::True); five rules, no ad hoc.
P4 Passive form or exponential death passify (DSA) before wp; assignment never duplicates Q.
P5 Modular: contracts, never bodies A Stmt::Call is verified against Spec, never callee body.
P6 Solver is an oracle behind a trait All SMT through SmtSolver::check; core is solver-agnostic.
P7 Every NO yields a counterexample Each VError carries a Counterexample mapped to source.
P8 Determinism: fixed seed → fixed verdict Same input + config ⇒ byte-identical SMT-LIB + verdict.
P9 Errors, never panic on untrusted source Malformed .spv ⇒ typed error, never panic!/UB.
P10 Triggers are load-bearing Every Forall ships explicit Vec<Trigger>; no naked quantifier.
P11 Unknown is not Verified SmtResult::Unknown ⇒ honest non-verdict, never silent pass.
P12 Reason-tag every assert Each IvlCmd::Assert carries Reason{kind,span} at birth.
P13 IR immutable after construction AST/IVL/VC built once, shared &, never mutated in place.
P14 One IVL semantics, many printers print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt render the same artifact verifying runs.
P15 Frame everything, havoc precisely Havoc exactly the modifies/loop-target set; unlisted state framed.

6.1 P1 — Soundness over completeness (cardinal rule)

  • Rule: the VCGen must NEVER let a buggy program verify. Reporting Failed on a correct program (false alarm / incompleteness) is acceptable; reporting Verified on a buggy one is the gravest failure.
  • Tradeoff: we accept false negatives (correct programs we cannot prove, Unknown, spurious VError) to eliminate false positives (buggy programs called Verified). Completeness is secondary, bounded by SMT.
  • Operational implication: every transform — desugar_method, passify, wp, encode — is assertion-preserving: dropping/weakening an IvlCmd::Assert, narrowing a Havoc, or mis-encoding an operator is a sound-ness bug, the top-severity defect class in Phase 13 (## 25). Soundness regressions block release; completeness regressions file a ticket. Unknown ⇒ VError-or-abstain, never Verified (see P11).
  • Violation example: wp(assert p, Q) mis-written as p => Q (the assume rule) drops the proof obligation p; a method with assert false would verify. The expected-FAIL benchmark in ## 25 must catch this; any expected-FAIL that flips to Verified is a soundness alarm and fails the "done" gate.

6.2 P2 — Lower everything to a tiny IVL

  • Rule: every source construct (assign, if, while, Stmt::Call, Return, Assert, Assume, contracts) desugars to just six guarded commands: Assume / Assert / Havoc / Assign / Seq / Choice. The trusted core reasons about 6 cases, not the full surface grammar.
  • Tradeoff: an extra lowering pass (spv-ivl) and loss of surface structure (recoverable via Reason.span) in exchange for a small, auditable wp (5 rules over 6 ctors) and exhaustive match.
  • Operational implication: new source features (Phase 12, ## 24: heap, sequences, datatypes) add desugaring, never new IvlCmd variants or wp rules; the verification kernel stays frozen. spv-vc/spv-smt never import spv-syntax.
  • Violation example: adding a IvlCmd::While "for efficiency" forces a new wp case with loop reasoning inside the calculus — exactly the untrusted complexity the invariant cut (Phase 6, ## 18) was designed to keep out of wp. Soundness audit surface explodes.

6.3 P3 — wp is the calculus (no ad-hoc VC)

  • Rule: the VC is defined as wp(desugar_method(m), VcFormula::True) via exactly five rules:
    wp(assume p, Q) = p => Q          wp(assert p, Q) = p AND Q
wp(havoc x, Q)  = forall x. Q     wp(x := e, Q)   = Q[e/x]
wp(c1 ; c2, Q)  = wp(c1, wp(c2, Q))   wp(c1 [] c2, Q) = wp(c1,Q) AND wp(c2,Q)
    Program correct ⇔ VC valid ⇔ NOT VC unsat (the P6 oracle call).
  • Tradeoff: a denotational, provable definition (each rule auditable against Dijkstra/Hoare) over hand-rolled per-construct VC shortcuts. Slightly larger formulas, far smaller trust base.
  • Operational implication: fn wp(c: &IvlCmd, post: VcFormula) -> VcFormula is total + structurally recursive over the 6 ctors; every IvlCmd has exactly one rule. Phase 7 (## 19) ships these and only these.
  • Violation example: special-casing "an if with no else" with a bespoke formula instead of Choice ⇒ a path that silently assumes the missing branch holds; a postcondition violable only on that branch slips through Verified.

6.4 P4 — Passive form or exponential death

  • Rule: run passify (dynamic single assignment) before wp. Naive wp(x := e, Q) = Q[e/x] duplicates Q; with Choice, wp(c1[]c2,Q) duplicates the continuation ⇒ VC size exponential in branch/assignment count (Flanagan–Saxe 2001).
  • Tradeoff: a DSA pass + fresh-version bookkeeping (Var versions, join Havoc+Assume equalities at Choice merges) for a VC that is roughly linear in program size.
  • Operational implication: in DSA each Var is assigned once; assignments become assume(x_k == e); wp over passive code never substitutes into a duplicated Q. Target: VC term count O(n) for an n-command method; budget in ## 25 is < 10·n SmtTerm nodes (else a passification regression).
  • Violation example: skipping passify on a method with 30 sequential ifs ⇒ ~2³⁰ VC paths; SMT emission OOMs or times out → looks like an incompleteness/Unknown (P11) when it is really an architecture bug.

6.5 P5 — Modular: verify against contracts, never bodies

  • Rule: lower a Stmt::Call to assert(callee.requires) ; havoc(callee.modifies) ; assume(callee.ensures). The callee body is never inlined or re-examined at a call site.
  • Tradeoff: the caller cannot exploit incidental callee behavior beyond its Spec (a too-weak ensures blocks the caller — a completeness cost), in exchange for per-method verification cost independent of callee size — the property that makes verification scale to whole programs.
  • Operational implication: each Method is one VC, one SmtSolver::check; cost is local. Recursion needs no fixpoint — the contract stands in for the body. Phase 5 (## 17) owns call lowering + framing.
  • Violation example: inlining a callee to "get a stronger fact" both breaks modularity (cost blows up) and risks soundness — the callee's own requires go unchecked at the boundary, so a precondition violation (PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold) is silently lost.

6.6 P6 — The solver is an oracle behind a trait

  • Rule: all SMT interaction is trait SmtSolver { fn check(&mut self, assertions: &[SmtTerm]) -> SmtResult; } returning Unsat | Sat(Model) | Unknown. The core never depends on a concrete solver; spv-smt/spv-verify speak only SmtTerm + SMT-LIB 2.
  • Tradeoff: an abstraction boundary (and SMT-LIB emission for portability) instead of a tight Z3 API binding; we cannot use solver-proprietary features, but we can swap #278 ⇄ Z3 freely (Phase 10, ## 22).
  • Operational implication: verify = "is NOT VC unsat?" — Unsat ⇒ Verified; Sat(model) ⇒ VError (P7); Unknown ⇒ honest non-verdict (P11). The trait is the single chokepoint where the verdict's truth is delegated.
  • Violation example: reading a Z3-specific model field directly in spv-verify couples the core to one solver; swapping to #278 breaks counterexample reconstruction, and the SmtResult/Model contract that guarantees verdict semantics is bypassed.

6.7 P7 — Every NO yields a counterexample

  • Rule: every VError carries a Counterexample{bindings: Vec<(Atom, Value)>, at: Span}. Sat(Model) is never reported as a bare failure; the model is reconstructed into source variables + the failing program point + a VErrorKind.
  • Tradeoff: Phase 11 (## 23) reconstruction work (parse Model, invert DSA/skolem renaming back to source Atom, locate Reason.span) for actionable diagnostics that make the tool usable rather than a yes/no black box.
  • Operational implication: the DSA renaming (P4) and Reason tags (P12) are kept specifically so a model maps back: x_7 ↦ 5 ⇒ source x = 5 at the Reason.span. A Counterexample with empty bindings on a Sat result is a reconstruction bug.
  • Violation example: discarding the Model and emitting only "postcondition might not hold" forces the user to guess inputs; worse, an unmapped model can hide that the failure is a spurious one from a weak invariant (see P5/loop pitfall), eroding trust.

6.8 P8 — Determinism: fixed seed → fixed verdict

  • Rule: same source.spv + same config (solver, timeout, seed) ⇒ byte-identical SMT-LIB, identical VerifyResult, identical Counterexample ordering. No nondeterministic iteration order leaks into output.
  • Tradeoff: must use ordered maps / stable sorts for Var versions, Forall binders, And/Or arms, and bindings — never raw HashMap iteration — costing some ergonomics for reproducibility.
  • Operational implication: print-smt is a golden-file fixture in ## 25; CI diffs it. The only sanctioned nondeterminism is the solver returning Unknown vs a verdict under a wall-clock timeout — which is recorded, not hidden.
  • Violation example: emitting asserts in HashMap order makes print-smt golden tests flaky and makes a soundness regression indistinguishable from a reordering; differential testing vs Boogie/Dafny becomes impossible to triage.

6.9 P9 — Errors, never panic on untrusted source

  • Rule: .spv input is untrusted. Lexing/parsing/typing failures surface as ParseError/TypeError (ParseErrorKind::{UnexpectedToken,BadSpec,BadType}, TypeError::{Undeclared,Mismatch,NonBoolSpec,OldOutsideEnsures,BadModifies,ArityMismatch}). No unwrap/expect/array-OOB/panic! on any input.
  • Tradeoff: pervasive Result threading and explicit error variants instead of fast-and-loose unwrapping; more code, but a verifier that cannot itself crash on adversarial input.
  • Operational implication: spv-syntax/spv-ast are no_std + alloc and panic-free; the fuzzers in Phase 13 (## 25) (parser, desugar, wp, SMT emission) assert no panics. A panic on any fuzz input is a release blocker.
  • Violation example: desugar_method indexing m.returns[0] for a method declaring no returns panics on valid-but-unusual input; a fuzzer-found crash means the verifier is a DoS target and cannot be trusted as an analysis gate.

6.10 P10 — Triggers are load-bearing for quantifiers

  • Rule: every VcFormula::Forall and source Expr::Quant carries an explicit Vec<Trigger>. Quantifier instantiation in SMT is trigger-driven; a missing/over-broad trigger ⇒ no instantiation (false Unknown/incompleteness) or a matching loop (timeout).
  • Tradeoff: authoring + validating triggers (Phase 9, ## 21) and threading them through wp(havoc x, Q)=forall x.Q and axioms (Phase 12, ## 24) instead of relying on solver auto-triggers, for predictable, bounded instantiation.
  • Operational implication: Forall(binders, triggers, body) keeps triggers non-empty for every shipped axiom (arrays, sequences, pure-function defining axioms with fuel); ## 21 conformance rejects a Forall with empty triggers. Triggers must mention all bound Vars and be ground-instantiable.
  • Violation example: a sequence-length axiom forall s. len(s) >= 0 with no trigger is never instantiated ⇒ a provable program reports Unknown; a trigger {len(s)} that also matches inside its own body ⇒ infinite instantiation ⇒ matching-loop timeout misread as incompleteness.

6.11 P11 — Unknown is not Verified (timeouts are honest)

  • Rule: SmtResult::Unknown (timeout / incompleteness / quantifier limits) is a distinct, surfaced outcome — never collapsed into Verified, never silently into Failed. Verified requires a definite Unsat.
  • Tradeoff: the user sees "could not decide" outcomes (less satisfying than a crisp yes/no) rather than a falsely confident pass — the only honest choice given SMT undecidability (P1 corollary).
  • Operational implication: spv-verify maps Unsat ⇒ Verified, Sat ⇒ Failed, and Unknown ⇒ an explicit non-verifying status with the solver's reason + budget. Per-method push/pop (## 22) bounds time; the timeout is config, logged, and deterministic-modulo-wall-clock (P8).
  • Violation example: treating Unknown as Verified "because it didn't find a counterexample" directly violates P1 — a buggy program whose VC merely timed out would be reported correct. This is the single most tempting and most dangerous soundness hole.

6.12 P12 — Reason-tag every assert at birth

  • Rule: every IvlCmd::Assert(expr, reason) is created with Reason{kind: VErrorKind, span: Span} identifying why the obligation exists (AssertionMightNotHold, PostconditionMightNotHold, PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, DivisionByZero, ModifiesViolation, MightNotTerminate).
  • Tradeoff: desugaring must carry source provenance through to the IVL (more plumbing) so a failed obligation classifies + locates itself for P7 reconstruction.
  • Operational implication: when Sat pins a failing conjunct, its originating Assert's Reason yields the VErrorKind + Span directly — no guessing which assertion failed. Generated asserts (e.g. implicit DivisionByZero guards) are tagged at the point they are synthesized.
  • Violation example: lowering a loop's inductive check as an untagged Assert ⇒ a failure can't tell LoopInvariantOnEntry from LoopInvariantNotMaintained; the user can't tell whether the invariant is too weak on entry or not preserved — the classic, hardest-to-debug verifier UX failure.

6.13 P13 — IR is immutable after construction

  • Rule: Program/typed AST, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm are built once and thereafter shared via & (or Box/Rc), never mutated in place. Transforms (desugar_method, passify, wp, encode) consume &In and produce a new Out.
  • Tradeoff: allocation of fresh trees per stage (some memory/copies) instead of in-place rewriting, bought back by freely shareable IR, trivial structural equality for fixtures, and no aliasing-mutation bugs in IR graphs (a core Rust-over-C++ rationale).
  • Operational implication: stages are pure functions ⇒ testable in isolation, parallelizable per method, and print-* views (P14) observe exactly what the next stage consumes.
  • Violation example: mutating a shared VcFormula subtree during simplification while another method's VC aliases it ⇒ cross-method corruption; under P8 golden tests this manifests as nondeterministic print-vc output.

6.14 P14 — One IVL semantics, many printers

  • Rule: spv-cli subcommands print-ast / print-ivl / print-vc / print-smt render the same artifacts that verify consumes — no separate "display" pipeline. What you print is what gets verified.
  • Tradeoff: printers must faithfully serialize the real IR (no simplified-for-humans variant) — a small constraint that guarantees inspection matches reality.
  • Operational implication: every counterexample/soundness bug is reproducible by dumping the intermediate stage; print-smt is the deterministic golden artifact (P8) and the differential-testing handle vs Boogie/Dafny in ## 25.
  • Violation example: a print-ivl that "cleans up" Choice into pretty if/else while verify runs the raw IvlCmd ⇒ an operator/desugar bug is invisible in the dump that engineers trust to debug it.

6.15 P15 — Frame everything; havoc precisely

  • Rule: Havoc(vars) scrambles exactly the declared change set — a call's modifies clause and a loop's assigned targets — and nothing else. All state not in the frame is implicitly preserved across the boundary.
  • Tradeoff: demands accurate modifies/target computation (Phase 5 ## 17, Phase 6 ## 18) and well-formed modifies checking (TypeError::BadModifies, Phase 2 ## 14); the payoff is that callers/loops retain facts about untouched state — essential for completeness.
  • Operational implication: havocking too little (omitting a modified var) is a soundness bug (the caller keeps a stale, false fact) → P1 breach; havocking too much is a completeness bug (lost frame facts → spurious VError). ModifiesViolation fires when a body writes outside its declared modifies.
  • Violation example: a call whose callee modifies a heap field but whose modifies omits it ⇒ Havoc leaves the caller assuming the old field value ⇒ a real post-state bug verifies as Verified — a frame-induced soundness hole, the subtlest class to detect in ## 25.

6.16 Tensions resolved by priority

Tension Lower-priority pull Resolution (winning principle)
Prove more programs vs never be wrong completeness P1 wins: abstain/Unknown (P11) before unsound Verified.
Inline callee for stronger facts vs scale locally stronger VC P5 wins: contracts only; strengthen ensures instead.
Compact VC via per-construct shortcuts vs auditability smaller formula P3 wins: five wp rules only; shrink via passify/simplify, not special cases.
Solver-specific power vs portability Z3 features P6 wins: SmtTerm + SMT-LIB through the SmtSolver trait.
Speed via in-place IR mutation vs safety fewer allocs P13 wins: immutable IR, pure transforms.
Pretty human output vs fidelity readability P14 wins: printers serialize the verified IR verbatim.

These principles are invariants, not guidelines: Phase 13 (## 25) conformance and the soundness gate test them directly, and every later phase cites them by number/name when a design choice is justified.

7. System Invariants

The invariants below are the load-bearing correctness contract of the verifier. They are ordered by gravity: INV-1..INV-4 guard soundness (never report Verified for a buggy program — the cardinal property); INV-5..INV-8 guard the semantic fidelity each soundness claim rests on; INV-9..INV-12 guard verdict honesty and counterexample faithfulness. Each is stated as a checkable proposition, with where it lives in the spv-* pipeline, how a violation surfaces, and how to debug it. "Behavior" = a (pre-state, terminating-or-diverging trace, post-state) of the source method's operational semantics.

Notation: [[c]] = set of behaviors of IVL command c; Q[e/x] = capture-avoiding substitution; VC(m) = wp(desugar_method(m), VcFormula::True) after pre/post wrapping; the solver is always asked check(NOT VC).

7.1 Invariant catalog (summary)

ID Invariant (one line) Owner crate Class Detector
INV-1 Verified ⇒ (NOT VC) is genuinely Unsat (soundness) spv-verify Soundness benchmark expected-FAIL set; differential vs Z3
INV-2 wp implements the five rules exactly (no rule dropped/weakened) spv-vc Soundness property tests; oracle re-derivation
INV-3 passify preserves VC validity: valid(VC) ⇔ valid(VC') spv-vc Soundness naive-vs-DSA equivalidity harness
INV-4 Desugaring is behavior-preserving: [[source m]] ⊆ [[desugar_method(m)]] spv-ivl Soundness trace-inclusion fuzzer; assert-coverage audit
INV-5 Loop inductive step never assumes the invariant it is proving spv-ivl Soundness structural lint on the cut; "weak-invariant" CX fixtures
INV-6 A Call uses only the callee contract, never its body spv-ivl/spv-verify Soundness+Modularity call-graph isolation check; body-mutation differential
INV-7 Havoc set ≡ exactly the modifies / loop-target set spv-ivl Soundness (under) + Completeness (over) frame-audit; under/over-havoc twin fixtures
INV-8 old(e) evaluates in the pre-state snapshot, never post spv-ast/spv-vc Soundness two-state fixtures; pre/post mutation test
INV-9 SmtResult::Unknown ⇒ never Verified; reported distinctly spv-verify Honesty timeout-injection; incompleteness fixtures
INV-10 Sat(model) ⇒ a Counterexample that re-executes to the failing assert spv-verify Faithfulness replay harness; model-soundness check
INV-11 IR is immutable-after-construction; every pass is pure/total all Determinism snapshot/idempotence tests
INV-12 VErrorKind of each VError matches the Reason of the failed assert spv-verify Diagnosis Reason↔Kind bijection audit

INV-1 — Soundness: Verified implies the program truly meets its spec

  • Statement. spv-verify emits VerifyResult::Verified for method m iff the solver returned SmtResult::Unsat for check(NOT VC(m)), and that Unsat is genuine. Equivalently: a program is correct iff VC is valid iff (NOT VC) is unsat; therefore a buggy program must never reach Verified.
  • Why it matters. The gravest failure mode. A false Verified is a silent lie about a correctness property — worse than a crash. All of INV-2..INV-8 exist solely to make INV-1 hold; a defect in any of them manifests here.
  • Where enforced. The Unsat => Verified / Sat => VError / Unknown => (separate) mapping in spv-verify. The only path to Verified is a literal SmtResult::Unsat; no other branch may construct VerifyResult::Verified.
  • Violation detection. Phase 13 benchmark suite: every expected-FAIL program must yield Failed; a single expected-FAIL turning Verified is a release-blocking soundness bug. Differential discharge vs Z3 (and #278) on the same SMT-LIB: disagreement where spv says Verified and Z3 says sat is a fatal encoding bug. Mutation testing: deleting any Assert from any IVL lowering must make at least one previously-failing fixture still fail (catches dropped obligations).
  • Telemetry. Per-method: {verdict, solver_result, vc_node_count, smt_assert_count, solve_ms}. Aggregate soundness gate: expected_fail_reported_verified == 0 (hard zero). Histogram of Unknown rate (INV-9).
  • Recovery. None at runtime — there is no safe fallback for a soundness defect. On suspicion, the verifier must fail closed: if the Unsat cannot be trusted (e.g., solver self-reports inconsistency), downgrade to Unknown, never up to Verified.
  • Debugging. Bisect the pipeline with the print-* subcommands: print-ivl (did an assert vanish? → INV-4/INV-7), print-vc (does wp match hand-derivation? → INV-2), print-smt (does (NOT VC) encode the negated obligation? → INV-9 mapping). Then re-run the failing fixture through the differential oracle.

INV-2 — wp implements the five rules exactly

  • Statement. For all IvlCmd c and VcFormula Q, wp(c, Q) equals, structurally:

    wp(Assume(p),      Q) = Imp(p, Q)            // p => Q
wp(Assert(p, _),   Q) = And([p, Q])          // p AND Q
wp(Havoc([x..]),   Q) = Forall([x..], _, Q)  // ∀ fresh x. Q
wp(Assign(x, e),   Q) = Q[e/x]               // substitute (capture-avoiding)
wp(Seq([c1,c2]),   Q) = wp(c1, wp(c2, Q))    // right-to-left fold
wp(Choice(c1,c2),  Q) = And([wp(c1,Q), wp(c2,Q)])

    No rule may be weakened (e.g., Assert collapsing to Imp, or Choice to Or).

  • Why it matters. wp is the VCGen semantics. Assert→And (not Imp) is what makes obligations provable rather than assumable; flipping it to Imp would make every assert vacuously dischargeable → mass false Verified. Choice→And (prove both branches), never Or.

  • Where enforced. spv-vc::wp — one exhaustive match arm per IvlCmd variant; #[deny(unreachable_patterns)] + no wildcard arm so a new variant forces a compile error rather than a silent default.

  • Violation detection. Property tests: for random c, Q, re-derive wp via an independent reference implementation and assert structural/logical equality (modulo simplification). Targeted fixtures pin each rule: Assert(false) must yield a non-valid VC; Havoc(x); Assert(x==0) must be invalid (∀x. x==0 is false); Assume(p); Assert(p) must be valid.

  • Telemetry. Count of wp invocations per variant; assertion that Forall quantifier count equals total havoc'd variable count (binder conservation).

  • Recovery. None — a wrong rule is a correctness defect. Guard: a debug-build assert! that wp(Assert(False, r), True) simplifies to False.

  • Debugging. print-vc on a one-line method exercising the suspect command; compare against the hand-applied rule. Bug class is almost always the Assert/Assume polarity or the Choice connective.

INV-3 — Passive form preserves VC validity (DSA equivalidity)

  • Statement. For every IvlCmd c, passify(c) is a passive (dynamic-single-assignment) command with valid(wp(c, Q)) ⇔ valid(wp(passify(c), Q)) for the wrapped Q. DSA replaces each Assign(x,e) with Assume(x_k == e) over fresh versions x_k, and merges Choice branches by renaming + equating live versions at the join — without changing the set of satisfying/violating states.
  • Why it matters. Naive wp over Assign (Q[e/x]) duplicates Q and Choice→And duplicates again ⇒ VC size exponential in path count (Flanagan–Saxe).
