DSA makes it ~linear, but only legitimately if it preserves validity; a buggy version-merge that adds a constraint can mask a counterexample → false Verified (a soundness escape), and one that drops a constraint → spurious failure.
  • Where enforced. spv-vc::passify, run before wp in the driver. Invariant maintained internally: every IVL variable is assigned at most once after passify; join points carry explicit Assume equalities linking branch versions to merged versions.
  • Violation detection. Equivalidity harness: on a corpus of small methods, discharge both wp(c,Q) (naive, may be large) and wp(passify(c),Q) (DSA); the SMT verdicts (Unsat/Sat) must agree for every fixture. VC-size regression: assert node_count(passify path) grows ≤ linearly while a deliberately-exponential nested-Choice fixture would blow the naive path.
  • Telemetry. {naive_vc_nodes, dsa_vc_nodes, blowup_ratio, version_count}; alert if blowup_ratio for DSA exceeds a path-count-independent bound. Target: nested if depth 20 stays under a few-thousand-node VC.
  • Recovery. If equivalidity ever fails for a fixture, disable DSA for that method and fall back to naive wp (slower but trusted) while flagging the input for triage — soundness over speed.
  • Debugging. Diff print-ivl pre/post passify (single-assignment property visible); diff print-vc for naive vs DSA; the defect is almost always a missing join-equality or a reused version number.

INV-4 — Desugaring is behavior-preserving ([[source]] ⊆ [[IVL]])

  • Statement. desugar_method(m) produces an IvlCmd whose behavior set over-approximates the source method: every concrete source execution corresponds to a permitted IVL trace, and every source obligation (assert, post, call-pre, invariant) appears as exactly one IvlCmd::Assert with a faithful Reason. if → Choice(Assume(g);then, Assume(!g);else); while → invariant cut (INV-5); Call → assert-pre / havoc-modifies / assume-post (INV-6); assign/assert/assume/return → their direct forms.
  • Why it matters. wp reasons about IVL, not source. If desugaring under-approximates (omits a reachable behavior, e.g. forgets the else-empty branch's fallthrough) or drops an assert, a buggy program can verify — INV-4 is the bridge that makes INV-1 about the actual program.
  • Where enforced. spv-ivl::desugar_method and the per-Stmt lowering helpers. Every Stmt::Assert, ensures, callee requires, and loop invariants element must yield ≥1 Assert(_, Reason{kind, span}); lowering is total over the typed AST (typecheck in spv-ast guarantees inputs are well-formed).
  • Violation detection. Assert-coverage audit: count source obligations and IVL asserts; mismatch ⇒ a dropped/duplicated obligation. Trace-inclusion fuzzer: generate random concrete programs + inputs, execute a reference operational semantics, and check each trace is admitted by the IVL (and that any failing source assert maps to a failing IVL assert). Mutation: corrupting a guard polarity in Choice must break a fixture.
  • Telemetry. {source_obligation_count, ivl_assert_count, choice_count, havoc_count} per method; the first two must be equal (after accounting for split ensures).
  • Recovery. None at runtime; lowering bugs are fixed in code. Defensive: debug_assert! that obligation/assert counts match before VC generation.
  • Debugging. print-ast then print-ivl side by side; trace each obligation to its Assert and each branch to its guarded Assume. Missing else fallthrough and swapped guard polarity are the common defects.

INV-5 — Loop handling never assumes the invariant on the inductive step

  • Statement. The invariant cut for while {guard, invariants: I, decreases: d, body} is, in order:

    assert(I)            // (a) holds on entry          Reason=LoopInvariantOnEntry
havoc(loop targets)  // (b) forget iteration state   (INV-7)
assume(I)            // (c) arbitrary iteration satisfying I
Choice(
  Seq[ assume(guard); body; assert(I); assume(False) ],  // (d) inductive step Reason=LoopInvariantNotMaintained
  assume(!guard) )                                       // (e) loop exit
// after: I AND !guard available to the continuation

    The step asserts I after the body (proving it is maintained); it must not assume(I) again after body, and the entry assert(I) (a) must precede the havoc (b). The assume(False) after the step-assert prunes the post-iteration path so only the exit branch flows out.

  • Why it matters. If the code mistakenly assume(I)s the post-body state, the maintenance obligation becomes vacuous → any invariant "verifies" → unsound. Conversely, the well-known usability pitfall: a weak/wrong user invariant makes assert (d) fail, producing a counterexample to the invariant, not the program — correct behavior, but must be reported as LoopInvariantNotMaintained, not as a program bug.

  • Where enforced. spv-ivl loop lowering. Structural lint: between the start of body lowering and assert (d) there is no Assume(I) over the same versions; the entry Assert precedes the Havoc.

  • Violation detection. "Vacuous-invariant" fixtures: a loop annotated with a false invariant must report LoopInvariantOnEntry (entry fails) or LoopInvariantNotMaintained — never Verified. "Weak-invariant" fixtures: a true-but-too-weak invariant must yield LoopInvariantNotMaintained with a CX. Termination: a loop with a non-decreasing decreases must report MightNotTerminate.

  • Telemetry. Per loop: {entry_assert, step_assert, exit_assume, havoc_targets, decreases_terms}; assert presence of all three obligations. Count of LoopInvariantNotMaintained verdicts (high rate ⇒ users writing weak invariants — a docs signal, not a bug).

  • Recovery. None; structurally fixed. The assume(False) after (d) is mandatory — its absence lets post-iteration state leak into the exit, weakening the post (a subtle unsoundness).

  • Debugging. print-ivl of the loop; verify the (a)→(b)→(c)→Choice[(d),(e)] shape and that (d) ends in assert(I); assume(False). For "false counterexample" user confusion, surface the failing invariant text via the Reason.span.

INV-6 — A call uses only the callee contract, never its body

  • Statement. Stmt::Call(lhs, callee, args) lowers to: assert(callee.requires[args/params]) (Reason=PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold); havoc(callee.modifies ∪ lhs); assume(callee.ensures[args/params, lhs/returns]). The callee's body is never inlined, unrolled, or otherwise consulted; verification of caller and callee are independent.
  • Why it matters. Modular verification is what makes the verifier scale (each method verified once against its contract). It also isolates faults: a buggy callee body cannot make a caller falsely fail or pass; only the contract relationship matters. Inlining a body would (a) break scalability and (b) couple correctness of caller to implementation of callee — both forbidden.
  • Where enforced. spv-ivl call lowering + spv-verify orchestration: the per-method VC build reads only Method.spec of callees, never Method.body. Recursive/mutually-recursive calls are handled by contract alone (no fixpoint over bodies).
  • Violation detection. Body-mutation differential: change a callee's body while keeping its spec fixed — every caller's verdict must be unchanged. Call-graph isolation check: assert the lowering touches no callee body field (enforced by an API that exposes only &Spec to the call lowerer). Frame fixture: a call whose modifies omits a variable the body would change must not let the caller assume that variable unchanged across the call (see INV-7).
  • Telemetry. Per call site: {callee, pre_asserted, modifies_havoced, post_assumed}; assert all three present. Count of call sites; depth of call graph (verification cost is independent of it).
  • Recovery. None; structural. If a callee lacks a contract (spec empty), its precondition is true and postcondition true — sound but maximally weak (caller learns nothing); this is reported, not silently strengthened.
  • Debugging. print-ivl of the caller around the call site: confirm the assert-pre / havoc-modifies / assume-post triple and that the body never appears. For "caller fails but callee is correct," inspect whether the callee's contract (not body) is too weak.

INV-7 — Havoc covers exactly the modifies / loop-target set

  • Statement. For a call, Havoc set = callee.modifies ∪ assigned-LValues(lhs). For a loop, Havoc set = the set of variables/locations assigned in the loop body (the loop targets). The set is exact: neither a subset (under-havoc) nor a superset (over-havoc).
  • Why it matters. This is a two-sided invariant:
    • Under-havoc ⇒ unsound. Failing to havoc a location the callee/loop may change lets the continuation assume a stale value → a buggy program verifies. (Soundness escape.)
    • Over-havoc ⇒ incomplete. Havocking a location that is provably unchanged forgets a true fact → a correct program fails to verify. (Completeness loss, not a lie, but a usability defect.) Framing of unmodified state (everything outside the set stays equal across the boundary) is what lets the caller/post-loop continuation retain known facts.
  • Where enforced. spv-ivl for both call and loop lowering; the modifies/target set is computed once during desugaring from Spec.modifies (calls) and a body-assignment scan (loops), and is the single source for the Havoc(Vec<Var>).
  • Violation detection. Twin fixtures: (under) a callee/loop that should change x but omits x from modifies/targets must surface either a ModifiesViolation (callee body violates its own modifies) or a downstream failure — never a false Verified; (over) a loop body that does not touch y but havocs y must be flagged by the over-havoc audit (verification fails where a non-havoc version verifies). Frame audit: assert havoc_set == declared_modifies ∪ lhs (calls) and havoc_set == scanned_assignments (loops), set-equality, not subset.
  • Telemetry. Per boundary: |havoc_set|, |declared_modifies|, |scanned_targets|; alert on any inequality. Track ModifiesViolation count (callee body assigning outside its modifies — itself a checked obligation).
  • Recovery. Fail closed toward soundness: if the target set cannot be computed precisely, over-approximate (havoc more) — sacrificing completeness, never soundness. Never under-approximate.
  • Debugging. print-ivl: read the Havoc([...]) arg at the boundary and diff against the source modifies clause / a manual scan of body assignments. Under-havoc shows as a missing variable; over-havoc as an extra one.

INV-8 — old(e) evaluates in the pre-state

  • Statement. Within a postcondition (ensures) or contract reasoning, Expr::Old(e) denotes the value of e in the method's entry (pre) state, captured before the body executes; it must never read the post-state. spv-ast already rejects old() outside ensures (TypeError::OldOutsideEnsures); spv-vc must encode the two-state semantics correctly.
  • Why it matters. Postconditions like ensures x == old(x) + 1 are the core of frame/effect specs. Mis-evaluating old(x) as the post value collapses such a spec to a tautology (x == x + 1 becomes nonsense or, worse, x == x after a wrong rewrite) → spec says nothing → unsound (a buggy increment "verifies").
  • Where enforced. Two-state lowering in spv-vc/spv-ivl: at method entry, snapshot each variable referenced under old(...) into a fresh pre-version x_old (via Assume(x_old == x) before the body); every Old(e) translates to e over the _old versions. The DSA pass (INV-3) keeps the pre-versions distinct from later versions.
  • Violation detection. Two-state fixtures: ensures x == old(x) + 1 over x := x + 1 must verify; over x := x + 2 must report PostconditionMightNotHold with a CX where old(x)≠post. Pre/post mutation test: swapping old(x)→x in the encoder must break a fixture. Nested old(old(e)) is idempotent (still pre-state) — pinned by a fixture.
  • Telemetry. Count of distinct _old snapshot versions per method; assert one per variable appearing under old. Flag any old reference whose pre-version was not snapshotted.
  • Recovery. None; encoding bug fixed in code. Defensive: assert no Old(_) node survives into the final VcFormula (all must be rewritten to _old versions).
  • Debugging. print-vc: confirm _old versions appear in the postcondition and are equated to entry values via entry Assumes; confirm no raw Old term reaches spv-smt.

INV-9 — Unknown is reported, never silently treated as Verified

  • Statement. SmtSolver::check may return SmtResult::Unknown (timeout, quantifier incompleteness, solver gives up). The driver maps only Unsat → Verified; Unknown maps to a distinct, explicit outcome (surfaced as a non-Verified verdict, e.g. an Unknown/inconclusive report), and Sat → Failed. There is no code path from Unknown to Verified.
  • Why it matters. Completeness is bounded by SMT (quantifiers, nonlinear arithmetic, triggers). Treating "I couldn't prove the negation unsat" as "the negation is unsat" is the textbook unsoundness — it confuses failure to refute with proof. INV-9 keeps Unknown honest and preserves INV-1.
  • Where enforced. spv-verify discharge mapping (the match SmtResult), with no wildcard that could fold Unknown into the Verified arm. Per-method incremental solving uses push/pop; on timeout the solver returns Unknown, which is propagated.
  • Violation detection. Timeout-injection: a fixture with a tiny solver timeout must report inconclusive, never Verified. Incompleteness fixtures: a valid-but-trigger-starved quantified VC may legitimately come back Unknown and must be reported as such. Audit: assert the Verified constructor is reachable from exactly one source location (the Unsat arm).
  • Telemetry. Unknown rate per run, per VErrorKind-less bucket; solver solve_ms distribution; timeout_count. Rising Unknown rate ⇒ trigger/encoding regression (actionable, but not a soundness alarm).
  • Recovery. On Unknown, optionally retry with a larger timeout / alternative triggers / the alternate solver behind the SmtSolver trait (#278 vs Z3); still never escalate to Verified. The verdict for the user remains inconclusive until a genuine Unsat.
  • Debugging. print-smt to inspect quantifier triggers and sort encodings; reproduce against Z3 directly to confirm the Unknown is solver-side, then tune triggers (Phase 9) rather than weakening the VC.

INV-10 — A Sat model always reconstructs to a re-executing counterexample

  • Statement. When check(NOT VC) returns SmtResult::Sat(model), spv-verify reconstructs a Counterexample { bindings, at } such that: (a) DSA/skolem names in the Model map back to source variable names + the failing program point (at = the Span from the failed assert's Reason); (b) executing the source method from a pre-state consistent with bindings actually reaches that program point with the asserted predicate false. The counterexample re-executes to the failing assertion.
  • Why it matters. A counterexample that does not re-execute is misleading — it erodes trust and can hide the real bug. INV-10 makes Failed actionable and is the dual of INV-1: just as Verified must be earned by Unsat, Failed must be witnessed by a concrete trace.
  • Where enforced. spv-verify counterexample reconstruction (Phase 11): parse Model, invert the DSA version map to recover source Atoms, pick the Reason.span of the assert whose negation the model satisfies, and populate Counterexample. VErrorKind is taken from that assert's Reason.kind (see INV-12).
  • Violation detection. Replay harness: for each expected-FAIL fixture, take the produced bindings, run the reference operational semantics from that pre-state, and assert the trace reaches at with the predicate false; a non-reproducing CX is a reconstruction bug. Model-soundness check: substitute bindings back into (NOT VC) and confirm it evaluates true (the model really satisfies the negation).
  • Telemetry. {cx_bindings_count, cx_replay_ok, mapped_unmapped_vars}; assert cx_replay_ok == true for every Failed. Count of DSA-version inversions; flag any model variable that fails to map to a source Atom.
  • Recovery. If a model fails to reconstruct (e.g., a skolem name with no source preimage), report the raw failing assertion + Span with a partial/best-effort binding and flag the model for triage — degrade the diagnostic richness, never claim Verified. (Failed is still correct; only the witness detail is reduced.)
  • Debugging. print-smt + the solver's raw model; trace each DSA version back through passify's version map; confirm the chosen failing assert's Reason.span points at the right source Stmt::Assert / ensures / invariant.

INV-11 — IR is immutable-after-construction; every pass is pure and total

  • Statement. Program, the typed AST, IvlCmd, VcFormula, and SmtTerm are immutable once built; each pipeline stage (Parser → TypeChecker → desugar_method → passify → wp → encode → check) is a pure function of its input (no hidden mutable global state) and total over well-typed inputs (no panics on valid input). Re-running a stage on identical input yields an identical output (determinism).
  • Why it matters. Immutability lets the IR be shared safely (Rust ownership) and makes every other invariant checkable: a pass that mutates shared IR could make print-vc and the discharged VC diverge, defeating debugging and reproducibility. Determinism is required for the differential and snapshot tests that guard INV-1..INV-10.
  • Where enforced. All spv-* crates: IR types expose no interior mutability; passes take &Input and return owned Output. spv-syntax/spv-ast are no_std + alloc (no ambient I/O, hence no nondeterminism); only spv-cli is std.
  • Violation detection. Idempotence/snapshot tests: wp(c,Q), passify(c), and desugar_method(m) must produce byte-identical print-* output across repeated runs and across processes. Determinism check: hash the VC and SMT-LIB; the hash must be stable for a fixed input. Any panic on a typechecked input is a totality bug (fuzzed in Phase 13).
  • Telemetry. Per stage: {output_hash, node_count, elapsed_ms}; alert on hash instability for unchanged inputs. Panic/Unknown-on-valid counters from the fuzzer.
  • Recovery. Determinism defects are fixed in code (typical causes: HashMap iteration order leaking into output — use ordered maps / sorted iteration). No runtime recovery; the build gate rejects non-deterministic output.
  • Debugging. Diff print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt across two runs; the first stage whose output differs localizes the nondeterminism (usually unordered iteration in name resolution or trigger collection).

INV-12 — VErrorKind matches the Reason of the failed assertion

  • Statement. Every IvlCmd::Assert carries Reason { kind: VErrorKind, span: Span } recording why it exists. When that assert is the one the Sat model refutes, the resulting VError.kind equals Reason.kind and VError.span == Reason.span. The mapping is a bijection from obligation-origin to verdict classification across all eight VErrorKinds: AssertionMightNotHold, PostconditionMightNotHold, PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, DivisionByZero, ModifiesViolation, MightNotTerminate.
  • Why it matters. A correct diagnosis (which kind of obligation failed, at which source point) is what turns a Failed into a fix. Mislabeling (e.g. reporting a maintained-invariant failure as a plain AssertionMightNotHold) wastes user time and hides the loop usability pitfall (INV-5). This is diagnostic faithfulness, the partner of INV-10.
  • Where enforced. Reason is attached at the point of lowering in spv-ivl (each desugaring step that emits an Assert sets the correct kind/span); spv-verify reads Reason straight into VError during reconstruction — it never re-classifies from VC shape.
  • Violation detection. Reason↔Kind audit fixtures: one expected-FAIL per VErrorKind, asserting the reported kind equals the intended one (e.g. a failing call-pre fixture ⇒ PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold; x / 0 ⇒ DivisionByZero; non-decreasing variant ⇒ MightNotTerminate; body assigning outside modifies ⇒ ModifiesViolation). Coverage check: every VErrorKind is reachable from ≥1 lowering site (no orphan kinds).
  • Telemetry. Distribution of reported VErrorKinds across the benchmark suite; assert each kind is exercised. Flag any VError whose span does not resolve to a source location (lost Reason.span).
  • Recovery. If a failed assert's Reason is somehow absent (should be impossible — construction sets it), default to AssertionMightNotHold with the assert's Span and flag the lowering site as missing a Reason — preserve a Failed verdict and some span, never drop the error.
  • Debugging. print-ivl shows each Assert's Reason; match the refuted assert's Reason.kind/span against the emitted VError. A mismatch localizes to the lowering step that built the assert (loop cut, call lowering, post wrapping, or a divide/termination check).

7.4 Invariant enforcement matrix

Invariant Compile-time guard Test-suite guard (Phase 13) Runtime guard Fail-mode on breach
INV-1 Soundness single Verified constructor site expected-FAIL == 0 verified; differential vs Z3 fail-closed to Unknown release blocker
INV-2 wp rules exhaustive match, no wildcard per-rule fixtures + oracle debug_assert! polarity wrong VC
INV-3 DSA validity — naive↔DSA equivalidity fall back to naive wp size blowup / mask CX
INV-4 desugar fidelity total lowering over typed AST assert-coverage + trace-inclusion fuzz debug_assert! count match dropped obligation
INV-5 loop cut structural lint on cut shape weak/false-invariant fixtures mandatory assume(False) vacuous invariant
INV-6 contract-only call lowerer sees only &Spec body-mutation differential — unmodular / unsound
INV-7 havoc exactness set built once from modifies/scan under/over-havoc twins over-approx if unsure unsound / incomplete
INV-8 old() pre-state OldOutsideEnsures typecheck two-state + mutation fixtures assert no Old in VC tautological spec
INV-9 Unknown honesty no Unknown→Verified arm timeout-injection fixtures retry, never escalate false Verified
INV-10 CX replays — replay + model-soundness harness partial CX on map-fail misleading diagnosis
INV-11 IR immutable/pure no interior mutability; no_std cores idempotence/snapshot/hash ordered iteration nondeterminism
INV-12 Reason↔Kind Reason set at lowering site one fixture per VErrorKind default + flag on missing mislabeled error

Hierarchy. INV-1 is the apex; it is derived from INV-2 (correct wp) ∧ INV-3 (DSA preserves validity) ∧ INV-4 (faithful IVL) ∧ INV-5..INV-8 (correct loop/call/havoc/old encodings) ∧ INV-9 (honest Unknown). INV-10 and INV-12 do not affect soundness but make every Failed verdict trustworthy and actionable. INV-11 underpins the testability of all the rest. Any breach of INV-1..INV-9 is treated as a soundness incident and gates release; INV-10/INV-11/INV-12 breaches gate on diagnostic-quality and reproducibility regressions.

8. Domain Model — Source Language, IVL, and Verification Conditions

The verifier is a five-stage lowering: source AST (spv-syntax) → typed AST (spv-ast) → IVL guarded commands IvlCmd (spv-ivl) → passive-form IVL + VC VcFormula (spv-vc) → SMT-LIB SmtTerm (spv-smt). This section pins the meaning of each representation; later phases (## 12–## 25) implement them. Soundness is the cardinal invariant: every lowering must over-approximate the program's executions so a buggy program can never be lost (no execution dropped between stages).

8.1 Source language — EBNF

The surface syntax parsed by spv-syntax's Lexer + recursive-descent Parser into Program. ASCII; // … line comments; identifiers interned as Atom. Operator precedence (low→high): ==>, ||, &&, == != < <= > >=, + -, * / %, unary ! -. ==> is right-assoc; arithmetic left-assoc.

program     = { decl } ;
decl        = method | function | axiom ;
method      = "method" Ident "(" [ params ] ")" [ "returns" "(" params ")" ] spec ( block | ";" ) ;
function    = "function" Ident "(" [ params ] ")" ":" type spec [ "{" expr "}" ] ;
axiom       = "axiom" expr ";" ;
params      = param { "," param } ;
param       = Ident ":" type ;
spec        = { requires | ensures | modifies | decreases } ;
requires    = "requires" expr ";" ;
ensures     = "ensures"  expr ";" ;        (* may contain old(...) *)
modifies    = "modifies" expr { "," expr } ";" ;
decreases   = "decreases" expr { "," expr } ";" ;
block       = "{" { stmt } "}" ;
stmt        = vardecl | assign | ifstmt | whilestmt | callstmt
            | "assert" expr ";" | "assume" expr ";" | "return" ";" ;
vardecl     = "var" Ident ":" type [ ":=" expr ] ";" ;
assign      = lvalue ":=" expr ";" ;
callstmt    = [ lvalue { "," lvalue } ":=" ] "call" Ident "(" [ args ] ")" ";" ;
ifstmt      = "if" "(" expr ")" block [ "else" block ] ;
whilestmt   = "while" "(" expr ")" { "invariant" expr ";" } { "decreases" expr ";" } block ;
lvalue      = Ident | expr "." Ident ;     (* var or field (heap) *)
args        = expr { "," expr } ;
expr        = quant | impl ;
impl        = orexpr [ "==>" impl ] ;
orexpr      = andexpr { "||" andexpr } ;
andexpr     = cmp { "&&" cmp } ;
cmp         = add { ( "==" | "!=" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=" ) add } ;
add         = mul { ( "+" | "-" ) mul } ;
mul         = unary { ( "*" | "/" | "%" ) unary } ;
unary       = ( "!" | "-" ) unary | atom ;
atom        = literal | Ident | call_expr | "old" "(" expr ")"
            | expr "." Ident | "(" expr ")" ;
call_expr   = Ident "(" [ args ] ")" ;     (* pure-function application *)
quant       = ( "forall" | "exists" ) qvars [ trigger ] "::" expr ;
qvars       = Ident ":" type { "," Ident ":" type } ;
trigger     = "{" expr { "," expr } "}" { "{" expr { "," expr } "}" } ;
type        = "int" | "bool" | "ref" | "map" "<" type "," type ">"
            | "seq" "<" type ">" | Ident ;
literal     = Number | "true" | "false" | "null" ;

Distinction (LOCKED): callstmt (the Stmt::Call guarded command, side-effecting, contract-checked) vs call_expr (the Expr::Call pure-function application, used inside formulas). method with ; instead of a block = an abstract method (contract only, body: None) — verified by clients, not itself.

8.2 Surface AST → resolved/typed AST

spv-syntax emits the AST shells in §Core Types; spv-ast resolves Atoms to binding sites and annotates every Expr with a Type. Mapping is structural and 1:1:

EBNF production AST node
method Decl::Method(Method{name,params,returns,spec,body})
function Decl::Function(Function)
axiom Decl::Axiom(Expr)
requires/ensures/modifies/decreases Spec{requires,ensures,modifies,decreases} (each Vec<Expr>)
vardecl Stmt::VarDecl(Atom,Type,Option<Expr>)
assign Stmt::Assign(LValue,Expr)
ifstmt Stmt::If(Expr,Block,Block) (empty else ⇒ empty Block)
whilestmt Stmt::While{guard,invariants,decreases,body}
callstmt Stmt::Call(Vec<LValue>,Atom,Vec<Expr>)
assert/assume/return Stmt::Assert/Assume/Return
==> / && / + / == … Expr::Bin(BinOp,…)
! / - Expr::Un(UnOp,…)
old(e) Expr::Old(Box<Expr>)
forall/exists Expr::Quant(Quantifier,Vec<(Atom,Type)>,Vec<Trigger>,Box<Expr>)
e.f Expr::Field(Box<Expr>,Atom)
call_expr Expr::Call(Atom,Vec<Expr>)
literals Expr::Lit(Value)

8.3 Type system

Type ∈ { Int, Bool, Ref, Map(K,V), Seq(E), Named(Atom) }. No subtyping, no implicit coercion, structural equality on Type. Typing judgement Γ ⊢ e : T (Γ = params ∪ returns ∪ in-scope vars ∪ quantifier vars).

Construct Rule (telegraphic) Failure ⇒ TypeError
Var x x:T ∈ Γ ⇒ Int/Bool/… Undeclared(x)
Lit n / true,false / null Int / Bool / Ref —
e1 + e2 (- * / %) both Int ⇒ Int Mismatch{expected:Int,got}
e1 < e2 (<= > >=) both Int ⇒ Bool Mismatch{Int,got}
e1 == e2 (!=) same T ⇒ Bool Mismatch{ty(e1),ty(e2)}
e1 && e2 (||, ==>) both Bool ⇒ Bool Mismatch{Bool,got}
!e Bool ⇒ Bool; -e: Int⇒Int Mismatch
m[k] (map sel) m:Map(K,V) ∧ k:K ⇒ V Mismatch
s[i] (seq sel) s:Seq(E) ∧ i:Int ⇒ E Mismatch
e.f e:Ref, f declared field of type T ⇒ T BadModifies/Undeclared(f)
f(a…) (pure fn) f:(T1..Tn)->T, ai:Ti, count=n ⇒ T ArityMismatch / Mismatch
old(e) ty(old e)=ty(e); legal context only OldOutsideEnsures
forall/exists x:T :: body body:Bool under Γ,x:T ⇒ Bool NonBoolSpec

Statement well-typedness: Assign(lv,e) ⇒ ty(lv)=ty(e); If/While guard :Bool; Assert/Assume arg :Bool; Call(outs,m,ins) ⇒ ins match m.params, outs match m.returns (else ArityMismatch/Mismatch); return only in method body.

8.4 Spec well-formedness (spv-ast)

Beyond typing, four spec-shape checks (a malformed spec is a tool input error, never silently dropped — that would threaten soundness):

Rule Constraint Violation ⇒ TypeError
WF-1 Boolean every requires/ensures/invariant clause :Bool NonBoolSpec
WF-2 old-scope Old(e) only inside ensures (and 2-state postconds); never in requires/invariant/decreases/body OldOutsideEnsures
WF-3 modifies each modifies term is an l-value path (x or e.f over Ref); requires/ensures may only mutate names in modifies BadModifies
WF-4 decreases each decreases term :Int (or a tuple of ints, lexicographic); present on every while needing total correctness and on recursive functions Mismatch{Int,got}

8.5 IVL grammar — IvlCmd (six forms)

spv-ivl lowers the typed AST to a Boogie-like guarded-command IVL. The whole language is six constructors — the deliberate minimalism is what makes wp (§8.7) a six-rule function.

IvlCmd = "assume" Expr                       (* assume p — restrict states to p     *)
       | "assert" Expr Reason                (* assert p — proof obligation (tagged) *)
       | "havoc" Var { "," Var }             (* set vars to arbitrary values         *)
       | Var ":=" Expr                       (* assign                               *)
       | IvlCmd { ";" IvlCmd }               (* Seq — sequencing                     *)
       | IvlCmd "[]" IvlCmd ;                (* Choice — nondeterministic choice     *)

Maps to IvlCmd::{Assume(Expr), Assert(Expr,Reason), Havoc(Vec<Var>), Assign(Var,Expr), Seq(Vec<IvlCmd>), Choice(Box<IvlCmd>,Box<IvlCmd>)}. Every Assert carries Reason{kind:VErrorKind, span:Span} so that a Sat model later (## 23) names which source assertion failed. Operational semantics: assume p blocks executions where ¬p; assert p fails (is an error) on executions where ¬p; havoc forks over all values; [] forks over both branches. Verification = no execution reaches a failing assert.

8.6 Desugaring rules — source construct → IvlCmd

desugar_method(m: &Method) -> IvlCmd produces, for method m: assume(⋀requires) ; havoc returns,locals ; ⟦body⟧ ; assert(⋀ensures, PostconditionMightNotHold). The body translation ⟦·⟧ (LOCKED):

Source construct IvlCmd lowering Reason.kind of generated asserts
x := e x := e —
var x:T := e x := e (fresh Var x) —
var x:T; havoc x —
assert p assert p AssertionMightNotHold
assume p assume p —
e1 / e2, e1 % e2 precede use with assert e2 != 0 DivisionByZero
s1 ; s2 ⟦s1⟧ ; ⟦s2⟧ —
if (g) {s1} else {s2} (assume g ; ⟦s1⟧) [] (assume !g ; ⟦s2⟧) —
call o := m(a) assert ⋀m.requires[a/params] ; havoc m.modifies ; o := havoc'd ; assume ⋀m.ensures[a/params, o/returns] PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold
return assume true (control reaches method-exit assert) —
while (g) inv I dec D {body} invariant cut, see below LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, MightNotTerminate

Loop invariant cut (Phase 6, ## 18) — loops are never unrolled; an invariant I = ⋀invariants cuts the loop into three obligations spliced as one straight-line IvlCmd:

assert I                       Reason: LoopInvariantOnEntry           (I holds before loop)
havoc t1,…,tk                  (t = loop targets: vars assigned in body)
assume I                       (arbitrary iteration starting in I)
(  assume g                                                          (inductive step…)
   ⟦body⟧
   assert I                    Reason: LoopInvariantNotMaintained     (…re-establishes I)
   assert D < old(D) && D>=0   Reason: MightNotTerminate              (variant decreases)
   assume false                                                      (cut: step explores no further)
) [] (
   assume !g                                                         (exit branch continues)
)

After the cut, the continuation observes only I ∧ ¬g. assume false makes the inductive-step branch contribute nothing to the post — that branch only discharges "I preserved". The pitfall (LOCKED): a too-weak I makes the LoopInvariantNotMaintained assert fail with a counterexample to the user's invariant, not the program.

Frame / modifies (Phase 5, ## 17): havoc m.modifies scrambles exactly the contracted set; everything outside it is implicitly framed (unchanged). A callee that mutates outside its modifies is caught by ModifiesViolation asserts emitted at the call site. Modular verification ⇒ a call is checked against the callee contract, never its body — the scaling property.

8.7 Weakest-precondition calculus — wp

wp(c: &IvlCmd, post: VcFormula) -> VcFormula in spv-vc. Exactly six rules (one per IvlCmd):

c wp(c, Q) intuition
assume p p ==> Q only states satisfying p survive
assert p p ∧ Q p must hold and the rest
havoc x ∀x. Q Q must hold for all values of x
x := e Q[e/x] substitute (only in passive form; see §8.8)
c1 ; c2 wp(c1, wp(c2, Q)) right-to-left threading
c1 [] c2 wp(c1, Q) ∧ wp(c2, Q) both branches must establish Q

The method VC = wp(desugar_method(m), True). Theorem (Hoare/Dijkstra, LOCKED): {P} c {Q} holds iff P ==> wp(c,Q). Hence the program meets its spec iff the VC is valid, which holds iff ¬VC is unsatisfiable in SMT. verify therefore asks the solver to refute ¬VC: Unsat ⇒ Verified; Sat(model) ⇒ VError + Counterexample. Partial correctness omits the MightNotTerminate asserts; total correctness includes them.

8.8 Passive form (dynamic single assignment)

passify(c: &IvlCmd) -> IvlCmd (Phase 8, ## 20). Naïve wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x] duplicates Q at every assignment, and [] duplicates again — wp over n assignments + m choices is O(2^m) in formula size (the exponential blowup, Flanagan–Saxe 2001). DSA fixes it: rename each variable to a fresh version x_0, x_1, … so every version is assigned exactly once; rewrite x_i := e into assume x_i == e[versions]; at a Choice join, havoc x_j to the max version, then each branch assume x_j == x_branch. With assignments now assumes of equalities, wp introduces no substitution duplication ⇒ VC size grows ~linearly.

Worked DSA on if (x>0){ y:=x } else { y:=0-x }; assert y>=0:

source IVL:  (assume x>0 ; y:=x) [] (assume !(x>0) ; y:=0-x) ; assert y>=0  [AssertionMightNotHold]

passive (DSA):                                  version map: x→x0; y on entry y0, joined y3
  ( assume x0>0  ; assume y1 == x0   )
  []
  ( assume !(x0>0) ; assume y2 == 0-x0 )
  ; assume (x0>0  ==> y3==y1) && (!(x0>0) ==> y3==y2)
  ; assert y3>=0

wp of the passive command into True (six rules, no blowup):

VC =  ( x0>0  ==> y1==x0  ==> ( (x0>0==>y3==y1)&&(!(x0>0)==>y3==y2) ==> y3>=0 ) )
   && ( !(x0>0) ==> y2==0-x0 ==> ( (x0>0==>y3==y1)&&(!(x0>0)==>y3==y2) ==> y3>=0 ) )

8.9 VcFormula (FOL) → SMT-LIB

VcFormula is sorted first-order logic: True | False | And(Vec) | Or(Vec) | Not | Imp | Forall(vars,triggers) | Exists(vars) | Atom(Pred). Pred = an atomic predicate over terms (arithmetic comparisons, equalities, uninterpreted-function applications). spv-smt maps it to SmtTerm over SmtSort ∈ { Int, Bool, Uninterp(Atom), Array(K,V) }, emitting SMT-LIB 2:

VcFormula / Type SMT-LIB
Type::Int / Bool Int / Bool
Type::Ref / Named(t) (declare-sort t 0) ⇒ Uninterp(t)
Type::Map(K,V) / Seq(E) (Array K V) / (Array Int E)
And[..] / Or[..] (and …) / (or …)
Not / Imp(a,b) (not a) / (=> a b)
Atom(eq a b) / < (= a b) / (< a b)
map/seq select m[k] (select m k)
map/seq store m[k:=v] (store m k v)
Forall(vs,trigs,b) (forall (vs…) (! b :pattern (trigs…)))
Exists(vs,b) (exists (vs…) b)
pure fn f (declare-fun f (T1..Tn) T) + defining (assert (forall … (= (f x) body))) w/ fuel if recursive

The solver call is (assert (not VC)) (check-sat) via SmtSolver::check(&[SmtTerm]) -> SmtResult. Triggers ({…}) guide quantifier instantiation; a bad/missing trigger ⇒ Unknown or a matching loop, not unsoundness. Unknown (timeout/incompleteness) is surfaced explicitly, never treated as Verified (soundness).

8.10 Fully worked example — Max

Source (spv-syntax):

method Max(a: int, b: int) returns (m: int)
  ensures m >= a;
  ensures m >= b;
  ensures m == a || m == b;
{
  if (a >= b) { m := a; } else { m := b; }
}

Typed AST (spv-ast): Γ = {a:Int, b:Int, m:Int}; all three ensures clauses type Bool (WF-1 ✓); no old() (WF-2 vacuous); empty modifies (WF-3 ✓). IVL (desugar_method):

assume true ;                                      (no requires)
havoc m ;
( (assume a>=b ; m:=a) [] (assume !(a>=b) ; m:=b) ) ;
assert (m>=a) && (m>=b) && (m==a || m==b)          Reason: PostconditionMightNotHold

Passive (DSA): m versions m1 (then-branch), m2 (else), joined m3:

( assume a>=b   ; assume m1==a )
[] ( assume !(a>=b) ; assume m2==b )
; assume (a>=b ==> m3==m1) && (!(a>=b) ==> m3==m2)
; assert (m3>=a)&&(m3>=b)&&(m3==a||m3==b)

VC (= wp(…, True), both [] branches conjoined; abbreviate the post as P(m3)):

VC =  ( a>=b   ==> m1==a ==> Join ==> P(m3) )
   && ( !(a>=b) ==> m2==b ==> Join ==> P(m3) )
   where Join = (a>=b==>m3==m1)&&(!(a>=b)==>m3==m2),  P(m)=(m>=a)&&(m>=b)&&(m==a||m==b)

SMT (spv-smt, ¬VC):

(declare-const a Int)(declare-const b Int)
(declare-const m1 Int)(declare-const m2 Int)(declare-const m3 Int)
(assert (not (and
  (=> (>= a b) (=> (= m1 a) (=> (and (=> (>= a b)(= m3 m1)) (=> (not (>= a b))(= m3 m2)))
        (and (>= m3 a)(>= m3 b)(or (= m3 a)(= m3 b))))))
  (=> (not (>= a b)) (=> (= m2 b) (=> (and (=> (>= a b)(= m3 m1)) (=> (not (>= a b))(= m3 m2)))
        (and (>= m3 a)(>= m3 b)(or (= m3 a)(= m3 b)))))))))
(check-sat)        ; => unsat   ⇒  VerifyResult::Verified

Buggy variant — drop the third ensures and write else { m := b; } as else { m := a; } (returns a always):

method MaxBug(a,b) returns (m) ensures m>=a; ensures m>=b;
{ if (a>=b){ m:=a; } else { m:=a; } }

VC negation is now Sat. SmtResult::Sat(Model{ a:=0, b:=5, m3:=0 }). spv-verify (## 23) maps m3 → m, reads Reason{kind:PostconditionMightNotHold, span:<ensures m>=b>}, and reconstructs:

VError { kind: PostconditionMightNotHold,
         span: <line: ensures m >= b>,
         counterexample: Counterexample {
           bindings: [(a,Int(0)),(b,Int(5)),(m,Int(0))], at: <method exit> } }
⇒ VerifyResult::Failed([VError])

8.11 Loop example — SumTo (invariant cut + DSA)

method SumTo(n: int) returns (s: int)
  requires n >= 0;
  ensures s == n*(n+1)/2;
{
  var i: int := 0; s := 0;
  while (i < n)
    invariant 0 <= i && i <= n;
    invariant s == i*(i+1)/2;
    decreases n - i;
  { i := i + 1; s := s + i; }
}

IVL (cut per §8.6; targets t = {i, s}; I = 0<=i && i<=n && s==i*(i+1)/2; D = n-i):

assume n>=0 ; havoc s ; i:=0 ; s:=0 ;
assert I                                   LoopInvariantOnEntry
havoc i,s ; assume I ;
( assume i<n ; i:=i+1 ; s:=s+i ;
  assert I                                 LoopInvariantNotMaintained
  assert (n-i < old(n-i)) && (n-i >= 0)    MightNotTerminate
  assume false )
[] ( assume !(i<n) ) ;
assert s == n*(n+1)/2                       PostconditionMightNotHold

After passify + wp the VC is valid (Unsat of ¬VC) ⇒ Verified. Two failure surfaces to test (Phase 13, ## 25): (1) drop invariant s == i*(i+1)/2 ⇒ the LoopInvariantNotMaintained assert is Sat (i=0,s=0 start, body gives s=1 but I can't relate it) — counterexample is to the weak invariant, not the code. (2) drop the decreases under total correctness ⇒ MightNotTerminate obligation unprovable ⇒ Sat.

8.12 Malformed inputs → diagnostics

Each well-formedness violation is rejected before VC generation (a dropped check would let a buggy program verify — the gravest failure):

Malformed source Caught by Diagnostic
ensures m + 1; (Int spec) WF-1, spv-ast TypeError::NonBoolSpec
requires old(a) > 0; WF-2 TypeError::OldOutsideEnsures
{ y := x + 1; } with x undeclared resolution TypeError::Undeclared("x")
var b: bool := 3; §8.3 TypeError::Mismatch{expected:Bool, got:Int}
m := Max(a); (Max takes 2) §8.3 Call TypeError::ArityMismatch
modifies a + b; (not an l-value) WF-3 TypeError::BadModifies
while(i<n){ i:=i+1; } no invariant desugar + VC not a type error: Assume true invariant ⇒ inductive LoopInvariantNotMaintained/post Sat ⇒ VError, not Verified
forall x:int :: x + 1 (Int body) WF-1 TypeError::NonBoolSpec
if (n) {…} (Int guard) §8.3 TypeError::Mismatch{expected:Bool, got:Int}
lex/parse garbage (method 7() spv-syntax ParseError{kind:UnexpectedToken} / BadSpec / BadType

Diagnostics ordering: ParseError (spv-syntax) precedes TypeError (spv-ast); both precede any IVL/VC work. A missing loop invariant is intentionally not a static error — it manifests as an honest VError so the user learns the invariant is too weak, consistent with the deductive-verification contract (§8.7).

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Architecture decisions for the spv deductive program verifier. Each row is a locked choice with its rejected alternatives and the engineering rationale (form: "X because Y; alternative rejected because Z"). Soundness is the cardinal axis — any decision that risks letting a buggy program verify is rejected regardless of convenience. ADRs are immutable once accepted; supersession requires a new numbered row, never an in-place edit.

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 OCaml/F# (Why3/Boogie lineage); C/C++; Python; Haskell Pipeline = parse → typecheck → IVL → VC → SMT; Rust enums + exhaustive match model AST, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm as zero-cost tagged unions, no null. Ownership keeps immutable-after-construction IR safe to share. Correctness on untrusted annotated input demands memory safety. OCaml/F# semantically fine but weaker systems story/tooling; C/C++ memory-unsafe IR graphs; Python too slow for VC/SMT term construction at scale + no sum types; Haskell laziness obscures term-size/perf reasoning. OCaml wins when the primary team is an academic PL/theorem-prover group with existing OCaml infrastructure (e.g. Why3, Coq, CompCert) and deployment as a research artifact where systems-level embedding is not required.
ADR-02 Workspace decomposition Seven crates: spv-syntax, spv-ast, spv-ivl, spv-vc, spv-smt, spv-verify, spv-cli Single monolithic crate; one crate per file; merge spv-ivl+spv-vc Crate boundaries = pipeline-stage boundaries → each stage independently testable, fuzzable, benchmarkable; dependency DAG is strictly linear (syntax→ast→ivl→vc→smt→verify, cli on top) so no stage can reach back into a later one (e.g. VCGen cannot touch the parser). Monolith hides illegal cross-stage coupling; per-file crates explode build graph; merging ivl+vc conflates desugaring with the wp calculus, the two stages most independently fuzzed. A single-crate layout wins when the tool is a proof-of-concept prototype with one engineer and <1k lines, where crate-boundary overhead (Cargo.toml upkeep, inter-crate feature flags, longer clean builds) outweighs the organizational benefit.
ADR-03 spv-syntax front end style Hand-written Lexer + recursive-descent Parser → Program AST, no_std+alloc Parser generator (LALRPOP, pest); parser combinators (nom) Annotated imperative grammar (methods, requires/ensures/modifies/decreases, old(), quantifiers + triggers) needs precise ParseError/ParseErrorKind spans for downstream VError mapping; hand-rolled gives full control over error recovery + span fidelity, zero codegen, fast builds. no_std+alloc keeps the front end embeddable. Generators emit opaque errors + hurt spans; combinators bloat error messages and stack depth. A parser generator (LALRPOP) wins for a full production language where the grammar is large (>100 productions), frequently evolves, and the team is willing to accept generator-opaque error messages in exchange for grammar correctness guarantees and automatic conflict detection.
ADR-04 Type system & spec checking placement spv-ast owns name resolution + TypeChecker + spec well-formedness in one crate Fold typecheck into parser; defer all checks to VCGen; separate resolve/typecheck crates Resolving identifiers, inferring/checking Type (Int/Bool/Ref/Map/Seq), and validating specs (specs are Bool; old() only in ensures; modifies well-formed; arity) are one semantic pass over a resolved AST; emitting precise TypeError (Undeclared/Mismatch/NonBoolSpec/OldOutsideEnsures/BadModifies/ArityMismatch) before IVL keeps malformed input out of the VC. Parser-fused checks tangle syntax/semantics; deferring to VCGen lets ill-typed terms reach SMT (unsound/confusing). Separate resolve/typecheck crates win when the type system is large enough that one pass requires hundreds of modules with its own tests, as in a production compiler (e.g. rustc's hir_analysis crate) where the boundary reduces inter-team merge conflicts.
ADR-05 Intermediate representation Lower to a Boogie-like IVL of guarded commands (IvlCmd) before VC Generate VC directly from source AST; LLVM IR; CFG-of-basic-blocks IR A small IvlCmd (Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign/Seq/Choice) is the proven Boogie/Why3 waist: every source construct desugars into it (if→Choice, while→invariant cut, call→assert-pre/havoc-modifies/assume-post), so the wp calculus has five cases, not dozens of AST shapes. Direct source→VC duplicates desugaring logic inside wp (unsound divergence risk); LLVM IR lacks assume/havoc/nondeterministic Choice semantics; raw CFG complicates wp vs the algebraic Seq/Choice form. A CFG-of-basic-blocks IR wins when the front-end language has unstructured control flow (gotos, break/continue targeting arbitrary labels, exception unwind paths) where the structured Seq/Choice algebra cannot represent the CFG faithfully without encoding overhead, as in CBMC's bitcode-level VCGen.
ADR-06 VC direction Backward weakest-precondition (wp) over IvlCmd Strongest-postcondition (forward sp); symbolic execution; bounded path enumeration Five locked rules — wp(assume p,Q)=p⇒Q, wp(assert p,Q)=p∧Q, wp(havoc x,Q)=∀x.Q, wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x], wp(c1;c2,Q)=wp(c1,wp(c2,Q)), wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q)∧wp(c2,Q) — compose cleanly; method VC = wp(desugar(method), true) (post/pre wrapped). sp accumulates existentials + needs quantifier elimination on havoc; symbolic execution/path enumeration is exponential in branches and loses the loop-cut (this is deductive verification, not state-space exploration — that is #279/#281). Strongest-postcondition wins in bug-finding tools (like ESC/Java's forward-mode) where the tool has a richer forward-abstraction domain, multiple pre-states are explored in parallel, and the emphasis is finding concrete counterexamples quickly rather than proving all-path correctness.
ADR-07 VC-size control Passive form / dynamic single assignment (DSA) via passify before wp Naive wp directly over mutating assignments + Choice; CNF-then-solve; let-binding the duplicated formula Naive wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x] over Choice duplicates Q at every join → exponential VC (Flanagan & Saxe 2001). passify rewrites to DSA (each var assigned once, fresh versions; assignments become assume of equalities) so VC grows ~linearly in program size. CNF eagerly explodes; let-binding helps SMT internals but Rust-side term DAG + DSA is the documented Boogie path and keeps VcFormula inspectable for print-vc. Naive wp wins for extremely short methods (< 5 assignments, no Choice) where the DSA overhead (version counters, join management) adds bookkeeping cost with no blowup benefit; also wins when the tool is a purely SAT-based model checker that feeds into a CNF pipeline that benefits from structural sharing.
ADR-08 Loop handling Invariant cut (assert-on-entry; havoc targets; assume inv; inductive step + exit) Bounded loop unrolling (k iterations); fixpoint/abstract-interpretation invariant inference Cut proves I on entry, preserved by one arbitrary iteration (havoc loop targets, assume I∧guard, run body, assert I), and I∧¬guard ⇒ post — verifying all iterations without unrolling. Unrolling is unsound for unbounded loops (only checks ≤k) and is model-checking, not deduction. Inference is out of scope (#280-adjacent); user supplies invariants/decreases. Accepted pitfall: a weak invariant fails the inductive assert (counterexample to the invariant, not the program) — surfaced as LoopInvariantNotMaintained. Bounded unrolling wins for bug-finding tools (CBMC, KLEE) targeting firmware with finite loop bounds, where proving all iterations is impossible but finding a concrete exploit in ≤k iterations within a deadline matters more than soundness across all loop counts.
ADR-09 Termination / total correctness decreases clause → well-founded variant; checked separately from partial correctness Partial correctness only (ignore termination); termination via SMT alone; whole-program ranking synthesis Total correctness needs a decreases measure proven to strictly decrease on a well-founded order each loop iteration / recursive call; failure → MightNotTerminate. Keeping it a distinct check means partial-correctness VCs stay simple and a missing variant degrades gracefully (verify partial, flag termination). Ignoring termination is unsound for total-correctness claims; synthesis is research-grade and unbounded. Partial-correctness-only wins in safety-property-focused tools (e.g. checking buffer overflows or null dereferences) where loops always terminate for the platform (bounded hardware loops) and adding termination proofs would require user annotation effort that does not yield safety benefit.
ADR-10 Procedure calls Modular (contract-based): assert callee requires; havoc modifies; assume callee ensures Inlining callee bodies; whole-program (interprocedural) VC; summary inference A call is verified against the callee contract, never its body — this is what makes verification scale to many methods and stays sound under separate compilation/recursion. Inlining is exponential and breaks on recursion; whole-program VC defeats modularity; summary inference is unsound without the user-written Spec. Mis-framing the havoc(modifies) is a top soundness hazard → dedicated fuzz + ModifiesViolation checks. Inlining wins for small closed programs (< 5 methods, no recursion, e.g. firmware device-driver hot paths) where inline expansion produces a strictly stronger VC that proves more cases than the contract alone, and the total VC size remains tractable.
ADR-11 Two-state / old() semantics old(e) evaluated in pre-state snapshot; legal only inside ensures Allow old() anywhere; implicit pre-state copy everywhere; no old() support Frame reasoning (ensures x == old(x)+1) needs the pre-state value; TypeChecker enforces old() only in ensures (OldOutsideEnsures), and lowering captures pre-state vars before the body's havoc/assignments. Allowing old() in requires/invariants is meaningless or unsound; eager full-state copy bloats VCs; omitting old() makes most postconditions inexpressible. Allowing old() in loop invariants wins in two-state separation-logic-based tools (e.g. Viper with history invariants) where the invariant spans pre- and post-states of heap mutation and the type system enforces consistency rules at a coarser level than our OldOutsideEnsures check.
ADR-12 SMT integration boundary SmtSolver trait + textual SMT-LIB 2 emission; pluggable backend Hard-wire one solver via FFI; bundle a vendored solver; build our own decision procedures trait SmtSolver { fn check(&mut self, &[SmtTerm]) -> SmtResult } with SmtResult::{Unsat,Sat(Model),Unknown} decouples the verifier from any engine — reuse #278 or shell to Z3 behind the same trait; SMT-LIB 2 text is the portable lingua franca and feeds print-smt. Hard-wired FFI couples us to one ABI; vendoring bloats the build + licensing; writing decision procedures is #278 (explicit NON-GOAL — we are not re-implementing a competitive SMT solver). Hard-wired Z3 FFI wins when maximum performance is critical (a tight integration avoids process-spawn and text-serialization overhead — e.g. Facebook Infer's SMALLFOOT/Z3 coupling) and solver portability is not a requirement because the tool ships as a closed binary with a bundled solver version.
ADR-13 Sat/Unsat ⇒ verdict mapping Discharge check(¬VC): Unsat ⇒ Verified; Sat(model) ⇒ VError; Unknown explicit Ask validity of VC directly; treat Unknown as verified; treat Unknown as failure-with-no-CE Program correct ⟺ VC valid ⟺ ¬VC unsat; so "verify" = refute the negated VC. Unknown (timeout/quantifier incompleteness) must be a distinct outcome — never folded into Verified (would be unsound, the cardinal sin) nor silently into a spurious VError. Direct validity queries aren't what SMT solvers answer; conflating Unknown either way breaks soundness or completeness honesty. Treating Unknown as Failed (instead of a distinct third verdict) wins in a strict CI gate tool where the team decides that any method not provably verified must block the build, and the engineering contract guarantees that every method will verify within the timeout when written correctly — accepting the operational constraint that trigger/fuel tuning is required before merge.
ADR-14 Heap / memory model Map-based heap (objects + fields as Map, à la dynamic frames) Separation logic; per-object axioms; linear types; no heap (values only) Type::Map(K,V) + Type::Ref model the heap as a total map; modifies clauses give framing (dynamic-frames style) that the SMT Array/Map theory discharges efficiently. Separation logic needs a bespoke entailment/frame-inference engine — explicit NON-GOAL (no full separation logic / concurrency). No-heap kills field updates; linear types over-restrict aliasing for an annotated imperative language. Separation logic wins when the language has fine-grained heap ownership and concurrent access patterns (e.g. Rust itself, or a parallel heap-manipulating C program verified with RustBelt or VeriFast) where the alias analysis precision of BI-logic is required to avoid false alarms on pointer aliasing.
ADR-15 Pure functions & recursion Uninterpreted function symbols + defining axioms + bounded fuel for recursive unfolding Inline/unfold every definition eagerly; treat functions as fully opaque; SMT-native recursive functions only A pure Function becomes an uninterpreted symbol plus a defining Axiom; recursive functions unfold under bounded fuel so the solver terminates instead of looping on infinite instantiation. Eager unfolding is non-terminating/explosive; fully opaque functions lose needed facts (incomplete); relying solely on solver-native recursion is non-portable across the SmtSolver trait. Well-founded recursion required for axiom consistency (unsound otherwise). Eager unfolding wins for a small set of non-recursive helper functions (≤ 3 calls deep) in a closed program where the constant-unfolding cost is bounded and the extra fuel axiom overhead would itself produce more quantifier instantiation pressure than simply inlining the two-level definition.
ADR-16 Quantifier handling Explicit Trigger annotations on every Forall/Exists carried VcFormula → SmtTerm Let the solver pick triggers (auto-MBQI); ground/instantiate quantifiers ourselves; forbid quantifiers Triggers (matching patterns) control instantiation → predictable solver behavior, fewer timeouts, reproducible Unknown boundaries; Quant/Forall carry Vec<Trigger> end-to-end so SMT-LIB :pattern is emitted, not guessed. Solver-picked triggers are non-deterministic across backends (breaks differential testing); self-instantiation re-implements the solver; forbidding quantifiers kills forall-specs and array/map axioms. Auto-MBQI (Model-Based Quantifier Instantiation) wins in a purely quantifier-heavy first-order theorem proving context (e.g. a logic solver with no annotation discipline) where users cannot author triggers and the solver's own MBQI algorithm is empirically more stable on the theorem class than any hand-authored trigger set.
ADR-17 Verification-error taxonomy Fixed VErrorKind enum (8 variants) tagged at assert creation via Reason Free-text error strings; numeric error codes; a single generic "verification failed" VErrorKind = {AssertionMightNotHold, PostconditionMightNotHold, PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, DivisionByZero, ModifiesViolation, MightNotTerminate}; each IvlCmd::Assert carries a Reason { kind, span } so a Sat model maps to the exact failing obligation + source point. Free text/codes can't be matched in tests; a single generic kind makes counterexamples unactionable. A single generic VerificationFailed wins in a minimal proof-of-concept verifier where the taxonomy is not yet stable and the team is still discovering which obligation types arise, deferring the classification investment until the VCGen architecture is frozen.
ADR-18 Error & failure type design Result<_, E> everywhere; typed ParseError/TypeError/VError; library code never panic! panic!/unwrap on bad input; Box<dyn Error> erasure; exit codes only A verifier ingesting untrusted annotated source must surface every fault as data — typed enums (ParseErrorKind, TypeError, VErrorKind) enable exhaustive handling + precise CLI output; #![deny(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::panic)] in lib crates (panic only on proven-internal invariant violations, never on input). panic on input is a DoS/UX failure; dyn Error loses match-ability; exit-codes-only loses spans/counterexamples. panic!/unwrap with a trusted-input API wins inside a tightly-controlled internal verification kernel (e.g. a proof-kernel in a theorem prover with a de Bruijn-style trusted kernel) where all inputs are produced by verified code, never raw user input, and the clarity of panic-on-violation is a correctness signal.
ADR-19 Differential oracle Differential testing vs a Boogie/Dafny subset where feasible (Phase 13) Hand-written golden VCs only; trust our own output; compare against an SMT solver alone For programs in the shared subset, our Verified/Failed verdict must agree with Boogie/Dafny — an independent oracle catches soundness drift the unit tests can't. Golden VCs only test our self-consistency; trusting our output has no external check; an SMT-only oracle validates the solver, not our VCGen (the part that must never let a bug through). Hand-written golden VCs win when no reference implementation exists in the same language subset (novel source language without a Boogie/Dafny front end) and the team is the primary authority on semantics — golden tests then become the authoritative record of intended behavior.
ADR-20 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets for Parser, Desugarer, wp, SMT emission No fuzzing; property tests only; AFL Each pipeline stage gets a fuzz target: random bytes→Parser (no panic, clean ParseError); random AST→desugar_method (well-formed IvlCmd); random IvlCmd→wp/passify (no panic, VC well-formed); random VcFormula→SMT (parseable SMT-LIB, round-trips). Soundness oracle: a fuzzed program must never flip a known-buggy case to Verified. cargo-fuzz is the Rust-native libFuzzer integration; AFL adds toolchain weight; property tests (used additionally) don't explore raw-byte parser paths. AFL wins on targets without sanitizers support (non-Linux, embedded cross-compilation) or on legacy C codebases where libFuzzer's instrumentation is incompatible, and AFL's fork-based model is more stable across diverse toolchains.
ADR-21 Benchmarking criterion for VC size + solver-time microbenchmarks against targets cargo bench (libtest, nightly); ad-hoc Instant timing; no perf gating criterion gives statistically sound, regression-tracked measurements on stable Rust: VC node count vs program size (must stay ~linear after DSA — guards ADR-07), wp construction time, SMT emission throughput, per-method end-to-end latency vs Phase-13 targets. Built-in cargo bench is nightly-only; Instant timing is noisy/non-gating; no perf gate lets the exponential-VC regression slip back in silently. Ad-hoc Instant timing with a simple assertion wins in a pure research artifact where rigorous statistical comparison across runs is unnecessary, CI is not gating on performance, and the engineering overhead of setting up criterion baselines and handling noise is not justified.
ADR-22 CI GitHub Actions: build + clippy(-D warnings) + fmt-check + test + fuzz-smoke + bench-smoke on MSRV & stable; Docker reproducible build No CI; manual local runs; CI on a single toolchain only Every push gates on the full matrix (MSRV 1.78 + stable) so a sound, working pipeline is the only mergeable state; fuzz-smoke (short-budget) + bench-smoke catch crashes/perf cliffs early; Dockerfile pins toolchain + solver for reproducible verification runs. Single-toolchain CI misses MSRV breakage; manual runs don't gate the soundness benchmark suite (the project "done" gate). Single-toolchain CI wins in early-stage exploration where the MSRV is not yet published and the overhead of maintaining a matrix (two toolchains, Docker caching, fuzz-smoke) slows iteration more than the protection it provides — acceptable until the API surface is stable enough to declare a MSRV.
ADR-23 MSRV policy Minimum supported Rust = 1.78, verified in CI Latest-stable-only; nightly features; no declared MSRV 1.78 supplies the stable feature surface needed (let-else, GATs as used) with broad availability; declaring + CI-checking it lets the seven crates be embedded as a verification library by downstream tools. Latest-only churns consumers; nightly forbids reproducibility + Docker pinning; undeclared MSRV breaks silently for embedders. Latest-stable-only wins for an in-house tool with a single deployment target (one internal CI cluster) where the team controls the toolchain and would benefit from using the latest ergonomics/performance improvements without the overhead of MSRV testing infrastructure.
ADR-24 Dependency policy Minimal, audited deps; no heavy framework; SMT engine behind the SmtSolver trait, not a hard dep Pull large crates freely; vendor a full solver; depend on an async runtime Trusted-base discipline: a verifier's soundness rests on its own code, so dependencies are few, popular, audited (cargo-deny in CI for licenses + advisories); the solver is an external process/optional backend behind SmtSolver, never a compiled-in dependency (keeps #278 swappable). Free dep growth widens the trust base + attack surface; vendoring a solver bloats build/licensing; no async runtime needed for a synchronous batch pipeline. Vendoring a solver wins when the tool ships in an air-gapped environment where internet access to package registries is unavailable at build time, reproducibility requires pinning a specific patched solver version, or the solver binary must be distributed as part of a regulated software package with SBOM requirements.
ADR-25 Licensing Dual MIT OR Apache-2.0 GPL/copyleft; proprietary; MIT-only; Apache-only Rust-ecosystem standard dual license maximizes downstream adoption (permissive + patent grant from Apache-2.0); every crate's Cargo.toml declares it and cargo-deny enforces dependency-license compatibility. Copyleft restricts integration into proprietary verifiers; MIT-only lacks an explicit patent grant; Apache-only is less universally accepted than the dual form. GPL wins when the tool is a research artifact whose authors want to ensure all derivative academic and commercial tools publish their source modifications, maximizing scientific reproducibility and preventing proprietary forks that hide improvements from the community.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace; seven spv-* crates; immutable-after-construction IR shared by Rc. Directory-to-phase alignment in §10.2. Total tracked ≈ 220 files at Phase 13 done; root governance frozen at Phase 0.

10.3 Makefile targets (canonical)

Target Action
make build cargo build --workspace (MSRV 1.78)
make test cargo test --workspace (unit + integration)
make fmt cargo fmt --check
make clippy cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings
make conformance run conformance/runner.rs over expected-pass/fail; fails on any false "verified"
make fuzz cargo fuzz run {parse,desugar,wp,smt} (time-boxed)
make bench criterion benches/* vs docs/performance/targets.md
make smt spv print-smt examples/*.spv -> .smt2 for inspection
make doc cargo doc --no-deps --workspace
make deny cargo deny check (license + advisory)
make ci fmt + clippy + build + test + conformance + deny (the merge gate)

10.4 Layout invariants

  • One workspace, one Cargo.lock committed → reproducible builds; binary crate present.
  • Crate dependency edges strictly downward: syntax → ast → ivl → vc → smt → verify → cli; no cycles; cargo enforces.
  • spv-syntax, spv-ast, spv-ivl, spv-vc, spv-smt are no_std + alloc; only spv-verify/spv-cli use std (process/IO for the solver). → core IR portable, testable without OS.
  • Every VErrorKind variant has ≥1 conformance/expected-fail/ case pinning kind + span; CI rejects a new variant without its case (templates/new-verror-kind.md).
  • conformance/expected-pass ⊥ expected-fail are disjoint and exhaustive over the feature matrix; unknown/ is the only escape hatch and never counts as PASS.
  • No full implementation source in docs//notes//blueprint (lprompt RULE 10) — signatures, EBNF, tables only.
  • SMT backend behind solver.rs trait + feature="z3"; default build has zero solver binary dependency at compile time (process-invoked at runtime) → keeps the IR crates pure.

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative type catalogue. Every phase (## 12..## 25) builds against these exact signatures. Grouped by crate, ascending in the pipeline spv-syntax -> spv-ast -> spv-ivl -> spv-vc -> spv-smt -> spv-verify (+ spv-cli driver). Rust-ish signatures + doc comments only; bodies <= 2 lines (lprompt CRITICAL RULE 10).

11.1 Cross-cutting conventions

Concept Decision Rationale
Interning Atom = interned identifier (u32 index into a StringInterner); Copy, Eq by index O(1) name compare; AST/IVL/VcFormula stay Copy-cheap for keys
Spans Span { lo: u32, hi: u32 } byte offsets into one source buffer every error + Reason carries one; counterexample maps back to it
Values Value = Int(i128) | Bool(bool) | Null (Ref) — literals + model values shared by Expr::Lit and Counterexample.bindings
Variables Var { name: Atom, version: u32 }; version=0 = surface var, >0 = DSA-renamed DSA (passive form) bumps version; surface always recoverable
Mutability AST / IvlCmd / VcFormula / SmtTerm are immutable after construction (built bottom-up, then read-only); only the SmtSolver session is mutable enables &-sharing + parallel per-method verification with zero locks on the IR
Boxing recursive variants Box<_>; sequences Vec<_> tagged-union AST, no null, no cycles -> trees not graphs
Concurrency per-method verification is independent and parallelizable (each owns its IvlCmd/VcFormula); an SmtSolver session is single-threaded (&mut self) spawn N method-workers; one solver session per worker, never shared
Errors ParseError/TypeError are front-end failures (reject input); VError is a verification outcome (well-formed program, spec may not hold) keep "ill-formed" vs "might-not-hold" strictly separate
no_std spv-syntax, spv-ast, spv-ivl, spv-vc, spv-smt are no_std + alloc; spv-verify mostly; spv-cli is std core pipeline embeddable; I/O isolated to the binary

Shared leaf enums (defined once in spv-syntax, re-exported downstream):

/// Interned identifier; Copy, equality by interner index. Never compared by string.
pub struct Atom(pub u32);
/// Byte range [lo,hi) into the single source buffer; on every diagnostic + Reason.
pub struct Span { pub lo: u32, pub hi: u32 }
/// Literal / model value. The only inhabited scalar domains: Int, Bool, Ref(Null).
pub enum Value { Int(i128), Bool(bool), Null }
pub enum UnOp  { Neg, Not }
pub enum BinOp { Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Mod, Eq, Ne, Lt, Le, Gt, Ge, And, Or, Imp, Iff }
pub enum Quantifier { Forall, Exists }
/// A multi-trigger: a set of term patterns guiding SMT quantifier instantiation.
pub struct Trigger { pub terms: Vec<Expr> }

Div/Mod are flagged downstream: every Div/Mod site emits a DivisionByZero proof obligation (## 16) — divisor != 0.

11.2 spv-syntax — surface AST + parse errors

Output of Lexer/Parser; immutable; owned by the caller, lent (&Program) to spv-ast. No type info yet (Type nodes are as-written, unresolved). Pretty-printable via print-ast.

Top-level declarations and procedure/specification shapes:

/// A whole compilation unit: an ordered list of top-level declarations.
pub struct Program { pub decls: Vec<Decl> }
/// Axiom(Expr) = a global assumed fact (asserted to SMT verbatim; unsound if false).
pub enum Decl { Method(Method), Function(Function), Axiom(Expr) }

/// A verifiable procedure. body=None => trusted (contract only, no proof obligation).
pub struct Method {
    pub name: Atom,
    pub params: Vec<Param>,    // inputs (immutable in body)
    pub returns: Vec<Param>,   // out-parameters (assignable; appear in ensures)
    pub spec: Spec,            // requires/ensures/modifies/decreases
    pub body: Option<Block>,   // None = abstract/trusted method
}
/// A pure (side-effect-free) function; lowers to uninterpreted SMT fn + axiom (## 21).
pub struct Function { pub name: Atom, pub params: Vec<Param>, pub ret: Type, pub body: Option<Expr> }
pub struct Param { pub name: Atom, pub ty: Type }
pub struct Block { pub stmts: Vec<Stmt> }
/// LValue: assignment target. Var for locals/returns; Field for heap object fields.
pub enum LValue { Var(Atom), Field(Box<Expr>, Atom) }
/// All four clause-vectors are conjunctive (empty = trivially true).
pub struct Spec {
    pub requires:  Vec<Expr>,   // assumed on entry
    pub ensures:   Vec<Expr>,   // asserted on exit (may use old())
    pub modifies:  Vec<Expr>,   // frame: heap locations the method may change
    pub decreases: Vec<Expr>,   // termination variant (lexicographic tuple)
}

Statement, expression, type, and parse-error shapes:

/// Imperative statement. While carries invariants+decreases (the loop cut, ## 18).
pub enum Stmt {
    VarDecl(Atom, Type, Option<Expr>),   // local introduction (+ optional init)
    Assign(LValue, Expr),                // l := e
    If(Expr, Block, Block),              // then / else (else may be empty)
    While { guard: Expr, invariants: Vec<Expr>, decreases: Vec<Expr>, body: Block },
    Call(Vec<LValue>, Atom, Vec<Expr>),  // outs := callee(args)
    Assert(Expr),                        // user proof obligation
    Assume(Expr),                        // user-supplied assumption (UNSOUND if wrong; warn)
    Return,                              // exit point; triggers ensures check
}
/// Side-effect-free expression. Old(e) = value of e in the pre-state (two-state).
/// Quant binds (Atom,Type) vars with Trigger patterns over a Box<Expr> body.
pub enum Expr {
    Lit(Value),
    Var(Atom),
    Un(UnOp, Box<Expr>),
    Bin(BinOp, Box<Expr>, Box<Expr>),
    Call(Atom, Vec<Expr>),               // pure-Function application only
    Old(Box<Expr>),                      // legal only inside ensures (## 14)
    Quant(Quantifier, Vec<(Atom, Type)>, Vec<Trigger>, Box<Expr>),
    Field(Box<Expr>, Atom),              // heap field access -> Map select (## 24)
}
/// Source-language type, as-written. Map/Seq structural; Named resolved in spv-ast.
pub enum Type { Int, Bool, Ref, Map(Box<Type>, Box<Type>), Seq(Box<Type>), Named(Atom) }
/// Parse failure: rejects ill-formed input before any semantic phase.
pub struct ParseError { pub kind: ParseErrorKind, pub span: Span }
pub enum ParseErrorKind { UnexpectedToken, BadSpec, BadType }

Token/Lexer/Parser are internal to spv-syntax (constructive, not normative IR); see ## 13. Parser is recursive-descent, single-pass, returns Result<Program, ParseError>.

11.3 spv-ast — resolved/typed AST + type errors

spv-ast resolves Atoms to declarations and annotates/checks Types in place over the same AST nodes (no second tree): it produces a Resolved view + a TypeError list. Type is owned here (re-exported). Spec well-formedness lives here.

/// Resolved + type-checked program. Same node shapes; identifiers now resolve and
/// every Expr has a checked Type. Immutable; lent to spv-ivl.
pub struct Typed<'p> { pub program: &'p Program, /* resolution + type tables */ }

/// Semantic (well-formedness) failure on a *parseable* program.
pub enum TypeError {
    Undeclared(Atom),                          // use of an unbound name
    Mismatch { expected: Type, got: Type },    // type clash
    NonBoolSpec,                               // a requires/ensures/invariant isn't Bool
    OldOutsideEnsures,                         // old() used outside an ensures clause
    BadModifies,                               // modifies entry isn't a heap location
    ArityMismatch,                             // call/quantifier wrong number of args
}

TypeChecker invariants enforced (## 14): specs : Bool; Old(_) only under ensures; modifies entries denote Field/heap locations; Call arity + arg types match; decreases entries : Int or well-ordered. On any error: collect all TypeErrors (do not stop at first), reject the program — no verification on ill-typed input.

11.4 spv-ivl — guarded-command IVL + Reason

The Boogie-like IVL: six commands. Immutable after desugar_method. Every Assert carries a Reason so a later SMT counterexample maps to a VErrorKind + Span. Pretty-printable via print-ivl.

/// Guarded command. The complete IVL alphabet (no other control constructs).
pub enum IvlCmd {
    Assume(Expr),                    // restrict to states satisfying Expr
    Assert(Expr, Reason),            // proof obligation; Reason = why it exists
    Havoc(Vec<Var>),                 // forget the listed vars (nondeterministic reassignment)
    Assign(Var, Expr),               // deterministic update (becomes assume(=) in DSA, ## 20)
    Seq(Vec<IvlCmd>),                // sequencing c1;..;cn
    Choice(Box<IvlCmd>, Box<IvlCmd>),// nondeterministic choice c1 [] c2 (if-desugaring)
}

/// Provenance of an Assert: drives error classification + source mapping.
pub struct Reason { pub kind: VErrorKind, pub span: Span }

/// Lower one method to a single IvlCmd:
///   Seq[ assume(requires); <desugared body>; assert(ensures, Reason{Postcondition..}) ].
/// if  -> Choice(assume g; then , assume !g; else)
/// while-> invariant cut (## 18); call -> assert pre / havoc modifies / assume post (## 17).
/// Pure, deterministic; &Method in, owned IvlCmd out. body=None => no obligation emitted.
pub fn desugar_method(m: &Method) -> IvlCmd;

VErrorKind/Span referenced here are the canonical ones defined in spv-verify (§11.7), re-exported into spv-ivl so Reason can be constructed at desugar time. Desugaring is the only writer of Reason; downstream stages read it.

11.5 spv-vc — VcFormula + wp + passify

First-order logic over Pred atoms. Immutable. Two transforms (passify then wp) plus simplification; both are pure functions IvlCmd -> _. Pretty-printable via print-vc.

/// First-order verification formula. Atom(Pred) wraps an atomic predicate over terms.
/// Forall carries Triggers (SMT instantiation hints); Exists does not (model-search).
pub enum VcFormula {
    True,
    False,
    And(Vec<VcFormula>),
    Or(Vec<VcFormula>),
    Not(Box<VcFormula>),
    Imp(Box<VcFormula>, Box<VcFormula>),
    Forall(Vec<(Var, Type)>, Vec<Trigger>, Box<VcFormula>),
    Exists(Vec<(Var, Type)>, Box<VcFormula>),
    Atom(Pred),
}

/// Atomic predicate: a relation over first-order terms (e.g. Lt(t1,t2), App(f,args), Eq).
/// Terms reuse Var/Value; Pred is the leaf of VcFormula. (Variant set fixed in ## 15.)
pub enum Pred { /* Cmp(BinOp,Term,Term) | App(Atom,Vec<Term>) | BoolVar(Var) | ... */ }

/// Weakest precondition. The five LOCKED rules:
///   wp(assume p,Q)=p=>Q ; wp(assert p,Q)=p AND Q ; wp(havoc x,Q)=forall x.Q ;
///   wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x] ; wp(c1;c2,Q)=wp(c1,wp(c2,Q)) ; wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q) AND wp(c2,Q).
/// Method VC = wp(desugar_method(m), True). VALID iff program meets spec.
pub fn wp(c: &IvlCmd, post: VcFormula) -> VcFormula;

/// Passive (dynamic-single-assignment) form: each Var assigned once (fresh `version`),
/// Assign -> Assume(=), Choice merged via versioned joins. Prevents exponential wp blowup
/// (Flanagan-Saxe). MUST run BEFORE wp. Pure; &IvlCmd in, owned IvlCmd out.
pub fn passify(c: &IvlCmd) -> IvlCmd;

Pipeline order is normative: passify -> wp -> simplify (constant-fold, flatten Imp/And/Or) -> hand VcFormula to spv-smt. After passify, no Assign remains (only Assume/Assert/Havoc/Seq/Choice) so wp's substitution rule never duplicates Q.

11.6 spv-smt — sorts, terms, solver trait, result/model

Encodes VcFormula -> SmtTerm over a fixed sort lattice; emits SMT-LIB 2 (serialization format); defines the solver boundary. SmtTerm is immutable; the SmtSolver session is the one mutable, single-threaded object in the pipeline.

/// SMT sort. Array models heaps (Ref->object) and Maps/Seqs (## 24). Uninterp = abstract type.
pub enum SmtSort { Int, Bool, Uninterp(Atom), Array(Box<SmtSort>, Box<SmtSort>) }

/// Sorted first-order SMT term: constants, sorted vars, function apps, arithmetic/bool ops,
/// select/store, quantifiers + triggers. The encoding target of VcFormula. Immutable.
pub enum SmtTerm { /* Const(Value) | Var(Var,SmtSort) | App(Atom,Vec<SmtTerm>) | Forall{..} | ... */ }

/// The solver boundary (Phase 9/10). Implementations: an in-process #278 binding OR a
/// Z3 process driver behind SMT-LIB 2. &mut self => the session is STATEFUL + single-threaded:
/// push/pop, incremental assertions, one session per parallel method-worker (never shared).
pub trait SmtSolver {
    /// Assert the conjunction of `assertions` (the negated VC) and check satisfiability.
    fn check(&mut self, assertions: &[SmtTerm]) -> SmtResult;
}

/// Solver verdict. Unsat => VC valid => Verified. Sat(Model) => counterexample exists.
/// Unknown => timeout / incompleteness (quantifiers) — NEVER treated as Verified (## 22).
pub enum SmtResult { Unsat, Sat(Model), Unknown }

/// A satisfying assignment: solver-name -> Value, parsed from get-model output.
/// DSA/skolem names here; spv-verify maps them back to source vars (## 23).
pub struct Model { /* assignments: Vec<(Atom, Value)> */ }

Trigger annotation: every VcFormula::Forall carries Vec<Trigger>; encoded as SMT-LIB :pattern. Missing/over-broad triggers => Unknown or matching loops — a completeness pitfall, never a soundness one. Sort-correctness is a soundness obligation: a mis-encoded operator (e.g. Div semantics, Map select/store axioms) can make a buggy program "verify".

11.7 spv-verify — verification outcome + counterexample

The driver maps solver verdicts to source-level results. VerifyResult is the public API of the tool; VError reconstructs a Counterexample from a Model using the Reason recorded at desugar time.

/// Top-level outcome of verifying one method (or program: Vec joined). The ONLY answer
/// surfaced to the user. Verified MUST be unreachable for a buggy program (soundness).
pub enum VerifyResult { Verified, Failed(Vec<VError>) }

/// One verification failure: a proof obligation the solver could not discharge.
pub struct VError {
    pub kind: VErrorKind,             // taken from the failing Assert's Reason
    pub span: Span,                   // source location of that obligation
    pub counterexample: Counterexample,
}

/// Why an obligation failed. Each maps 1:1 to a category of Assert emitted by desugaring.
pub enum VErrorKind {
    AssertionMightNotHold,            // user assert
    PostconditionMightNotHold,        // ensures at return
    PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold,   // callee requires at a Call site
    LoopInvariantOnEntry,             // invariant fails before first iteration
    LoopInvariantNotMaintained,       // invariant not preserved by one arbitrary iteration
    DivisionByZero,                   // a Div/Mod divisor obligation
    ModifiesViolation,                // write outside the modifies frame
