  • Forensic artifacts: the version map, pre/post IvlCmd, equivalence-check counterexample (the Q and model distinguishing them).
  • Long-term mitigation: property "passify preserves validity" as a permanent fuzz target; assert single-assignment invariant (each DSA Var assigned exactly once) post-transform.

F-3 — Spurious failure: correct program reported Failed (S1, dominant usability failure)

  • Symptom: VError on a program the author believes correct; the Counterexample describes a state the user thinks impossible. By far the most frequent real-world complaint.
  • Root cause (ranked): (1) too-weak / missing loop invariant — the inductive Assert(inv) after the body fails because inv doesn't reconstrain a havoced target; the counterexample refutes the invariant, not the code (classic pitfall — "false counterexample to the user's invariant"); (2) missing/weak trigger on a Forall — the solver never instantiates the needed axiom/quantifier, so a true fact is unprovable; (3) loop-target set too broad (havocing a var the loop doesn't change but inv doesn't re-pin); (4) callee contract too weak (ensures omits a fact the caller needs) — modular-verification limitation, not a tool bug; (5) integer/Map axioms insufficient for the goal.
  • Detection: distinguish from F-1/F-4 via VErrorKind: LoopInvariantNotMaintained ⇒ almost always (1); PostconditionMightNotHold/AssertionMightNotHold with a model assigning "wild" quantified values ⇒ (2). The expected-PASS suite catches regressions where we newly fail a known-good program.
  • Automatic recovery: none changes the answer (would risk soundness), but spv aids the user: emit the Reason tag (VErrorKind + Span) on the failing Assert, the reconstructed Counterexample.bindings, and at: Span; optionally --suggest heuristics (e.g. "invariant does not mention havoced target i").
  • Manual recovery (the debugging methodology — emphasize):
    1. Read VErrorKind + Counterexample — which assertion, which program point, which variable values.
    2. spv print-ivl <method> — confirm the failing Assert and its Reason are the loop-inv / post you expect, and the Havoc set is right.
    3. spv print-vc <method> — read the exact VcFormula clause that's invalid; the counterexample model satisfies its negation.
    4. spv print-smt <method> — confirm encoding + triggers; check whether the needed Forall could even instantiate.
    5. If (1): strengthen the invariants clause so it survives havoc targets; assume inv; body; assert inv. If (2): add/loosen a Trigger. If (4): strengthen callee ensures.
  • Operator actions: none (user-side fix); but a spike in F-3 reports after a release ⇒ suspect a trigger-strategy or desugaring regression — diff print-vc against the prior version on the regression corpus.
  • Forensic artifacts: Counterexample{bindings, at}, the failing Reason{kind, span}, the print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt triple, solver model.
  • Long-term mitigation: ship a "verification debugging" guide keyed off each VErrorKind; invest in trigger-inference quality; counterexample readability (map DSA/skolem names → source vars — see F-6); a curated expected-PASS corpus to catch completeness regressions early. Completeness is bounded by SMT and by the user's annotations — F-3 is partly inherent to deductive verification, not eliminable.

F-4 — Solver Unknown / timeout (S1)

  • Symptom: SmtResult::Unknown from SmtSolver::check; cannot conclude Verified or Failed.
  • Root cause: quantifier-heavy VC (incompleteness of E-matching), large VcFormula (passify failed → F-2 exponential), non-linear arithmetic, or genuine resource cap (timeout/memory) hit.
  • Detection: explicit — Unknown is a first-class SmtResult variant; never silently coerced to Verified (that would be F-1) nor to Failed. spv-verify reports it as Unknown (distinct from VerifyResult::Failed).
  • Automatic recovery: staged solving — (a) retry with longer per-method timeout (push/pop incremental, Phase 10); (b) re-run after VC simplification (constant fold, implication flatten, Phase 8); (c) try alternate trigger strategy / solver backend behind the SmtSolver trait (#278 or Z3); (d) if all fail, surface Unknown honestly.
  • Manual recovery: split the method (smaller VCs), add asserts as proof hints (cut points), tighten triggers, replace non-linear goals with lemmas.
  • Operator actions: tune the global timeout budget; if Unknown rate climbs, check solver version/seed drift; consider parallel per-method dispatch.
  • Forensic artifacts: VC size (term count, quantifier count), solver time/memory, timeout value, print-smt output, solver statistics (instantiations).
  • Long-term mitigation: VC-size budget (target ≤ ~10⁴ SmtTerm nodes/method; alert past threshold) so passify keeps the VC near-linear; trigger discipline; benchmark solver-time vs targets in CI (regression gate).

F-5 — Matching loop / quantifier non-termination (S1)

  • Symptom: check never returns; wall-clock hang; unbounded solver memory.
  • Root cause: a Trigger that lets the solver instantiate a Forall to produce a new term matching the same trigger ad infinitum (matching loop) — e.g. trigger {f(x)} on ∀x. f(x) = f(g(x)).
  • Detection: hard wall-clock + memory cap on every check (no unbounded solve); the cap converts the hang into F-4 Unknown. Trigger-shape linter flags self-feeding triggers at encode time.
  • Automatic recovery: enforced solver timeout ⇒ Unknown; on detection, retry with a more restrictive (multi-pattern) trigger or quantifier instantiation cap.
  • Manual recovery: rewrite the offending Trigger/axiom to break the cycle; add a fuel parameter to recursive pure-function axioms (Phase 12) to bound unfolding.
  • Operator actions: ensure the kill-timeout is always set in deployment config; a hang reaching it should log the offending method + axioms.
  • Forensic artifacts: the emitted Forall + its Trigger, solver instantiation log, the axiom set, time-to-cap.
  • Long-term mitigation: trigger linter in spv-smt rejecting obviously-looping patterns; fuel-bounded recursion as default for user pure functions; fuzz SMT emission (Phase 13) with adversarial axioms.

F-6 — Counterexample does not re-execute (model-decode bug) (S1)

  • Symptom: the reported Counterexample.bindings describe a state that, when run, does not actually trigger the VError; user loses trust though the underlying Sat verdict may be correct.
  • Root cause: Phase 11 reconstruction error — wrong mapping of DSA/skolem Var names back to source Atom, picking the wrong DSA version at the failing point, or mis-decoding an SMT Model value (Array/Map element, uninterpreted-sort constant) into a source Value.
  • Detection: counterexample-replays-the-bug check — feed Counterexample.bindings back as assumes at the method entry and re-verify; the same Assert must fail. Mismatch ⇒ decode bug (a presentation defect, distinct from F-1: the Sat verdict can be right while the story is wrong).
  • Automatic recovery: if replay fails, downgrade the rendered counterexample to "raw model" (still report Failed — the Sat verdict stands) and flag the decode mismatch; do not flip to Verified.
  • Manual recovery: inspect the DSA version table + skolem map; fix the Atom↔DSA name mapping or Model value decoder; add the case to the reconstruction test corpus.
  • Operator actions: none (S1 cosmetic-ish), but track replay-failure rate as a quality metric.
  • Forensic artifacts: raw SMT Model, the DSA/skolem→Atom map, the Span of the failing Assert, replay result.
  • Long-term mitigation: property test "every produced Counterexample replays to the same VErrorKind+Span"; keep the DSA naming scheme injective and recorded alongside the VC.

F-7 — Spec well-formedness errors (S2)

  • Symptom: TypeError::{NonBoolSpec, OldOutsideEnsures, BadModifies, ArityMismatch} from spv-ast; verification not attempted.
  • Root cause: non-Bool requires/ensures/invariants; old() used outside an ensures (two-state expr illegal in requires/body); modifies referencing a non-lvalue or undeclared frame; callee-call arity mismatch.
  • Detection: TypeChecker + spec well-formedness pass (Phase 2) — deterministic, pre-VC.
  • Automatic recovery: none (semantic error); fail fast with precise TypeError + Span.
  • Manual recovery: user fixes the annotation per the diagnostic.
  • Operator actions: none.
  • Forensic artifacts: TypeError variant + Span; print-ast to show the offending Spec.
  • Long-term mitigation: clear messages naming the clause (requires/ensures/modifies/decreases) and the rule violated; golden-diagnostic tests so messages don't regress.

F-8 — Parse / type errors (S2)

  • Symptom: ParseError{ParseErrorKind::{UnexpectedToken,BadSpec,BadType}} or TypeError::{Undeclared,Mismatch,ArityMismatch}; no IVL produced.
  • Root cause: malformed source (bad token, ill-formed spec clause, bad Type syntax); undeclared identifier; type mismatch (Int vs Bool, wrong Map/Seq element type).
  • Detection: Lexer/Parser (Phase 1), name resolution + TypeChecker (Phase 2); fully deterministic.
  • Automatic recovery: none; report with line/column Span and a caret; recover-and-continue in the parser to batch multiple diagnostics where safe.
  • Manual recovery: user edits source.
  • Operator actions: none.
  • Forensic artifacts: ParseError/TypeError + Span; the offending token/Token stream.
  • Long-term mitigation: robust error-recovery in recursive descent; golden-diagnostic corpus; fuzz the parser (Phase 13) to ensure malformed input never panics (must always yield a ParseError, never abort).

F-9 — Driver / IO / OOM / panic (S1)

  • Symptom: spv aborts (nonzero exit), Rust panic, or OOM on a pathological VC/SMT term graph.
  • Root cause: unbounded recursion in wp/desugar on deeply nested Seq/Choice; file IO failure; SMT-LIB emission to a closed pipe; allocator exhaustion on a giant VcFormula.
  • Detection: panic hook + nonzero exit; CI fuzzers (parser, desugar, wp, SMT emission) treat any panic as a bug.
  • Automatic recovery: none mid-process; the CLI returns a distinct exit code per stage (parse/type/ivl/vc/smt/verify) so callers can route; per-method work is isolated so one method's OOM doesn't corrupt others' results in a multi-method run.
  • Manual recovery: reduce input; raise memory; bisect with print-ast/print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt to find the stage that blows up.
  • Operator actions: set memory/time ulimits; capture core/backtrace; file with the minimized input.
  • Forensic artifacts: panic backtrace, the stage that failed, input size metrics, partial print-* output.
  • Long-term mitigation: convert deep recursion in wp/desugar/passify to explicit work-stacks to avoid stack overflow on adversarial nesting; bound VC size (shared with F-4); fuzz every stage (Phase 13) with "never panic" as the invariant.

F-10 — Vacuous proof (S0, soundness of claim)

A method with requires false or mutually-inconsistent preconditions (e.g., requires x > 0; requires x < 0;) trivially satisfies any postcondition: the VC becomes false ⇒ ensures, which is a tautology. spv verify correctly reports Verified from a logical standpoint, but the verification is meaningless — the method can never be called.

  • Symptom: Verified reported; vacuity probe (spv verify --probe-vacuity) emits VacuousSpec warning.
  • Root cause: user writes contradictory or unsatisfiable preconditions; IVL desugaring produces assume(requires); ... where the assume is assume false — the body is dead code, all asserts trivially hold.
  • Detection: add a --probe-vacuity mode: after Verified, additionally check SAT(requires) — if Unsat, emit VacuousSpec{method, span}. Phase 13 conformance suite: one vacuous/ subdirectory of methods that MUST trigger the vacuity warning.
  • Automatic recovery: spv emits warning VacuousSpec even though VResult::Verified; build integrators treat this as S1 (block on warning policy).
  • Manual recovery: inspect requires clauses; print-ivl will show assume false or a contradiction; simplify preconditions until SAT(requires) holds.
  • Forensic artifacts: VacuousSpec log entry; print-smt of requires-only formula showing Unsat.
  • Long-term mitigation: vacuity probe integrated into Phase 13 conformance runner; warning count tracked in CI dashboard; --strict-vacuity flag turns warning into error.

Cross-cutting recovery & escalation

Signal Likely class First diagnostic Escalation
expected-FAIL prog returns Verified F-1 (or F-2) --audit assert count; print-ivl/print-vc S0 page; freeze releases
print-vc differs pre/post passify F-2 equivalence harness; disable passify S0
LoopInvariantNotMaintained on good code F-3 (1) print-ivl (havoc set) → print-vc user strengthens invariants
*MightNotHold with wild quantified model F-3 (2) print-smt triggers add/loosen Trigger
Unknown returned F-4 VC size + solver stats retry/split; tune timeout
check never returns (hits cap) F-5 trigger linter; instantiation log rewrite trigger; add fuel
counterexample won't replay F-6 replay check; DSA/skolem map downgrade to raw model
  • Vacuity guard (anti-F-1): an assume false reachable on every path makes any postcondition verify trivially. Periodically discharge wp(desugar(method), false) per method; if Unsat fails to hold only because of a genuine path that's fine, but a method whose reachability VC is itself unsat (every path assumes false) ⇒ flag WARN vacuous-method — a correct-by-vacuity verdict is worthless and often hides F-1.
  • Determinism for forensics: fix solver seed + version; identical input ⇒ identical VcFormula, SMT-LIB, and verdict, so any field failure replays exactly. Non-determinism in a verdict is itself an S0 alarm.
  • Invariant restated: the only acceptable wrong answer is F-3/F-4 (refuse to verify a correct program / Unknown); F-1 (verify a wrong program) is never acceptable and gates every release.

Operational Readiness

Framing: spv is a batch verifier tool/library, not a long-running service — no request loop, no uptime SLA, no live traffic. "Operational" = running spv verify over a large annotated codebase in CI / dev loop and keeping that loop fast, debuggable, and sound. Observability targets the verification run, not a process. The cardinal SLO is correctness (soundness), not availability.

27.1 Observability model — what a verification run emits

Per-run telemetry is structured, machine-readable (JSON lines on stderr / --report report.json), and keyed by method. Library surface is VerifyResult (Verified / Failed(Vec<VError>)); the CLI wraps it with progress + metrics. No global mutable state, no metrics daemon.

Metric Type Unit Source crate Why it matters
methods_total / methods_verified / methods_failed / methods_unknown counter count spv-verify top-line verdict split
vc_size histogram VcFormula nodes (post-passify+simplify) spv-vc #1 predictor of solver time; blowup alarm
ivl_size histogram IvlCmd nodes (post-desugar_method) spv-ivl desugar cost; loop/call fan-out
solver_time histogram ms, p50/p95/p99 spv-smt SmtSolver::check wall time per method
solver_result counter {Unsat, Sat, Unknown} spv-smt maps to Verified / VError / timeout-or-incompleteness
unknown_rate gauge Unknown / methods_total spv-verify completeness erosion (quantifiers/triggers/timeout)
timeout_rate gauge timeouts / methods_total spv-smt distinct from logical Unknown; budget pressure
counterexample_count counter count spv-verify = methods_failed (one Counterexample per VError min)
assertions_per_method histogram count of Assert(_, Reason) spv-ivl proof-obligation density; complexity proxy
verror_kind counter by VErrorKind spv-verify failure taxonomy (which class dominates)
smt_emit_bytes histogram bytes SMT-LIB 2 spv-smt encoding size; solver parse cost
quantifier_count / trigger_count histogram count spv-smt matching-loop / Unknown risk
wall_total / cpu_total gauge s spv-cli end-to-end run cost (CI budget)
peak_rss gauge MB spv-cli VC/SmtTerm construction memory

Counters/gauges/histograms are computed in-process and dumped at run end; no external collector required. Histogram = explicit buckets (e.g. vc_size: 10/100/1k/10k/100k+ nodes).

27.2 Structured tracing (per-method span)

One trace event per method (tracing crate, JSON layer). Pipeline-stage timings let an operator see where a slow/stuck method spent its budget.

{"method":"BinarySearch", "ivl_size":214, "vc_size":1830, "verror_kinds":[],
 "stages_ms":{"parse":0,"typecheck":1,"desugar":2,"wp":4,"passify":3,"simplify":1,"smt_emit":2,"solver":812},
 "solver_result":"Unsat", "verdict":"Verified", "triggers":7, "quantifiers":3}
  • Span fields LOCKED to spine stages: parse (spv-syntax) -> typecheck (spv-ast) -> desugar (spv-ivl) -> wp + passify + simplify (spv-vc) -> smt_emit (spv-smt) -> solver (SmtSolver::check).
  • Trace level: per-method = INFO; per-stage IvlCmd/VcFormula/SmtTerm dumps = DEBUG (gated by print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt). Solver stdin/stdout = TRACE only — verbose, PII-free (synthetic SMT names).
  • Sampling: none — every method is traced (bounded N, batch tool). For very large codebases (>50k methods) emit summary buckets at INFO, full spans at DEBUG.

27.3 Logging philosophy

  • Library is silent + pure: spv-verify returns VerifyResult; emits no logs, never exit()s, never prints. Diagnostics surface as typed values (VError{kind,span,counterexample}, TypeError, ParseErrorKind). Optional IVL/VC/SMT artifacts returned on request, not logged.
  • CLI owns I/O: spv logs progress (verified 412/500 methods…), the verdict table, and metrics. RUST_LOG/--verbose controls level. --report json for CI ingestion; human table by default.
  • Deterministic logs: no timestamps in artifact dumps (reproducible diffs); wall-clock only in the run-summary line. Synthetic DSA/skolem names in any solver-facing log; never leak source secrets — source is the user's, logs echo only spans + identifiers already in the program.
  • Exit codes: 0 all Verified; 1 any Failed; 2 any Unknown/timeout (distinguish "found a bug" from "couldn't decide"); >2 tool error (parse/type). CI gates on 0.

27.4 The verification dashboard (large-codebase operator view)

Audience: an engineer verifying / re-verifying a big annotated codebase, or watching the CI gate. Built from --report report.json (no live service; render via static HTML / CI summary).

  • Verdict board: verified / failed / unknown / timeout counts + % green; trend vs previous run (regression = a method that was Verified now Failed/Unknown).
  • Slowest methods: top-N by solver_time p99 + their vc_size, quantifier_count, trigger_count — the triage queue.
  • Heaviest VCs: top-N by vc_size / ivl_size — candidates for method splitting or invariant strengthening.
  • Failure taxonomy: VErrorKind breakdown (e.g. LoopInvariantNotMaintained 60% => invariant-quality problem, not bugs).
  • Unknown/timeout panel: list with quantifier_count/trigger_count — completeness erosion watchlist.
  • Soundness banner: last differential-vs-Boogie/Dafny + benchmark-suite (Phase 13) result; RED if any expected-FAIL program reported Verified. This banner outranks everything.

27.5 SLOs / SLIs / error budget

Objective SLI Target Severity if breached
Soundness (absolute, non-negotiable) expected-FAIL programs reported Verified 0, always SEV-0 — stop ship; the gravest failure
Per-method latency solver_time p95 < 2 s SEV-3
Per-method latency tail solver_time p99 < 10 s (then hit per-method timeout) SEV-3
Per-method timeout configurable wall cap default 10 s/method —
Unknown rate unknown_rate < 1% of methods SEV-2 (completeness)
Timeout rate timeout_rate < 0.5% SEV-2
VC size vc_size p95 < 10k nodes (post-passify) SEV-3 (blowup -> check DSA)
Whole-codebase run wall_total (10k methods, parallel) < 10 min on 8 cores SEV-3
Determinism re-run verdict diff 0 (same input -> same verdicts) SEV-2 (flaky proofs erode trust)

Soundness has no error budget — a single false "verified" is a release blocker. Latency/Unknown budgets are advisory; they gate triage, never correctness. passify (DSA) is the guardrail keeping vc_size linear-ish vs exponential (Flanagan-Saxe); a VC-size SLO breach is the canary for a passive-form regression.

27.6 Incident response & runbooks

Symptom Likely cause Runbook
Method times out / Unknown VC too big; bad/missing triggers; matching loop; method too large spv print-vc --method M (inspect vc_size, quantifier/Trigger count); add/sharpen triggers; raise --smt-timeout; split the method into smaller contracted methods (modular verification scales); strengthen the loop invariant to cut search
Spurious failure (you believe the program is correct) weak/wrong loop invariant (inductive assert(inv) fails — false counterexample to your invariant, not the program); missing requires; under-specified callee ensures spv print-ivl/print-vc --method M; read the Counterexample bindings at VError.span; check LoopInvariantNotMaintained vs …OnEntry; strengthen invariant / add requires / tighten callee contract
"Verified" but I think it is buggy the gravest case — possible soundness bug: dropped Assert, mis-framed Havoc(modifies), mis-encoded operator -> SmtTerm, or wrong wp rule escalate SEV-0; run the Phase 13 benchmark suite + differential vs Boogie/Dafny subset; print-smt --method M and inspect check(NOT VC); add the failing program as an expected-FAIL fixture; do NOT ship until the soundness suite is green
Solver returns Unknown only on some host nondeterministic solver, version/seed drift, --smt-timeout race pin solver version; set solver random seed; raise timeout; assert determinism in CI
Run OOM / peak_rss spike giant VcFormula/SmtTerm (no/broken simplify) check simplify ran; cap per-method size; split method; stream SMT-LIB emission
Counterexample names unreadable (x@3, skolems) reconstruction not mapping DSA/skolem -> source verify Phase 11 mapping (Counterexample.bindings: Vec<(Atom, Value)>); ensure Reason{kind,span} propagated from desugar
Parse/type errors block the run malformed annotations ParseErrorKind / TypeError are returned, not panics; fix source; tool exit >2

Escalation: soundness (SEV-0) -> halt release, freeze spv-vc/spv-ivl/spv-smt until a regression fixture is added and green. Latency/Unknown (SEV-2/3) -> open triage, never block on correct-but-slow.

27.7 Deployment, configuration & versioning

  • Distribution: workspace published as crates (spv-syntax … spv-verify) on a registry + the spv binary (static, via Dockerfile). Semver per crate; the spine type names (Program, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm, VerifyResult, VErrorKind) are the public API — breaking renames = major bump.
  • SMT backend is pluggable behind the SmtSolver trait: --solver z3|spv-smt278|<path>; default = bundled. #278 or Z3 selected at runtime; no recompile. SMT-LIB 2 is the wire contract.
  • Knobs (CLI flags / config file, all defaulted): --smt-timeout <ms> (per-method, default 10000), --jobs <N> (per-method parallelism — methods are independent, embarrassingly parallel), --smt-seed, --fuel <k> (recursive pure-function unfolding), --report json, --print-{ast,ivl,vc,smt}, --strict (treat any Unknown as failure for the gate). Push/pop incremental solving is per-method (one solver context, push contract, check(NOT VC), pop).
  • Reproducibility: pin solver version + seed + crate versions => identical verdicts across hosts; this is the deployment contract (a verifier whose verdict drifts is useless). CI records spv --version + solver version + suite hash in the report.
  • Capacity: scales by --jobs across cores; memory bounded per method by vc_size cap; no shared state between methods (modular verification) => linear horizontal scaling, no coordination.

27.8 Operational invariants (must always hold in production)

  • A reported Verified is never wrong on the Phase 13 soundness suite — checked every CI run; failure = release-blocking SEV-0.
  • Every VError carries a non-degenerate span + VErrorKind + reconstructed Counterexample (no "verification failed" with no location).
  • Same source + same solver version + same seed => byte-identical verdicts (determinism).
  • Unknown/timeout is reported as itself (exit 2), never silently downgraded to Verified — completeness gaps stay visible.
  • The library never panics on attacker-controlled annotated input — malformed input yields ParseErrorKind/TypeError, fuzz-checked (Phase 13).

Compatibility & Versioning

Scope: what spv promises to consumers across versions — the public Rust API of the seven spv-* crates, the .spv source language grammar, the SMT-LIB 2 wire format to external solvers, the VErrorKind taxonomy, and the differential-parity contract vs a pinned Boogie/Dafny subset. Soundness is never a compatibility bargaining chip: a release MAY tighten what verifies (regain a dropped check), MUST NOT loosen it (let a buggy program pass).

28.1 Compatibility surfaces (ranked by blast radius)

# Surface Owner crate Consumer Stability tier
1 .spv source-language grammar spv-syntax every .spv file ever written frozen — pinned snapshot, additive-only
2 SmtSolver trait + SMT-LIB 2 dialect spv-smt #278 / Z3 / cvc5 backends stable — trait semver + wire profile
3 VErrorKind taxonomy spv-verify CI gates, IDE plugins, scripts matching error kinds stable — additive-only, non-#[non_exhaustive] removal forbidden
4 spv CLI subcommands + exit codes spv-cli shell scripts, CI stable — flags additive, exit codes frozen
5 Core IR types (Program,Stmt,Expr,IvlCmd,VcFormula,SmtTerm) spv-syntax/ast/ivl/vc/smt downstream Rust crates, plugins semver — #[non_exhaustive] enums, breaking only on major
6 print-ast/print-ivl/print-vc/print-smt textual dumps spv-cli golden tests, debugging best-effort — format may drift on minor; not a parse target

Tier semantics: frozen = no breaking change ever (deprecate, never remove). stable = breaking only on major version, ≥1 minor of deprecation warning first. semver = ordinary Cargo semver. best-effort = no guarantee; do not script against it.

28.2 Cargo semver across the workspace

  • All seven spv-* crates version in lockstep (single workspace version); a consumer pins one and gets a coherent set. Rationale: spv-vc depends on spv-ivl depends on spv-ast … — independent semver would let mismatched IR types compile but mis-encode.
  • Pre-1.0: 0.MINOR.PATCH; a MINOR bump may break (Cargo treats 0.x minors as majors). Post-1.0: standard MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.
  • All public enums that the spine lists with variants (Stmt, Expr, Type, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtSort, VErrorKind, ParseErrorKind, TypeError, SmtResult) are #[non_exhaustive] → adding a variant is minor, not major. Consumers must keep a _ => arm.
  • #[doc(hidden)] items (e.g. internal DSA-versioning helpers in passify, skolem-name mangling) are not public surface; may change any release.
  • cargo-semver-checks runs in CI (Phase 13 gate); a flagged breaking change fails the build unless the major is bumped.

28.3 .spv source language versioning (Surface 1 — frozen)

  • The grammar (Phase 1 EBNF) ships as a pinned snapshot grammar/spv-v1.ebnf, content-hashed; the hash is embedded in the spv binary and printed by spv --version.
  • Additive-only evolution: new statements (Stmt), types (Type), spec clauses (requires/ensures/modifies/decreases siblings), quantifier/trigger forms, or expression operators are added so that every previously-valid .spv file still parses and type-checks to the same AST and still verifies (modulo soundness re-tightening, §28.7).
  • Reserved-word policy: a fixed reserved-word list is frozen at v1; new keywords come from this reserve so existing identifiers never collide. New features needing genuinely new keywords land only on a grammar major (spv-v2.ebnf) with a migration note.
  • Deprecation: a construct slated for removal emits a ParseError-adjacent warning diagnostic (not ParseErrorKind) for ≥2 minors before any hard error; spv verify --deny-deprecated opts CI into early failure.
  • A // spv:lang v1 pragma may pin a file to a grammar epoch; absent pragma = newest. Cross-epoch programs in one run are rejected with a clear diagnostic, never silently coerced.

28.4 SMT-LIB 2 interop (Surface 2 — stable)

  • spv-smt emits a conservative SMT-LIB 2.6 profile: logic AUFLIA/AUFNIA (arrays + uninterpreted functions + linear/nonlinear int arithmetic), (set-option :produce-models true), (check-sat), (get-model). No solver-proprietary commands in the portable path.
  • The SmtSolver trait is the pluggability seam: fn check(&mut self, assertions: &[SmtTerm]) -> SmtResult. Adding a method to the trait is breaking unless it has a default body; new optional capabilities (incremental push/pop, unsat cores, timeouts) are surfaced via default-method extension traits (e.g. SmtSolverIncremental: SmtSolver) so existing backends keep compiling.
  • Backend-agnostic guarantee: any solver consuming the emitted SMT-LIB 2 must reproduce the Unsat/Sat/Unknown verdict; #278 and Z3 are interchangeable behind the trait. Trigger annotations (Phase 9) use standard :pattern syntax → portable across Z3/cvc5.
  • Model parsing tolerates solver-specific value syntax (e.g. (- 5) vs -5, as-array); the parser is profiled per backend but the Model struct (var → Value) is stable. A solver returning an unparseable model degrades to Unknown, never to a false Verified.
  • SMT-LIB version bumps (2.6 → 2.7+) are absorbed inside spv-smt without changing SmtTerm/SmtSort/SmtResult public types.

28.5 VErrorKind taxonomy stability (Surface 3 — stable, additive-only)

  • The eight variants are a public, scriptable contract: AssertionMightNotHold, PostconditionMightNotHold, PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, DivisionByZero, ModifiesViolation, MightNotTerminate. CI gates and IDE plugins match on these names + the stable machine codes (e.g. E-ASSERT, E-POST, E-PRE-CALL, E-INV-ENTRY, E-INV-STEP, E-DIV0, E-MODIFIES, E-TERM).
  • Renaming or removing a variant/code is forbidden; only addition (e.g. a future E-OVERFLOW) is allowed and is a minor (enum is #[non_exhaustive]).
  • A Reason carried on an IvlCmd::Assert always maps to exactly one VErrorKind; the mapping table (Phase 11) is part of the contract — a given desugaring pattern must always classify to the same kind across versions, so counterexample tooling stays stable.
  • Exit codes (Surface 4): 0 = Verified, 1 = Failed (≥1 VError), 2 = usage/ParseError/TypeError, 3 = Unknown from SMT (timeout/incompleteness). Frozen across all minors; Unknown never collapses to 0.

28.6 MSRV policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78 at v1.0. MSRV bumps are a minor-version event, documented in the changelog, and may not exceed stable − 2 (lag the toolchain by ≤2 releases) so consumers on slightly older toolchains keep building.
  • spv-syntax, spv-ast, spv-ivl, spv-vc, spv-smt are no_std + alloc (per spine) → MSRV governs only alloc/core features; spv-verify/spv-cli are std. An MSRV bump must not silently require std in a no_std crate.
  • CI pins the MSRV toolchain in a dedicated job; an inadvertent use of a newer-than-MSRV API fails that job.

28.7 Adding statements / types / spec features without breaking consumers

Procedure (every new language feature follows it end to end):

  1. Grammar (spv-syntax): extend EBNF additively (§28.3); new Stmt/Expr/Type variant on a #[non_exhaustive] enum → minor.
  2. Typing (spv-ast): add TypeChecker rules + any new TypeError variant (additive); old programs unaffected.
  3. Desugaring (spv-ivl): lower the new construct to existing IvlCmd primitives (Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign/Seq/Choice) if possible → no new IVL variant, no consumer break. A genuinely new IVL primitive is a rare, reviewed event (new IvlCmd variant = minor under #[non_exhaustive]).
  4. wp/passify (spv-vc): if step 3 reused existing IvlCmd, wp and passify need zero change — the strongest stability lever in the design. New VcFormula/Pred forms only when the logic genuinely grows.
  5. SMT (spv-smt): new SmtSort/SmtTerm constructors as needed (#[non_exhaustive]), with triggers; portable SMT-LIB profile preserved.
  6. Soundness re-check: the new path gets expected-PASS and expected-FAIL fixtures before merge (Phase 13). A feature that cannot be shown sound does not ship.

Invariant: lowering through existing IVL/VC primitives is the default, so most features are additions to the front/back of the pipeline and never perturb wp/passify — the parts whose correctness is hardest to re-establish.

28.8 Differential-parity guarantee vs pinned Boogie/Dafny subset

  • A frozen reference subset (the .spv constructs expressible in a chosen Boogie/Dafny fragment) is pinned by version + a corpus of programs (Phase 13 differential suite).
  • Parity contract on that subset: for every corpus program, spv's verdict must agree on PASS/FAIL with the reference tool, OR spv may legitimately return Unknown (SMT incompleteness) — but spv returning Verified where the reference reports a real failure is a release-blocking soundness regression.
  • Parity is directional: 100% soundness parity (no false Verified the reference catches) is a hard gate; completeness parity (matching every Verified) is tracked, not gated — Unknown/timeouts and trigger/quantifier differences are expected, recorded as a known-gap list per release.
  • The reference tool + its version are pinned in CI; bumping them is a deliberate, changelog-noted event with a re-baselined known-gap list. New spv features outside the reference subset are exempt from parity (no comparator exists) but still carry their own PASS/FAIL fixtures.

28.9 What is explicitly NOT guaranteed

Not guaranteed Why
Stable solver runtime / VC size across versions Simplification (Phase 8) & trigger tuning evolve; perf is tracked vs targets, not frozen
print-vc/print-ivl/print-smt text format Debugging aid; golden tests may need rebaselining on minors
Exact counterexample variable spelling / DSA-name rendering Skolem/DSA mangling is internal; Counterexample.bindings maps to source Atoms, which are stable
Identical Unknown vs Verified outcome for quantifier-heavy programs across solvers Bounded by SMT incompleteness, not a verifier bug
Non-spine internal APIs (#[doc(hidden)], crate-private) Implementation detail; change freely

Bottom line: grammar + SmtSolver trait + VErrorKind taxonomy + soundness are the load-bearing promises; everything observable downstream of them is additive-or-major, and no release may ever trade soundness for compatibility.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Tests exist to defend the section-7 invariants — soundness foremost (a buggy program must NEVER report Verified). Every layer of the parse -> typecheck -> IVL -> wp -> SMT pipeline gets unit coverage; the whole pipeline gets integration + soundness/completeness suites; the algebraically risky transforms (wp, passify, desugar_*) get executable-semantics property tests; the byte-level surfaces get fuzzers. Coverage target: >= 90% line / >= 85% branch per crate (cargo llvm-cov), 100% of IvlCmd/VcFormula/VErrorKind enum arms exercised.

29.1 Invariant -> test-class traceability

Inv (§7) Statement Primary guard Test class
I1 VCGen never lets a buggy program verify soundness soundness/ corpus + property wp_soundness
I2 wp rules match Dijkstra (5 rules) wp algebra unit wp::* + property vs executable semantics
I3 passify preserves validity DSA correctness property passify_preserves_validity
I4 desugar_method preserves source behavior IVL lowering property desugar_preserves_behavior
I5 Loop = invariant cut, never unrolled modular loops unit ivl::loop_cut + soundness loop cases
I6 Call uses contract, never body modular verif unit ivl::call_lowering + cross-method soundness
I7 SMT encoding faithful (valid VC <=> Unsat ¬VC) encoding unit smt::encode + differential vs Z3
I8 Unknown is never reported as Verified solver honesty unit verify::unknown_* + timeout fixtures
I9 Counterexample maps to real source var/point CE reconstruction unit verify::ce_* + golden CE

Each test asserts its invariant tag in a comment // INV: I1 so a coverage script can prove every invariant has >= 1 live test.

29.2 Unit tests (per-stage, table-driven)

  • spv-syntax / Parser — one test per grammar production (§13 EBNF): method header, each Stmt arm (VarDecl/Assign/If/While/Call/Assert/Assume/Return), each Expr arm (Lit/Var/Un/Bin/Call/Old/Quant/Field), every spec clause (requires/ensures/modifies/decreases), trigger lists, operator precedence/associativity. Negative cases assert exact ParseErrorKind (UnexpectedToken/BadSpec/BadType) + span. Round-trip: parse(print_ast(p)) == p (AST-equality).
  • spv-ast / TypeChecker — one test per rule: Int/Bool/Ref/Map/Seq inference, spec-must-be-Bool -> NonBoolSpec, old() only in ensures -> OldOutsideEnsures, modifies well-formedness -> BadModifies, arity -> ArityMismatch, undeclared -> Undeclared, mismatch -> Mismatch{expected,got}. Each negative test asserts the precise TypeError variant and span.
  • spv-ivl / Desugarer — golden IvlCmd per construct: if -> Choice(Seq[assume g; ..], Seq[assume ¬g; ..]); while -> cut [assert I; havoc targets; assume I; (assume g; body; assert I; assume false) [] (assume ¬g)]; call -> Seq[assert pre; havoc modifies; assume post]; Assign/Return. Assert each emitted Assert carries the correct Reason{kind,span} (LoopInvariantOnEntry vs LoopInvariantNotMaintained vs PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, etc.). Snapshot tests via insta on the pretty-printed IVL.
  • spv-vc / wp — one test per rule, smallest-witness form: wp(assume p,Q)=p=>Q; wp(assert p,Q)=p∧Q; wp(havoc x,Q)=∀x.Q; wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x]; wp(c1;c2,Q)=wp(c1,wp(c2,Q)); wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q)∧wp(c2,Q). Substitution-capture test: wp(havoc y, ∀y…) must alpha-rename. passify units: each var assigned once, fresh versions monotone, Assign -> Assume(=), Choice join renames. Simplifier units: constant fold, Imp(True,Q)=Q, And/Or flatten/dedupe — and a guard that simplification is validity-preserving (sat-equiv check on small inputs).
  • spv-smt / encode — VcFormula -> SmtTerm per arm; sort assignment (Int/Bool/Uninterp/Array); old() -> second state var; quantifier emitted with :pattern trigger; SMT-LIB string golden (insta) vs (set-logic …)(assert …)(check-sat). Assert NO ground term escapes a declared sort (well-sortedness checker).
  • spv-verify / mapping — Unsat -> Verified; Sat(model) -> Failed(vec![VError{kind,span,counterexample}]); Unknown -> Failed with MightNotTerminate/incompleteness note, never Verified (I8); VErrorKind chosen from the failing Reason; CE bindings decode model ints/bools to Value, at = failing Span.

29.3 Integration tests (source.spv -> VerifyResult)

End-to-end through the real spv-verify driver with the default SmtSolver (Z3 or #278): feed a .spv file, assert VerifyResult. Each fixture pairs a program with // EXPECT: Verified or // EXPECT: Failed(<VErrorKind>@<line>). Drives spv-cli subcommands too: print-ast, print-ivl, print-vc, print-smt outputs are insta-snapshotted so a pipeline drift is caught at the earliest stage. Latency budget per fixture < 2 s wall (CI gate).

29.4 Soundness suite — tests/soundness/ (expected-FAIL; the cardinal gate)

Programs that are buggy; the verifier MUST report Failed with the right VErrorKind. A single false Verified here = build-failing, release-blocking (I1). Buckets, each with the VErrorKind it must trigger:

Bucket Example bug Must report
assert assert x>0 with x unconstrained AssertionMightNotHold
postcondition body returns wrong value PostconditionMightNotHold
call-pre call site violates callee requires PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold
loop-entry invariant false before loop LoopInvariantOnEntry
loop-step invariant not preserved by body LoopInvariantNotMaintained
div-zero a / b, b may be 0 DivisionByZero
frame writes outside modifies ModifiesViolation
termination decreases not decreasing / missing MightNotTerminate

Mutation testing (cargo-mutants) on spv-ivl/spv-vc/spv-smt: a mutant that drops an Assert, weakens a Reason, flips a wp connective, or mis-encodes an operator MUST be killed by this suite (target >= 95% mutants killed). Surviving mutants -> add a soundness fixture. This directly stresses the §-key-facts pitfall "a dropped assertion / mis-framed havoc / mis-encoded operator => false verified."

29.5 Completeness suite — tests/completeness/ (expected-PASS; tracked separately)

Correct, fully-annotated programs that SHOULD verify: max/abs/swap, sum/factorial loops with invariants, binary search, array-bounds, two-state old() post, pure-function callers, contract-only modular calls. Each // EXPECT: Verified. Completeness is secondary, bounded by SMT — failures here downgrade to known-fail (timeout/Unknown/quantifier instantiation), never block release; tracked in the ledger (29.9). Distinguish a real completeness regression (was-PASS now-FAIL on a stable solver) — that blocks — from solver-version flake.

29.6 Differential testing vs a Boogie/Dafny subset

For the overlapping language subset, transpile each soundness/+completeness/ fixture to Boogie/Dafny and compare verdicts: disagreement is a bug. Strongest signal = we say Verified while Boogie/Dafny says fail (a soundness hole, hard fail). Also differential at the SMT layer: emit our SMT-LIB, run through both #278 and Z3; both must agree Unsat/Sat (guards I7 — encoding faithfulness). Gated --feature differential (needs external boogie/dafny/z3 on PATH); skipped, not failed, when absent.

29.7 Property-based testing (proptest) — the algebraic guarantees

  • wp_soundness (I2) — generate a small IvlCmd (bounded depth, Int/Bool vars) + a random initial store; an executable IVL interpreter (test-only, runs Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign/Seq/Choice over concrete states) and wp(c, Q) must agree: every initial state satisfying wp(c,Q) yields no Assert failure and a final state satisfying Q. Counterexample => wp rule bug.
  • passify_preserves_validity (I3) — for random IvlCmd c, VC(c) valid <=> VC(passify(c)) valid (check both via the SMT backend); plus structural: every DSA var assigned exactly once.
  • desugar_preserves_behavior (I4) — random source Method; the interpreter on the surface semantics and on desugar_method(m) agree on accept/reject + observable returns.
  • encode_roundtrip — random VcFormula; SmtTerm well-sorted, and valid(f) <=> Unsat(encode(¬f)) on the backend.
  • simplify_sound — simplify(f) is logically equivalent to f (SMT equivalence on small f).

Shrinking on. proptest-regressions/ committed so every found counterexample becomes a permanent regression.

29.8 Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer; nightly + pre-release)

Target Input Oracle (no UB, no panic, no false-Verified)
fuzz_parse arbitrary bytes Parser returns Program or ParseError; never panics/loops
fuzz_desugar arbitrary typed AST desugar_method total; output well-formed IvlCmd
fuzz_wp arbitrary IvlCmd wp terminates; no capture; well-formed VcFormula
fuzz_smt arbitrary VcFormula SMT-LIB emission well-sorted, parseable by Z3

Differential fuzz fuzz_verify_consistency: a generated program through full pipeline must not return Verified if the test-interpreter found a reachable Assert failure (catches I1 on adversarial input). Corpus seeded from tests/ fixtures; ASan/UBSan on; >= 24 h clean before any release tag.

29.9 Conformance buckets + pass/fail/known-fail ledger

tests/CONFORMANCE.md ledger: every fixture row = {id, bucket, expect, status∈{pass,fail,known-fail}, solver, note}. make conformance recomputes and diffs against committed ledger — an undeclared transition fails CI. Rules: a soundness row may NEVER be known-fail (only pass); a completeness row may be known-fail only with a logged cause (timeout / Unknown / quantifier). xtask report renders the matrix (bucket × status) so soundness=100% pass is visible at a glance.

29.10 Performance regression gates (criterion)

Benchmarks tie to §-targets; criterion baseline committed, CI fails on > 10% regression:

Bench Metric Target
bench_wp VC nodes for N-statement straight-line linear-ish (no exponential) — N=1000 < 50 ms
bench_passify DSA size vs naive DSA VcFormula <= 2× input; assert no exponential blow-up
bench_encode SMT-LIB build time < 20 ms / method
bench_solve end-to-end discharge, median fixture < 500 ms
bench_mem peak RSS, largest benchmark method < 256 MB

A passify regression that reintroduces Flanagan-Saxe exponential growth is caught here (VC-node count assertion), not just by wall-clock.

29.11 CI wiring

make test = unit + integration + soundness + completeness (deterministic seed). Stages: cargo fmt --check, clippy -D warnings, cargo test --workspace, cargo llvm-cov (coverage gate), proptest (fixed seed in CI), cargo-mutants (PR-gated on spv-vc/spv-ivl/spv-smt), differential + fuzz (nightly/pre-release). Release gate = soundness 100% pass + mutants >= 95% killed + 24 h clean fuzz + no perf regression + ledger diff clean. Soundness failure is non-overridable.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducibility is a soundness adjunct: a verifier that says "verified" must be rebuildable bit-stably and pinned to an exact solver, else Unsat verdicts are not auditable. This section locks the toolchain, the CI matrix, the supply-chain gates, the crates.io publication order, and the signed spv artifact pipeline.

30.1 Reproducible builds — toolchain & lockfile pinning

Artifact Pin mechanism Value Why
Rust toolchain rust-toolchain.toml (committed, repo root) channel = "1.78.0" (MSRV == release pin) byte-stable codegen; no float nightly drift
Components same rustfmt, clippy gate tools versioned with compiler
Profile same profile = "minimal" CI cache size
CLI deps Cargo.lock committed exact =x.y.z graph spv is a binary → lock is authoritative
Lib deps Cargo.lock committed but advisory — libs publish with caret ranges; lock only constrains workspace builds
Solver solvers.lock (custom, repo root) z3 == 4.13.0 SHA256 + spv-smt-z3 == <git rev> for #278 VC verdict reproducibility (Phase 10) 
#rust-toolchain.toml  (LOCKED — channel == MSRV)
[toolchain]
channel    = "1.78.0"
components = ["rustfmt", "clippy"]
profile    = "minimal"
  • Cargo.lock checked in → cargo build --locked everywhere; CI fails on lock drift (--locked errors if regeneration needed).
  • RUSTFLAGS frozen per profile in .cargo/config.toml; release adds -C strip=symbols only for the binary, never for fuzz/bench builds (need symbols).
  • Determinism target: two clean builds of spv-cli on the same host produce identical SHA256 (modulo embedded build timestamp, which is suppressed via SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH).
  • solvers.lock consumed by Phase 13 SMT-suite job and Phase 10 integration tests; a solver version bump is a deliberate PR (re-baselines expected Unsat/Sat/Unknown per fixture) — never silent, because Unknown↔Unsat boundary shifts with solver versions.

30.2 Build & test matrix

.github/workflows/ci.yml  jobs (matrix)
+----------------+----------+-----------+--------------------------------------------+
| job            | toolchain| os        | command                                    |
+----------------+----------+-----------+--------------------------------------------+
| test-stable    | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo test --workspace --locked --all-feat |
| test-msrv      | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo build --workspace --locked (== pin)  |
| test-mac       | 1.78.0   | macos arm | cargo test --workspace --locked            |
| no_std-check   | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo build -p spv-syntax -p spv-ast \      |
|                |          |           |   --no-default-features (no_std+alloc)     |
| fmt            | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo fmt --all -- --check                 |
| clippy         | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets \    |
|                |          |           |   --all-features -- -D warnings            |
| doc            | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo doc --workspace --no-deps (RUSTDOC.. |
| smt-suite      | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | see 30.3 (pinned solver, Phase 13 gate)    |
| fuzz-smoke     | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | see 30.4 (cargo-fuzz, time-boxed)          |
| bench-guard    | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | see 30.5 (criterion, regression check)     |
| deny           | 1.78.0   | linux x64 | cargo deny check (30.6)                     |
+----------------+----------+-----------+--------------------------------------------+
  • MSRV == release channel == 1.78.0 → only one compiler, so test-msrv collapses to a --locked build-only check (catches deps that raise their MSRV). If MSRV is ever raised, it is a rust-toolchain.toml PR + CHANGELOG note.
  • no_std-check enforces the spv-syntax/spv-ast no_std + alloc invariant (Core Types): build with --no-default-features; a std-only import there fails the job, protecting downstream embeddability.
  • --all-features in clippy/test-stable exercises the spv-smt z3/smt278 backend feature flags so feature-gated SmtSolver impls are linted.
  • doc runs with RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" → broken intra-doc links (to VcFormula, IvlCmd, wp, desugar_method) fail CI.
  • All workspace jobs use --locked; cache key = hash(Cargo.lock + rust-toolchain.toml).

30.3 SMT-backed suite job (smt-suite)

Runs the Phase 13 expected-PASS / expected-FAIL benchmark through a pinned solver — the soundness gate in CI.

Step Detail
install solver fetch z3 == 4.13.0 (or build #278 at solvers.lock rev), verify SHA256
run cargo test -p spv-verify --test suite --locked -- --test-threads=1
per-fixture timeout 10 s wall (SmtSolver (set-option :timeout ...)); exceed → Unknown, not a hang
soundness assertion any expected-FAIL fixture reporting Verified ⇒ hard CI failure (the cardinal property)
completeness drift expected-PASS now Unknown/Failed ⇒ failure unless annotated #[ignore = "known-incomplete: <reason>"]
determinism (set-option :random-seed 0) + single thread → stable Unsat/Sat/Unknown per fixture
solver matrix (optional) also run against Z3 + #278 to cross-check verdicts (differential, Phase 13)
  • Soundness failures (expected-FAIL → Verified) are non-overridable: no --allow, no skip label. Completeness regressions are overridable only via an explicit #[ignore] with a reason string, tracked in the CHANGELOG.
  • Job records VC size (formula node count) + solver wall-time per fixture to an artifact for trend tracking (feeds 30.5).

30.4 Fuzz job (.github/workflows/fuzz.yml + fuzz-smoke in CI)

cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) targets, one per pipeline stage (Phase 13 fuzzing requirement):

Target Entry Invariant fuzzed
fuzz_parse Parser on arbitrary bytes no panic; Err(ParseError) or valid Program
fuzz_typeck arbitrary Program → TypeChecker no panic; Ok or precise TypeError
fuzz_desugar typed AST → desugar_method no panic; well-formed IvlCmd; every Assert carries a Reason
fuzz_wp IvlCmd → wp(c, True) no panic; terminates; well-formed VcFormula
fuzz_passify IvlCmd → passify then wp DSA single-assignment property holds; no var assigned twice
fuzz_smt_emit VcFormula → SMT-LIB string emits parseable SMT-LIB 2; no panic
  • CI fuzz-smoke: each target 60 s, -max_len=4096, fail on crash/timeout/OOM. Seed corpus committed under fuzz/corpus/<target>/ (minimized via cargo fuzz cmin).
  • Scheduled fuzz.yml: nightly cron, 30 min/target, ASan + UBSan, uploads new crashers as artifacts and opens an issue.
  • Differential fuzz (fuzz_wp vs fuzz_passify+wp): wp(c, Q) and wp(passify(c), Q) must be equi-valid (Flanagan-Saxe) — divergence ⇒ a DSA bug that could mask an assertion ⇒ soundness risk.

30.5 Bench job (.github/workflows/bench.yml + bench-guard)

criterion benches, regression-gated:

Bench Metric Target CI guard
bench_wp_blowup VC node count, naive vs passify DSA VC ≤ O(n) vs O(2^k) passive growth ≥ linear-ish; flag superlinear
bench_vc_size VcFormula nodes / 1000-LOC method ≤ ~50k nodes +15% vs baseline ⇒ warn
bench_solve_time SmtSolver.check wall, pinned solver ≤ 2 s p50, ≤ 10 s p99 per method +20% regression ⇒ fail
bench_pipeline parse→typeck→ivl→vc→smt end-to-end ≤ 5 s for 1k-LOC program +20% ⇒ fail
  • bench-guard in CI: quick subset (--sample-size 10), compares against committed benches/baseline.json via critcmp; gate thresholds above.
  • bench.yml: scheduled + on release tags, full sample, dedicated runner, publishes flamegraphs + critcmp diff as artifacts. Baseline re-committed only on deliberate perf PRs.
  • Solver time uses the solvers.lock solver so numbers are comparable across runs.

30.6 Supply-chain: cargo-deny, SBOM, audit

deny.toml  (LOCKED policy)
[advisories]  vulnerability = "deny"  unmaintained = "warn"  yanked = "deny"
[bans]        multiple-versions = "warn"  wildcards = "deny"   # no "*" deps
[licenses]    allow = ["MIT","Apache-2.0","Unicode-DFS-2016","BSD-3-Clause"]
              confidence-threshold = 0.93
[sources]     unknown-registry = "deny"  unknown-git = "deny"
Tool Job Output
cargo-deny check deny (CI, every PR) advisories + bans + licenses + sources
cargo-auditable build used for all release spv binaries embeds dependency graph SBOM into the ELF/Mach-O
cargo audit bin spv release verification step re-reads embedded SBOM, scans RUSTSEC offline
cargo cyclonedx release emits spv.cdx.json (CycloneDX SBOM) alongside artifacts
  • Wildcard deps denied → forces exact/caret pins; solver FFI (z3/#278) audited as a source.
  • SBOM is embedded (cargo-auditable) and sidecar (CycloneDX) → the shipped binary self-describes its dependency tree, auditable post-hoc without the repo.

30.7 crates.io publication order & semver

Publication MUST follow the dependency DAG bottom-up (a crate cannot publish before its deps exist on the registry):

spv-syntax → spv-ast → spv-ivl → spv-vc → spv-smt → spv-verify → spv-cli
   (1)         (2)        (3)       (4)       (5)        (6)         (7)
Rule Detail
version unison all seven share one workspace.package.version (version.workspace = true); released as a set
order publish strictly 1→7; release.yml runs cargo publish -p <crate> in that sequence with a --locked build
dry-run cargo publish --dry-run -p <crate> for all seven on every release-PR before any real publish
semver gate cargo-semver-checks check-release on libs (1–6) vs last tag; a breaking change without a major bump fails
breaking ⇒ major changing wp/passify/desugar_method signatures, IvlCmd/VcFormula/SmtTerm/VErrorKind variants ⇒ semver-major
spv-cli not API-versioned for downstream use, but version-stamped identically; ships the spv binary
  • Pre-1.0: 0.y semver — 0.y.0 is a "major", 0.y.z patch; cargo-semver-checks configured for 0.x rules.
  • A failed publish mid-sequence (e.g. step 4) is recoverable: registry is append-only, re-run resumes at the first unpublished crate (idempotent by version).

30.8 Release artifacts — signed spv binary

Property Value
targets x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu, x86_64-apple-darwin, aarch64-apple-darwin
build cargo auditable build --release --locked -p spv-cli per target (cross via cross for linux-arm)
determinism SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH = tag commit time; -C strip=symbols; reproducible across runners
naming spv-<version>-<target>.tar.gz
checksums SHA256SUMS over all archives
signing cosign sign-blob (keyless, OIDC) → *.sig + *.pem; and detached minisign/GPG over SHA256SUMS
SBOM spv.cdx.json (CycloneDX) + embedded cargo-auditable graph per binary
provenance SLSA provenance attestation (cosign attest) bound to the workflow + commit
solver note release notes record the solvers.lock solver pin; spv shells to the solver, it is not bundled
  • Verify chain: download archive + SHA256SUMS + sig → cosign verify-blob (or minisign -V) → cargo audit bin spv re-scans embedded SBOM. A release that fails self-verify is blocked.

30.9 Workflow topology (.github/workflows/)

ci.yml      on: [push, pull_request]   jobs: test-stable, test-msrv, test-mac,
                                              no_std-check, fmt, clippy, doc,
                                              smt-suite, fuzz-smoke, bench-guard, deny
fuzz.yml    on: schedule (nightly) + workflow_dispatch
                                        jobs: fuzz_{parse,typeck,desugar,wp,passify,smt_emit}
                                              (ASan/UBSan, 30 min each, crash → issue)
bench.yml   on: schedule (weekly) + push tags 'v*'
                                        jobs: bench_{wp_blowup,vc_size,solve_time,pipeline}
                                              (full criterion, critcmp vs baseline, flamegraphs)
release.yml on: push tags 'v*'
                                        jobs: gate (re-run ci + smt-suite) → semver-checks →
                                              publish (spv-syntax..spv-cli, ordered) →
                                              build-artifacts (4 targets, cargo-auditable) →
                                              sign (cosign + minisign, SBOM, SLSA) →
                                              github-release (archives + SHA256SUMS + sigs + SBOM)
  • release.yml re-runs the full ci.yml + smt-suite as a gate job before publishing — no tag ships unless the soundness suite is green on the tagged commit.
  • Tag protocol: vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH; tag triggers the only path that publishes crates or uploads binaries (no manual cargo publish).
  • Concurrency guard: release.yml is concurrency: release (cancel-in-progress false) so two tags cannot race the ordered publish.

30.10 Failure modes & operational notes

Failure Detection Mitigation
solver version drift silently flips Unknown↔Unsat smt-suite baseline mismatch solvers.lock pin; bump = deliberate re-baseline PR
lock drift across PRs --locked build error committed Cargo.lock; bot updates via dedicated PR
MSRV creep from a dep test-msrv build fail pin dep; or raise rust-toolchain.toml channel + note
publish DAG out of order cargo publish "dep not found" enforced 1→7 order in release.yml; dry-run first
nondeterministic VC/SMT in CI flaky smt-suite --test-threads=1, :random-seed 0, :timeout fixed
unsigned/tampered artifact release self-verify step cosign + minisign + SHA256SUMS gate before upload
fuzz finds parser/wp panic fuzz.yml crasher corpus-minimize, add regression fixture, fix, re-run
  • Caching: Swatinem/rust-cache keyed on Cargo.lock+toolchain; fuzz/bench caches separate (different profiles).
  • Runner sizing: bench.yml/smt-suite on a fixed-class runner so solve-time numbers are comparable; ci.yml on standard.
  • Secrets: cosign keyless via OIDC (no long-lived keys); crates.io token scoped to publish-only, used only by release.yml.

Performance Engineering

Verification is solver-dominated, but VCGen pathology (exponential wp, weak triggers, IR allocation churn) silently inflates solver time. This section makes every stage measurable and ties each number to the section-4 targets. Golden rule: never optimize the front end while the SMT call owns 90%+ of wall time — profile first, attribute, then cut the dominant cost.

31.1 Cost model — where time goes

Per-method verify pipeline, expected wall-time share at the section-4 reference scale (≤200 methods, ≤50 stmts/method, ≤3 loops, ≤5 quantifiers/VC):

Stage Crate / fn Typical share Cost driver Pathology
Lex + parse spv-syntax Lexer/Parser 1–3% source bytes none material
Resolve + typeck spv-ast TypeChecker 1–3% decls × scope depth quadratic scope lookup
Desugar spv-ivl desugar_method 1–4% stmts, loop/call count call/loop fan-out
passify (DSA) spv-vc passify 2–6% assigns × Choice depth version explosion
wp spv-vc wp 5–15% IvlCmd size × post size naive Q[e/x] duplication → exp
simplify spv-vc 1–4% VcFormula nodes over-eager normalization
encode + emit spv-smt VcFormula→SmtTerm + SMT-LIB 2–8% term count, triggers string build, dup subterms
SMT check spv-smt SmtSolver::check 60–90% quantifier instantiation, arith, arrays trigger loops, matching depth

Implication: invest in trigger quality, VC size (DSA), and incremental solving (the three solver-time levers), then IR allocation. Front-end micro-opt is last.

31.2 Benchmark taxonomy — criterion micro + macro

cargo bench via criterion (statistical, outlier-rejecting). Three tiers:

Tier Target Inputs Metric Wired in phase
Micro (per-stage) parse, typeck, desugar, passify, wp, encode fixed fixtures, sizes N∈{10,50,200 stmts} ns/op, MB/s, allocs/iter 1–9
Macro (verify) verify one method, Unsat path growing annotated programs ms/method, VC nodes, SMT µs 10–13
Suite (regression) full benchmark corpus (## 25) expected-PASS + expected-FAIL total wall, p50/p95/p99 per method 13

Micro-bench harness shape (criterion Bencher, signature only — no body): fn bench_wp(c: &mut Criterion), registering one c.bench_function("wp/sl200", ..) case that times wp over a pre-built 200-command IvlCmd fixture wrapped in black_box to defeat const-folding.

Bench fixtures live in benches/fixtures/ as compiled-once IvlCmd/VcFormula values; never re-parse inside the timed loop (iter body measures only the target fn). Use black_box on inputs to defeat const-folding the call away.

Scaling benches parameterize size: bench_function_over_inputs with N=stmts and Q=quantifier-count, asserting empirical complexity (regression slope) matches the target order — wp after DSA must be ≈ linear in IvlCmd nodes, not exponential.

31.3 The passive-form win (naive wp vs DSA) — measured, not assumed

Core risk: naive wp(x:=e, Q)=Q[e/x] duplicates Q; through nested Choice (wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q) AND wp(c2,Q)) VC size is O(2^k) in branch/assignment depth (Flanagan-Saxe). passify (DSA: each Var assigned once, fresh version; assignment → assume(x_i == e)) makes it near-linear.

Dedicated A/B bench: build the same IvlCmd, run (a) wp directly on the unpassified command, (b) wp on passify(cmd), count VcFormula nodes and time both. Fixture: k sequential if-then-else over an accumulator (the canonical blow-up shape).

k (branches) naive VC nodes DSA VC nodes naive build DSA build
5 ~2^5·c ~5·c sub-ms sub-ms
10 ~2^10·c ~10·c ms sub-ms
20 ~2^20·c (≈1M) ~20·c seconds / OOM sub-ms
40 intractable (refuse) ~40·c — <1 ms

Target (§4): VC nodes linear in IVL size, ≤ ~10× IvlCmd node count after DSA+simplify; reference method VC ≤ ~10⁴ VcFormula nodes; method that blows past that without DSA is the regression canary. CI gate: assert!(dsa_nodes <= 12 * ivl_nodes) on the k=20 fixture; fail the build if passify regresses toward exponential. This bench is the single most important perf artifact — it guards soundness-adjacent tractability.

31.4 Trigger tuning impact on solver time

Quantifier instantiation in SmtSolver::check is the dominant variable cost on quantifier-heavy VCs. spv-smt emits explicit :pattern triggers (from Trigger on VcFormula::Forall); bad triggers cause matching loops (term explosion, → Unknown/timeout) or too few instantiations (incompleteness, spurious Unknown).

Trigger A/B matrix benched per quantifier-heavy fixture (array/Seq axioms, pure-fn defining axioms, frame axioms):

Trigger strategy Instantiations Solver p50 Solver p99 Failure mode
No trigger (solver-auto MBQI) unbounded high, unstable timeout nondeterministic Unknown
Loose (single subterm) high medium spikes matching loop on transitive axioms
Tight (explicit multi-pattern) bounded low low over-restrictive → missed proof
Tight + loop-guarded bounded low stable best; tune per axiom

Bench records (check_time, result, est. instantiations via solver stats) for each strategy; regression gate: a fixture that flips Unsat→Unknown after a trigger change is a perf+completeness regression — block. Target (§4): reference quantifier VC (≤5 quantifiers, array+fn axioms) solves <200 ms p50, <1 s p99 with tight triggers; matching-loop fixture must terminate under the per-method timeout (default 10 s, configurable).

31.5 Profiling — perf / flamegraph

Tools: cargo flamegraph (perf-based, Linux) and perf record/perf report; samply as cross-platform fallback. Attribution workflow:

  1. Run macro-bench on a large annotated program (e.g. sorted-array binary search + loop invariants + frame).
  2. Flamegraph → expect a wide SmtSolver::check frame (external solver or FFI). If a spv-* frame is wide instead, that's the bug to chase.
  3. If solver dominates → tune triggers / VC size / incremental reuse (§31.3–4, §31.6), not Rust code.
  4. If wp/passify/encode dominate → check for accidental Q duplication (DSA regression), VcFormula deep-clone, or per-term String allocation in SMT-LIB emission.

Counters tracked alongside time: VcFormula node count, SmtTerm count, distinct quantifiers, trigger count, solver instantiations (from solver stats), bytes emitted to SMT-LIB. Rule: every perf claim cites a flamegraph + a counter delta, never intuition.

31.6 Incremental solving (push/pop) benefit

Phase 10 mandates per-method push/pop incremental solving: shared axioms/datatype/array theory background asserted once (the base frame), each method's negated VC pushed/popped on top. Avoids re-parsing + re-asserting the (large) axiom set 200×.

Mode Axiom asserts Solver warm-up 200-method suite wall
Fresh solver per method 200× full 200× cold baseline (slow)
Single solver, push/pop VC 1× background + 200 push/pop 1× warm target: ≥2–5× faster

Bench suite_incremental vs suite_fresh; measure total wall + per-method p50/p95/p99. Target (§4): incremental ≥2× faster on the 200-method corpus; lemma/theory reuse keeps per-method overhead amortized. Pitfall: a leaked assertion across pop breaks soundness (a stale assumption proves a later method) — incremental correctness is checked by parity vs fresh-solver results in CI (must be identical Verified/Failed set), not just speed.

31.7 Allocator analysis — AST / IVL / VC IR

IR is immutable-after-construction, deeply nested (Box-linked enums: Expr, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm). Allocation hotspots and mitigations:

Concern Symptom Mitigation Target
Per-node Box heap allocs many small allocs in parse/desugar/wp arena (bumpalo) per pipeline run; or interned Atom symbols <1 alloc/node amortized
VcFormula deep clone in wp quadratic copy of post Q structural sharing (Rc/arena ref); DSA already removes the duplication no Q clone in seq rule
Atom string dup repeated identifier strings string interning → Atom = u32 id O(1) compare, dedup
SMT-LIB emission strings per-term format! churn single reused String buffer, write! into it linear in term count

Measurement: dhat/heaptrack heap profiling on the macro-bench; criterion allocs/iter via a counting allocator on micro-benches. Target (§4): peak RSS <200 MB verifying the 200-method reference corpus; per-method IR allocation linear in AST/IVL/VC node count (no quadratic copy). Regression gate: alloc count per wp call must not exceed c·nodes.

31.8 Tail latency on quantifier-heavy VCs

Quantifier instantiation makes solver time heavy-tailed: p50 cheap, p99 dominated by a few methods (array/Seq axioms, recursive pure functions with fuel, nested frames). Track distribution, not just mean.

  • Per-method timeout budget (default 10 s) bounds the tail; on timeout → SmtResult::Unknown → reported (never silently "Verified" — soundness).
  • Fuel on recursive pure-function defining axioms bounds unrolling depth → bounds instantiation blowup; fuel↑ trades completeness for tail latency. Bench sweeps fuel∈{1,2,3} per recursive-fn fixture, recording p99 + Unknown-rate.
  • Report metrics: p50 / p95 / p99 per-method solver time, Unknown rate, timeout count, max instantiations.

Targets (§4): reference corpus per-method solver p50 <100 ms, p95 <500 ms, p99 <2 s; whole 200-method suite <60 s wall incremental; Unknown rate 0 on the expected-PASS set (any nonzero is a completeness regression to triage). Tail offenders are logged with their trigger/fuel profile for targeted tuning, never by relaxing the timeout (which risks masking nontermination of MightNotTerminate checks).

31.9 Performance regression gates (CI)

Gate Fixture Assertion On fail
DSA linearity k=20 branches dsa_nodes ≤ 12·ivl_nodes block
wp non-exponential scaling N slope ≤ linear+ε block
Trigger stability quantifier corpus no Unsat→Unknown flip block
Incremental parity+speed 200-method identical results AND ≥2× vs fresh block
Peak RSS reference corpus <200 MB warn→block
Suite wall reference corpus <60 s; criterion no >10% regression warn

Criterion baselines stored (--save-baseline); CI compares vs main, flags >10% slowdown. All gates run in the same harness as the §25 validation suite so perf and soundness regressions surface together.

31.10 Performance Targets Registry (M-table)

Every numbered performance target stated above (and in §4 / Scalability Analysis) gets a stable M# id here for cross-referencing — restates, never invents, a number; each row names where it was derived.

ID Target Derived in CI Gate
M1 Cold workspace build ≤ 60 s (4-core CI runner) §12.4 A7 make ci build step
