Scalability Analysis

Scaling axes for spv: (1) per-method body size, (2) whole-program size, (3) quantifier/trigger complexity, (4) heap/array axiom count. Each axis hits a different stage; the dominant ceiling is SMT solver time driven by quantifier instantiation, not the front-end pipeline.

Cost model per stage

Stage Crate Dominant cost vs input Big-O target Notes
Lex + parse spv-syntax source bytes B O(B) single pass, recursive-descent; no backtracking blowup
Resolve + typecheck spv-ast AST nodes N O(N) (O(N·d) worst, d=scope depth) scope map lookups amortized O(1)
Desugar -> IvlCmd spv-ivl AST nodes N O(N) if->Choice, while->cut, call->3 cmds; constant fan-out per node
passify (DSA) spv-vc IvlCmd nodes K O(K) one version counter pass; merge points add O(vars) renames per Choice join
wp spv-vc passive IvlCmd nodes K O(K) terms (passive) vs O(2^branches) (naive) passive form is the whole reason wp stays tractable
VC simplify spv-vc VcFormula nodes F O(F) constant fold, Imp/And flatten
Encode -> SmtTerm + SMT-LIB spv-smt VcFormula nodes F O(F) linear term walk; trigger annotation O(quantifiers)
SmtSolver.check(NOT VC) spv-smt/#278/Z3 quantifiers Q, triggers, axioms A worst-case undecidable; practically super-linear in Q·A THE bottleneck

Everything left of check is linear-ish in program text; the cost concentrates in the solver. Engineering effort on scaling = keeping the VC small/decidable, not speeding the pipeline.

Per-method size (VC linear under passive form)

  • Naive wp(x := e, Q) = Q[e/x] duplicates Q; sequential assignments + Choice -> wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q) AND wp(c2,Q) -> VC size exponential in branch count (Flanagan-Saxe 2001). A method with n ifs -> 2^n paths.
  • passify (DSA) makes each variable assigned once (fresh versions); Assign becomes Assume(x_k == e); the duplicated-Q substitution vanishes. VC size becomes O(K) (linear-ish) in IvlCmd size, not O(2^branches).
  • Invariant: post-passify, VC node count F is O(K) with small constant; verify in Phase 8 acceptance (VC size on a 10-if method: linear, NOT 1024×).
  • Target: method of K ≈ 10^3 guarded commands -> VcFormula ≤ ~10^4 nodes -> SMT-LIB ≤ ~10^5 bytes.

Whole-program size (modular verification -> per-method + parallel)

  • Modular verification is the key whole-program scaler: a Call is verified against the callee contract (assert pre; havoc modifies; assume post), NEVER its body. So whole-program cost = Σ per-method cost, with no cross-method inlining/explosion. Program of M methods -> M independent VCs.
  • Cost is per-method-bounded: adding methods grows total linearly in M, never multiplicatively. A 10^4-method program is 10^4 independent O(method) jobs.
  • Embarrassingly parallel: each method's VC build + check is independent (shares only the read-only typed AST + axiom set). spv-verify driver -> thread pool (one method per task) or process fan-out for solver isolation.
    • Speedup ≈ min(cores, M); near-linear until solver-memory-bound. Target: 8× on 8 cores for ≥8 methods.
    • Per-method timeout budget (e.g. 10 s) caps tail latency; one hard VC cannot stall the batch.
    • Determinism: results merged by method order -> stable Vec<VError> regardless of completion order.
  • Incremental re-verify: only methods whose body OR whose callee contracts changed need re-running (contract = interface boundary). Cache key = (method AST hash, transitive contract hashes).

Quantifier / trigger complexity (the SMT bottleneck)

  • Quantifiers (Forall/Exists from source Quant, framing axioms, function defs) move the VC into undecidable FOL; the solver uses E-matching on triggers. This is where time/timeouts/Unknown live.
  • Matching loop = a trigger whose instantiation produces a term matching the same trigger -> unbounded instantiation -> blowup / timeout -> SmtResult::Unknown. The single worst scaling failure.
    • Cause: badly chosen Trigger (e.g. trigger f(x) on axiom f(x) == f(x+1)).
    • Mitigation: curate triggers in spv-smt (most-specific, loop-free); forbid trigger terms that re-derive themselves; cap instantiation rounds via solver option; surface Unknown explicitly (Phase 10) instead of hanging.
  • Cost grows roughly with (#quantifiers × #ground terms matching each trigger) per round, compounded over rounds. Deeply nested quantifiers / multi-pattern triggers multiply ground-term sets.
  • Mitigations: minimal trigger sets; prefer Map/select-store axioms with tight triggers; split quantifier-heavy methods; avoid Exists in hypotheses (skolemize early in DSA).

Heap / array axiom count

  • Heap (objects+fields as Map), Seq, arrays add axiom schemas (select/store, sequence length/index, datatype constructors). Each instantiated axiom is a quantifier the solver must manage.
  • Cost scales with A = #distinct heap/array operations in the VC (drives ground terms) × axiom triggers, NOT with heap size at runtime (verification is symbolic — no concrete heap).
  • Frame axioms (modifies framing) are O(modifies-set) per call; large modifies -> more framing terms. Mitigation: minimal modifies clauses; dynamic-frames region encoding to bound framing quantifiers.
  • User pure functions: uninterpreted symbol + defining axiom, recursion bounded by fuel (Phase 12) -> caps unfolding depth -> bounds instantiation; without fuel, recursive defs are a matching-loop source.

Dominant ceiling & mitigation summary

ceiling:  SMT check(NOT VC)  >>  encode  >  wp/passify  >  parse/typecheck
          (quantifier instantiation, matching loops)        (all linear-ish)
Bottleneck Symptom Mitigation
Branch explosion VC O(2^n), huge SMT-LIB passify / DSA (linear VC)
Matching loop timeout, Unknown loop-free triggers; instantiation cap; report Unknown
Deep arithmetic / nonlinear solver slow/Unknown keep arithmetic linear; helper lemmas; assert intermediate facts
Heap/array axiom flood many instantiations tight select/store triggers; minimal modifies; dynamic frames
Recursive functions unbounded unfolding fuel-bounded unfolding
One hard method stalls batch per-method timeout; parallel isolation; method splitting
  • Method splitting: a method too hard for the solver -> factor into smaller methods with explicit contracts (turns one giant VC into several small ones; modular boundary cuts the search). Recommended when single-method check exceeds timeout.

Memory scaling of the IR

  • All IR (Program AST, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm) is immutable-after-construction; Box/Vec trees, no cycles -> bytes ≈ O(nodes), freed when the per-method job ends.
  • Per-method IR is transient: build VC, emit SMT, drop -> only the read-only typed Program + axioms persist for the whole run; peak memory ≈ O(largest single method's IR) + O(program AST), NOT O(Σ all VCs).
  • Atom interning (string -> u32) keeps identifier memory flat across M methods. Target: per-method IR ≤ ~10 MB for K≈10^3; whole-program resident ≤ a few hundred MB at 10^4 methods (AST + intern table dominate).
  • Parallel workers each hold one method's IR -> memory scales with active threads, bounded by pool size, independent of M.

Mental Models & First Principles

Expert intuitions that compress the core design decisions of spv. Each is a predictive model — not a summary of what the code does, but a frame that lets an expert anticipate failures, pick the right abstraction, and read the architecture without being surprised. The resolving § identifies where spv applies the model.

MM1 — A program is a formula; "is it correct?" is "is this formula valid?"

The expert insight: the entire pipeline from source to SMT is a semantics-preserving translation, not a heuristic search. Every transformation (desugar → IVL, wp, DSA, SMT encoding) must preserve the set of satisfying assignments. An engineer who holds this model knows that a dropped assertion is not a "missed check" — it is a falsehood about the program's semantics introduced into the formula, and that the formula remains satisfiable over inputs that should be refuted.

What it predicts: any encoding bug (wrong BinOp polarity, mis-scoped old(), over-narrow Havoc) is not a diagnostic quality issue but a validity-preserving failure — it changes which programs are classified as "correct."

Bug class written without it: treating wp(assert p, Q) = p ⇒ Q (the assume rule) instead of p ∧ Q (the assert rule) — the two are logically equivalent when Q is assumed true, but the difference is precisely whether p is an obligation or a free assumption. Without MM1, this polarity flip looks like a small code typo; with MM1, it is a soundness catastrophe.

Resolving §: §8.7 (the locked five wp rules), §§19–20 (Phase 7–8 VCGen), §7 INV-2.

MM2 — Substitution duplicates; DSA eliminates; exponential is a structural property, not a tuning knob

The expert insight: naive wp(x:=e, Q) = Q[e/x] is not just slow — it is structurally exponential in the number of assignments across nondeterministic choices. The exponent is not in the number of paths executed; it is in the term size of the formula produced, which grows as 2^(number of Choice nodes) regardless of whether any of those paths are ever exercised. passify (DSA) changes the shape of the formula, not its meaning.

What it predicts: a method with 30 nested if statements produces a VC with 2^30 nodes under naive wp, which is ~10^9 — OOM regardless of solver speed, within the VCGen itself. With DSA, the same method produces a VC with ~60 nodes. The factor-of-10^7 difference is architectural, not a tuning choice.

Bug class written without it: "we'll optimize passify later" — the developer who does not hold MM2 may defer DSA to Phase 8 but accidentally wire naive wp in Phase 7, creating a verifier that works on trivial benchmarks and silently OOMs on real code.

Resolving §: §4 C5/C6, §6 P4, §8.8, Phase 8 (§20).

MM3 — The solver is an oracle; the soundness obligation lives entirely in the VC

The expert insight: the SMT solver is a black box that answers Sat/Unsat/Unknown. It is trusted: a genuine Unsat means the VC is valid. The entire soundness responsibility falls on producing the right VC — if the encoded negation ¬VC is satisfiable when the program is buggy, the solver will find the model. The solver cannot compensate for a dropped assertion or a mis-framed havoc.

What it predicts: a result of Unsat is only as trustworthy as the formula handed to the solver. A mis-encoded operator (e.g. wrong division semantics) makes ¬VC spuriously unsatisfiable — the solver returns Unsat and the verifier reports Verified for a program that is, in fact, buggy.

Bug class written without it: "the solver said Unsat, so the verification is correct" — trusting the solver output without auditing the encoding. MM3 tells the engineer to generate and inspect the SMT-LIB on every unexpected Verified result (the print-smt subcommand is specifically for this).

Resolving §: §3.6 (spv-smt trust boundary), §4 C8/C17, §6 P6/P11, Phase 9 (§21), Phase 10 (§22).

MM4 — Modular verification is a correctness-by-construction scaling argument, not an optimization

The expert insight: verifying a call against the callee's contract (not body) is not an approximation — it is the correct semantics for a language with procedural abstraction. The caller cannot depend on implementation details; if the callee's spec is too weak to prove the caller's postcondition, the spec must be strengthened. This is the verification analog of information-hiding: the module boundary is not an obstacle to overcome but a semantic tool.

What it predicts: verification of a program with N methods costs O(Σ per-method VC sizes), not O(product) — there is no cross-method VC explosion. Adding methods scales linearly in total work.

Bug class written without it: "let me inline the callee for a stronger fact" — the engineer who does not hold MM4 inlines a callee body into a caller's VC to get a fact the spec does not expose, producing an exponentially larger VC, breaking recursion, and coupling caller correctness to callee implementation — all the things Spec exists to prevent.

Resolving §: §6 P5, §7 INV-6, §8.6 call desugaring, Phase 5 (§17).

MM5 — A counterexample is a model of the negated VC mapped through the DSA inversion

The expert insight: when the solver returns Sat(Model), the model is a satisfying assignment for ¬VC — it witnesses why the VC is invalid. This model is expressed in terms of DSA-renamed variables (x_0, x_2, m3). The counterexample reconstruction step inverts this renaming to recover source variables. The Reason tag on the failing Assert picks the source location. The entire chain is a deterministic, invertible pipeline.

What it predicts: a counterexample that references x_7 = 3 is not a raw solver artifact — it is the value of source variable x after the seventh assignment in the method, recoverable by the DSA version map. A missing DSA inversion produces a model variable with no source preimage, which is a reconstruction bug (not a soundness bug, but a diagnostics failure).

Bug class written without it: treating the Model as opaque and displaying raw x_7 = 3 to the user, who then cannot relate it to their source program and loses trust in the tool's counterexamples.

Resolving §: §7 INV-10/INV-12, Phase 11 (§23), §11.7 (Counterexample type).

MM6 — Unknown is not a verdict; it is an incompleteness signal

The expert insight: SmtResult::Unknown means the solver gave up — it did not prove and did not disprove. This is neither Verified nor Failed. It arises from quantifier instantiation limits, matching loops, solver timeouts, and nonlinear arithmetic. The structural cause is that the full first-order theory (with quantifiers and the array/heap axioms) is undecidable — Unknown is expected, not a bug.

What it predicts: any code path that maps Unknown to Verified is the most dangerous soundness hole in the codebase — it confuses "I failed to disprove" with "I proved." The mapping Unsat⇒Verified / Sat⇒Failed / Unknown⇒Unknown must be a single match arm per case with no default coalescing.

Bug class written without it: a _ => Verified wildcard arm in the solver result match — a one-line bug that makes every verification task that times out report success.

Resolving §: §4 C8/C17, §6 P11, §7 INV-9, Phase 10 (§22), §3.6.

MM7 — Loop invariants are the user's claim; a failed inductive step is a counterexample to the claim, not the program

The expert insight: the invariant cut encodes the user's assertion about loop behavior as a formal obligation. When the inductive step fails — when the solver finds a state satisfying I ∧ guard but where the body does not re-establish I — it is producing a counterexample to the invariant, not necessarily to the program. The program may be correct; the invariant may just be too weak to capture the inductive property.

What it predicts: the most common usability failure in Dafny/Boogie tools is a user believing their program is wrong because the verifier rejects it, when in fact the invariant needs strengthening. The LoopInvariantNotMaintained error kind exists to make this distinction explicit.

Bug class written without it: classifying a LoopInvariantNotMaintained failure as PostconditionMightNotHold or a generic AssertionMightNotHold — the user does not know whether to fix the program or strengthen the invariant.

Resolving §: §6 P12 (Reason tagging), §7 INV-5/INV-12, §8.6 loop invariant cut, Phase 6 (§18).

Comparative Architecture Table

spv as a deductive verifier built around a Boogie-like IVL, compared to four real production systems in the static-analysis and formal-verification space.

Dimension spv (this blueprint) Boogie (Microsoft) Dafny (Microsoft/Amazon) Why3 (INRIA) CBMC (TU Delft/Diffblue)
Source language Custom annotated imperative (spv language) with Hoare specs Boogie 2 language (the IVL is itself the source) Dafny language (ML-like, object-oriented, functional) WhyML (functional+imperative, multi-theory) C/C++ with ANSI/MISRA contracts (no annotation required)
IVL/core representation IvlCmd guarded commands (6 constructors, Seq/Choice/Assume/Assert/Havoc/Assign) Boogie 2 is its own IVL — the reference model this blueprint clones Lowers to Boogie 2 as its backend Transforms to an internal "Why" logic IR; many backends CFG of GOTO programs over the SSA/bitvector level
VC direction Backward wp over passive-form IVL (Flanagan-Saxe DSA) Backward wp over passive form — the canonical implementation of Flanagan-Saxe Backward wp via Boogie (all verification goes through Boogie) Forward sp and backward wp depending on transformation chosen Bounded symbolic execution (forward SSA-based)
Loop handling Invariant cut: user writes invariant/decreases; unbounded verification Invariant cut; user-provided invariant; optional modifies Same as Boogie (desugars decreases to Boogie termination check) User-provided loop variants; multiple refinement strategies Bounded unrolling (k iterations); optionally k-induction
Heap/memory model Map-based dynamic frames; modifies clauses Map-based heap + permission-checking (where clauses) Dynamic frames / reads clauses with ownership tracking Arbitrary algebraic model + SMT theories Concrete bitvector memory model (byte-accurate, no logic aliasing)
Solver backend Pluggable via SmtSolver trait + SMT-LIB 2; primary: Z3 or #278 Hard-wired to Z3 (via Boogie's ProverInterface) Hard-wired to Z3 via Boogie Multi-prover: Alt-Ergo, CVC5, Z3, Coq, Isabelle CaDiCaL / MiniSat SAT solver + bitvector theories; no SMT-LIB abstraction
Proof obligation taxonomy 8 VErrorKinds tagged at desugar time via Reason Error labels attached to axiom names; no structured kind enum Dafny error messages map to Boogie labels; richer error taxonomy Proof obligations identified by transformation name Reachability / assertion violation / overflow / null-deref (property-driven)
Counterexample model DSA-inverted Model → source Atom → Counterexample{bindings,at} Boogie model (DSA names); external BoogieX86Emulator for CE Model mapped to Dafny source expressions; IDE integration Back-annotation per back-end (Alt-Ergo gives none; Z3 gives partial) Concrete C witness trace (executable test input); CBMC's "goto-trace"
Scope: soundness vs bug-finding Soundness cardinal — Unknown explicit, never Verified Soundness-focused (Boogie's stated goal: prove correctness) Soundness-focused with user annotation Soundness via deductive verification; incompleteness surfaced Bug-finding primary: bounded unsound for large programs; optional full k-induction
Annotation burden User writes requires/ensures/invariants/decreases on every method and loop Same — Boogie is the target after annotation by a front-end Dafny infers some postconditions; user writes most invariants WhyML annotations close to Boogie level Optional: CBMC can verify unannotated C with bounded model checking

Boogie is the direct architectural ancestor of spv-ivl / spv-vc. Its central decision — a reusable IVL that any annotated language front-end can target, with wp+DSA and Z3 as the backend — is precisely the Boogie-2 paper's contribution. The one decision Boogie makes differently is hard-wiring Z3: Boogie's ProverInterface does have a plugin point, but in practice it is Z3-only (the CVC5/Simplify paths are unmaintained). This is the right call for a production tool that ships inside Visual Studio, but it means swapping to a different solver requires forking Boogie. spv fixes this by making solver pluggability a first-class contract via the SmtSolver trait.

Dafny adds a high-productivity surface language above Boogie and owns the user experience: the IDE integration, the incremental verification, the richer ADT/collection types, the fuel-based recursive functions, the reads/modifies frame inference. Dafny's decisive advantage over spv is the Boogie back-end: by compiling to Boogie rather than reimplementing wp, Dafny benefits from 15 years of Boogie robustness. Dafny wins decisively in the regime where a user-facing tool with IDE polish is the goal. spv exists to expose the internals that Dafny hides, building the pipeline from scratch as the educational objective.

Why3 makes the orthogonal choice of multi-backend: it supports Alt-Ergo, CVC5, Z3, Coq, and Isabelle as proof backends over a common logic layer. Why3 wins decisively when the target theorem requires interactive proof steps (the Coq/Isabelle backends) or when an arithmetic-heavy proof needs CVC5's nonlinear arithmetic. The cost is that Why3's driver layer is more complex than spv's single-trait approach, and counterexample quality varies heavily across backends (Alt-Ergo produces no models; Z3 produces partial ones). spv's single SmtSolver trait is a deliberate simplification: one solver, consistent model semantics, reproducible differential testing.

CBMC occupies a fundamentally different point in the design space: it is a bounded model checker targeting unannotated C/C++, verifying properties like buffer overflows and integer overflows by symbolically executing a program up to a bound on loop iterations and array sizes. CBMC wins for security-focused analysis of legacy C code with no annotations and a team that cannot afford to write loop invariants. The tradeoff is incompleteness: CBMC's Verified means "no violation in at most k iterations," not "no violation ever." spv's deductive approach requires annotation but produces Verified that is unconditionally sound across all loop iterations.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Six derived numbers for the spv verifier, each step-by-step from the §4 System Constraints table. All arithmetic visible; no asserted-only numbers.

Number 1 — VC node count for a 1000-statement method (linear DSA guarantee)

From §4 C5: VC node count ≤ k · N where k ≤ 8 and N = IVL command count. An n-statement source method desugars to roughly 2n–4n IVL commands (each if → 2 Assume + 2 arms; each Call → 3 commands; straight-line ~ 1:1). For n = 1000 source statements:

N = 2000 (IVL commands, conservative)
VC nodes ≤ 8 × 2000 = 16,000 nodes
At ~48 bytes/node (VcFormula enum with Box): 16,000 × 48 ≈ 768 KB per method
Conclusion: well inside §4 C11 (128 MB ceiling); a 1000-statement method is cheap.

Without DSA (naive wp): N=20 nested ifs → 2^20 ≈ 10^6 nodes × 48 bytes = 48 MB for 20 statements — an OOM at 30 nested ifs. DSA is architecturally mandatory.

Number 2 — SMT-LIB text size for the 1000-statement method

From §4 C5 derivation above: 16,000 VcFormula nodes. Each node emits ~8–15 bytes of SMT-LIB text (parentheses + operator + variable name). Using 12 bytes/node as average:

SMT-LIB text ≈ 16,000 nodes × 12 bytes/node = 192 KB per method
At the §4 C7 budget of 1 check per method: one solver call reads ~200 KB of text input.
For 500 methods (§4 C2): 500 × 200 KB = 100 MB total SMT-LIB emitted in a full run.
Conclusion: well within process bandwidth; SMT-LIB I/O is not a bottleneck.

Number 3 — Peak RSS for the full 500-method program

From §4 C2 (max 500 methods), C12 (RSS ceiling 2 GB), C9/C10/C11 (per-method IR memory):

AST (program, read-only): 50k nodes × 1.3 kB/node (§4 C9) = 65 MB
Peak per-method IR (IVL + VC + SmtTerm): 96 + 128 = 224 MB (§4 C10+C11), but only ONE method live at a time per worker
Worker pool size: ≤ 8 (§4 C3 concurrency)
Active IR at peak: 8 workers × 224 MB = 1,792 MB
Plus AST: 65 MB
Total: ~1,857 MB
Margin to §4 C12 (2 GB): ~143 MB — tight but within budget.
Recommendation: tune worker pool to 6 on 16 GB machines; 8 workers require a 2 GB RSS ceiling.

Number 4 — Solver time budget for a 500-method suite

From §4 C3 (per-method p50 < 1 s, p95 < 10 s) and C2 (500 methods) with §4 C4 parallelism (8 workers):

Expected p50 solve time: 0.5 s/method × 500 methods / 8 workers = 31.25 s wall-clock
Expected p95 tail: 10 s × 500 methods / 8 workers = 625 s (worst case, all at p95)
Practical p95 (5% of 500 = 25 hard methods): 25 × 10 s / 8 workers = 31.25 s additional tail
Realistic total wall: ~31 s (median) + ~31 s (tail) = ~62 s
The §4 incremental push/pop target (≥2× speedup) brings this to ~31 s.
Budget conclusion: 500-method suite completes < 60 s wall on 8 cores with incremental solving.

Number 5 — Trigger matching overhead on a quantifier-heavy method

From §4 C18 (every Forall has ≥1 trigger) and §4 C7 (≤3 solver calls per method):

A heap-heavy method with 10 heap-field accesses: 10 × 2 axioms (select/store) = 20 quantifiers.
Each quantifier with 1 trigger: solver E-matches 20 patterns per instantiation round.
A matching loop would instantiate: unbounded. Fuel bounds recursive functions at depth F.
With F=3 fuel and 3 recursive pure functions: 3^3 = 27 ground terms per fuel expansion.
Total instantiation budget: 20 axiom quantifiers + 27 fuel terms = 47 E-match patterns.
At Z3's typical 10^5 instantiations/second: 47 patterns saturate in < 1 ms.
Pathological trigger loop (trigger re-derives itself): diverges — prevented by §21 trigger lint.

Number 6 — DSA version count for a 100-variable method with 50 assignments

From §8.8 and §4 C5 (each var assigned once per DSA path):

50 assignments over 100 variables: average 0.5 assignments per variable per method.
Maximum versions per variable: bounded by depth of nesting × assignments per path.
For 10 nested Choice nodes, 5 assignments per variable in worst case: 5 × 10 = 50 versions per variable.
At 100 variables: 100 × 50 = 5000 total version symbols emitted to SMT-LIB.
Each symbol is ~6 bytes (x_NN format): 5000 × 6 = 30 KB of symbol declarations.
Well within the SMT-LIB budget (Number 2 above: 192 KB total).
DSA join points (Choice merges): 10 × 100 variables = 1000 join Assume(=) terms.
Total Assume terms from DSA: 1000 terms × ~15 bytes/term = 15 KB additional SMT-LIB.
Conclusion: DSA overhead on SMT-LIB size is <10% of total for this method.

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

These 20 questions require synthesizing two or more sections. None is answerable by quoting a single line. The resolving § names where the answer lives.

HQ1 — A method with assert false at line 3 and assert false at line 7 is submitted to spv verify. The counterexample reports the failure at line 7. Is this a soundness bug? What property of the pipeline determines which failure is reported, and what invariant is at risk if the ordering is arbitrary? (Resolving §: §7 INV-10/INV-12, §8.6 desugaring, §6 P7)

HQ2 — The DSA pass renames x to x_0, x_1, x_2 across a method with two if branches. After solving, the model says x_2 = 5. The engineer reports x = 5 to the user. Explain, using the version map and the join-equality structure from §8.8, exactly how x_2 maps to a specific source assignment, and what additional information is needed to trace x_2 to the correct source line. (Resolving §: §8.8 passive form, §7 INV-10, Phase 11 §23)

HQ3 — A callee method has modifies x; in its spec but the body also modifies y. A caller asserts y == old(y) in its postcondition and the verifier reports Verified. Is this sound? Explain the exact IvlCmd sequence emitted for the call site, identify which IVL invariant would catch this case, and state what additional verification obligation would fire. (Resolving §: §6 P5/P15, §7 INV-6/INV-7, §8.6 call desugaring, Phase 5 §17)

HQ4 — A user adds fuel = 3 to a recursive pure function f(n). The method calling f times out with Unknown. Explain the mechanism by which fuel bounds instantiation, why Unknown (not Failed) is the correct output, and what two changes — one to the annotation and one to the trigger — could resolve the timeout without raising fuel. (Resolving §: §6 P10/P11, §4 C18, §8.9 pure functions, Phase 12 §24)

HQ5 — The passive-form pass produces a correct DSA IVL. The wp step is run on the non-passive IVL by mistake (a bug introduced in the driver). A 10-branch method now takes 30 seconds to verify. Is the result sound? Why might the result still be correct (same verdict) even though the VCGen is incorrect (formula exponentially large)? What CI gate catches this before it ships? (Resolving §: §4 C5/C6, §6 P4, §7 INV-3, Phase 8 §20)

HQ6 — A method M verifies successfully. The engineer changes the body of a callee K (adding an extra assignment to a field not in K's modifies clause) but does not change K's spec. Does M need to be re-verified? If no, why not? If a field outside K's modifies was actually modified, what verification failure would surface, at which method, and with which VErrorKind? (Resolving §: §6 P5, §7 INV-6/INV-7, §3.7 spv-verify modular contract)

HQ7 — A verifier engineer replaces wp(assert p, Q) = And([p, Q]) with wp(assert p, Q) = Imp(p, Q) by mistake. Name four distinct expected-FAIL fixtures from the §25 conformance suite that would become unexpectedly Verified as a result. Explain the logical reason for each. (Resolving §: §7 INV-2, §6 P1/P3, §8.7, Phase 13 §25)

HQ8 — The loop invariant for a counting loop while (i < n) invariant i >= 0 && i <= n; is submitted. The inductive step fails. The counterexample shows i = n, body runs, i = n+1, invariant (i <= n) fails. Is this a bug in the program or in the invariant? What would a correct but complete invariant look like, and what IVL shape guarantees the step checker sees the precise failing condition? (Resolving §: §7 INV-5, §8.6 loop invariant cut, §6 P12/MM7)

HQ9 — spv-smt emits (declare-fun f (Int) Int) for a pure function f and a defining axiom. The axiom uses trigger {f(x)}. The solver returns Unsat for a method calling f. The engineer then removes the trigger (makes it {}). Now the solver returns Unknown. Explain the mechanism: what changed in the solver's behavior, and why Unknown here does not compromise soundness even though it compromises completeness. (Resolving §: §4 C18, §6 P10/P11, §7 INV-9, §8.9)

HQ10 — A program contains var x: int; x := 5 / (y - y);. The type checker accepts this. Which phase emits the DivisionByZero proof obligation? As a VcFormula atom or as an IvlCmd? What is the exact IvlCmd sequence produced by §8.6 for the expression 5 / (y - y), and what Reason.kind does the generated assert carry? (Resolving §: §8.3 typing, §8.6 desugaring, Phase 3 §15 Pred::DivOk, Phase 4 §16)

HQ11 — Two engineers both add methods to the same source.spv file on separate branches. Engineer A's method passes all tests. Engineer B's method introduces an inconsistent axiom (axiom false;). After merge, spv verify reports all methods as Verified. Explain the soundness failure, identify which section's constraint was violated, and describe the CI gate that should detect an inconsistent axiom set. (Resolving §: §8.9 Axiom semantics, §4 C17, Phase 12 §24 axiom consistency check)

HQ12 — The print-smt subcommand is run on a method and the engineer inspects the output. They observe a quantifier with :pattern ((select heap_0 r_3)) where r_3 is a DSA-renamed variable. What does this pattern tell the solver? Why is r_3 used instead of the source name r? If the trigger were instead ((select heap_0 r)) (the source name), would the solver instantiate more or fewer times, and why? (Resolving §: §8.8 DSA, §8.9 triggers, §6 P10, Phase 9 §21)

HQ13 — The SmtSolver trait has &mut self in check. Why is mutability required? If two methods are verified in parallel, what is the concurrency contract: can two threads share one SmtSolver instance? What data structure in §11 enforces this, and what safety issue would arise from sharing a single incremental session across parallel method-verification tasks? (Resolving §: §4 C13, §11.1 concurrency conventions, §11.6 SmtSolver, Phase 10 §22)

HQ14 — A method's postcondition ensures result == old(heap[r.field]); fails with PostconditionMightNotHold. The engineer examines the counterexample and finds heap_0 = some_map and r_2 = some_ref. Explain the two-state encoding: what variable represents the pre-state heap, what represents r at method entry, and how the lowering in §8 ensures heap_0 is the pre-state rather than the post-state snapshot. (Resolving §: §7 INV-8, §8.6 two-state, Phase 3 §15 old/twostate, Phase 7 §19)

HQ15 — A staff engineer claims the passify step is strictly optional: "we can just let the solver handle large VCs with let-bindings." Evaluate this claim using the §4 C5 complexity argument, the §31.7 allocator analysis, and the specific measurement from Phase 8 (§20): what numerical bound does the acceptance criterion assert, and what would happen to a 30-nested-if method if passify were bypassed? (Resolving §: §4 C5/C6, §6 P4, §7 INV-3, Phase 8 §20, §31.7)

HQ16 — A new VErrorKind variant ArrayOutOfBounds needs to be added. List all the files and sections that must be updated, in order, to ensure: (1) the desugar step emits the obligation with the correct Reason, (2) the conformance suite gains a new expected-FAIL bucket, (3) the VErrorKind is reachable from at least one lowering site (§7 INV-12), and (4) the ADR table is not violated. (Resolving §: §7 INV-12, §11.7, Phase 4 §16, Phase 13 §25, ADR-17)

HQ17 — The soundness suite has 200 expected-FAIL programs. A mutation testing run (cargo-mutants) on spv-ivl/src/desugar.rs produces 15 mutants that delete one Assert each. Only 12 are killed by the soundness suite. What does it mean that 3 survived? What procedure from §25 (Phase 13) is invoked to address surviving mutants, and what property of the conformance fixtures guarantees every dropped obligation is eventually caught? (Resolving §: §7 INV-4, Phase 13 §25, §29.4 soundness suite)

HQ18 — A user annotates a method with decreases n - i where n and i are both loop-assigned. The verifier reports MightNotTerminate. The user insists the loop terminates because n is not modified in the body. Identify the IVL obligation that fires, explain which subset of the invariant cut generates the MightNotTerminate assert (§8.6), and describe the condition under which n would need to appear in the loop's modifies clause even though the user did not modify it. (Resolving §: §7 INV-5, §8.6 loop cut decreases, Phase 6 §18)

HQ19 — The Architectural Overview (§3) shows the concurrency boundary: "per-method verification is independent and parallelizable." Describe the three specific properties that make this true at the type level in §11, why a HashMap in the DSA join-map could violate the determinism invariant (§4 C13), and how the output ordering guarantee is maintained when method workers complete out of order. (Resolving §: §3.9, §4 C13, §11.1, §7 INV-11)

HQ20 — After 6 months of operation, a user reports that method BatchProcessor.process verifies but a concrete test input finds a bug. The engineer needs to determine whether this is a soundness failure or a specification gap. Describe the triage procedure: which print-* subcommand to run first, what property of the SMT-LIB output to check, how to determine whether the missing behavior is an unmodeled pre-state (spec gap) or a mis-encoded operator (soundness bug), and what the differential oracle (§29.6) would confirm. (Resolving §: §3, §6 P1, §7 INV-1, Phase 13 §25, §29.4/29.6)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Alt-1 — Bounded Model Checker (CBMC-style bitvector symbolic execution)

Source (C/C++ or spv)
        |
        v
   CFG extraction
        |
        v
   SSA (bitvector)        <-- word-width integers, not mathematical Int
        |
        v
   Bounded unrolling      <-- k iterations, explicit bound parameter
   (k=5 default)
        |
        v
   CNF/SAT/bitvector SMT  <-- DPLL(T) on bit-level theory
        |
        v
   SAT -> concrete witness trace (executable)
   UNSAT -> "no bug in <= k iterations"

Regime where it wins: security-focused analysis of unannotated C/C++ firmware where (a) the programmer cannot afford to write loop invariants, (b) the loop bounds are finite and small (hardware protocol handlers, packet parsers), (c) finding one concrete exploit in ≤k iterations is the goal, and (d) soundness across all iterations is neither required nor achievable. CBMC, KLEE, and S2E operate in this regime.

Real system: CBMC (TU Delft / Diffblue; Kroening & Tautschnig, TACAS 2004). Used at Amazon (s2n-bignum), OpenSSL, and embedded automotive code.

Why rejected: CBMC is unsound for unbounded loops (claims Unsat only up to k); this blueprint is a deductive verifier where soundness is the cardinal property (§6 P1, §4 C17). Additionally, bitvector arithmetic requires a different source language (machine integers with overflow semantics), a different heap model (byte-accurate), and a different solver theory (QF_BV) — incompatible with the mathematical-integer Int model that makes wp's loop invariant cut tractable (unbounded loops become finite-formula obligations only with a user-provided invariant).

Alt-2 — Interactive Theorem Prover (Coq/Lean-style tactic-driven proof)

Source (Coq/Lean term)
        |
        v
   Type theory kernel     <-- calculus of constructions / dependent types
        |
        v
   Tactic elaboration     <-- user writes `intro/apply/rewrite/omega/ring`
        |                     each step produces a proof term
        v
   Kernel type-checker    <-- trusted, tiny, decidable
        |
        v
   Proof term = verified  (no SMT call)

Regime where it wins: mathematical and cryptographic proofs (e.g. proving the correctness of elliptic curve point addition), compiler correctness proofs (CompCert), OS kernel proofs (seL4), and any result that requires manipulation of infinite-domain mathematics, dependent types, or generic algebraic structures that first-order SMT cannot handle. The explicit proof term is the certificate; no trusted oracle (solver) is required.

Real system: Coq (INRIA; Bertot & Castéran, 2004), Lean 4 (Microsoft Research; de Moura et al., 2021), Isabelle/HOL (University of Cambridge/TU Munich). Used for seL4 (NICTA), CompCert (INRIA), Fiat Cryptography (MIT).

Why rejected: interactive proving requires manual tactic steps for every proof obligation — automation is through specialized tactic languages (omega, decide, ring), not a push-button SMT call. This makes the tool unusable for the "annotate an imperative program and verify it automatically" use case that motivates spv. The wp/SMT pipeline produces Verified or Failed without user intervention on the proof; an interactive prover would require a proof script for each method that scales with proof complexity, not programmer annotation complexity.

Alt-3 — Abstract Interpretation (Astree/Polyspace-style fixed-point analysis)

Source
   |
   v
CFG extraction
   |
   v
Abstract domain lattice
(interval, octagons, polyhedra...)
   |
   v
Transfer functions
per basic block        <-- abstract semantics of each instruction
   |
   v
Widening at loop heads <-- forces fixpoint convergence in finite steps
   |
   v
Join at merge points
   |
   v
Final abstract state   ->  sound over-approximation

Regime where it wins: automatic, annotation-free analysis of safety properties (variable ranges, potential overflows, null-pointer dereferences, divide-by-zero) in production code without any user annotations. The analysis is sound-by-construction (the abstract domain over-approximates all concrete behaviors) and terminates automatically without requiring loop invariants from the user. Astrée (used for Airbus A380 flight software) found zero false alarms on the full fly-by-wire codebase.

Real system: Astrée (ENS Paris / AbsInt; Cousot et al., ESOP 2005), Polyspace (MathWorks), Clang's static analyzer (a flow-sensitive but unsound checker), Frama-C/EVA (CEA LIST).

Why rejected: abstract interpretation over a fixed abstract domain (intervals, octagons) is provably incomplete for the property class spv targets — it cannot prove that a sorting algorithm produces a sorted output, verify a contract ensures result == a + b precisely, or handle user-specified first-order postconditions. The domain determines what is provable; widening loses information at loop heads in ways that produce false alarms (sound but imprecise). spv trades the annotation burden (user writes invariants) for precision: when spv says Verified, the postcondition holds exactly, not over an abstract over-approximation. The two tools are complementary, not competing.

Alt-4 — Symbolic Execution Engine (KLEE/Java Pathfinder-style)

Source
   |
   v
Interpreter (concrete + symbolic state)
   |
   v
Fork at each branch
(both true/false branches)    <-- path explosion
   |
   v
Path condition updated
at each branch point
   |
   v
SMT query per path           <-- "is this path feasible?"
   |
   v
If feasible: concrete input  -> test case generation
If assertion fails: counterexample

Regime where it wins: test generation for programs without annotations, finding concrete inputs that exercise specific paths and trigger assertion failures, and coverage-guided fuzzing augmentation. KLEE automatically generates test suites achieving high branch coverage; Java PathFinder finds concurrency bugs via systematic state exploration. Symbolic execution wins when the goal is finding bugs by generating concrete inputs, not proving all executions are correct.

Real system: KLEE (Stanford; Cadar et al., OSDI 2008), Java PathFinder (NASA; Visser et al., 2003), angr (UC Santa Barbara). Used widely in security research (binary analysis, exploit generation).

Why rejected: symbolic execution has two fundamental limitations for spv's goal: (1) path explosion — the number of paths grows exponentially in branch count, making it infeasible for long methods with many loops (the same exponential problem that passify solves at the formula level); (2) loop incompleteness — symbolic execution unrolls loops up to a bound and cannot prove properties for all iterations without user-provided invariants, reverting to the CBMC limitation. The deductive approach (wp + invariants) handles unbounded loops in finite time by requiring the user to annotate the invariant — a tradeoff in automation for completeness over all paths.

Deferred Features Register

Every PhaseDeferred(...) anchor used in this blueprint resolves to a row below. Features deferred out of the initial 13-phase scope; the "un-defer trigger" column states the condition under which the work is picked up. No PhaseDeferred reference in this document may be absent from this table.

Feature Anchor §N.s Building Phase Un-defer Trigger
Separation logic / dynamic frames beyond Map heap (full BI-logic, fractional permissions, rely-guarantee) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24.7") Post-Phase 12 (after heap/Map is complete) A client project requires concurrent program verification or needs frame inference that dynamic-frames cannot provide; explicit design phase needed
Concurrency / rely-guarantee / thread-local reasoning PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 24.8") Post-Phase 12 Demand for concurrent program correctness beyond single-threaded spv; requires a new concurrency-aware IVL and a concurrent semantics layer
Generics / polymorphic methods (type-parameterized method Sort<T>) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 14.9") Post-Phase 2 Front-end language generics added; requires polymorphic type-checking in spv-ast, instantiation in IVL lowering, and monomorphization or sort quantification in SMT
Bitvectors, reals, string sorts (machine-word arithmetic, floating-point specs) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 21.8") Post-Phase 9 Client code with machine-integer overflow semantics or floating-point contracts; requires a new SmtSort::BitVec(width) + QF_BV encoding layer
Counterexample minimization / model-driven invariant inference (ICE learning, CEGIS) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 23.9") Post-Phase 11 Users repeatedly struggle with weak invariants; triggers a CEGIS loop over the counterexample stream — requires a loop-learning oracle on top of the existing Sat(Model) pipeline
Proof-certificate / unsat-core export (DRUP/LRAT proofs for trusted external checking) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 22.9") Post-Phase 10 Certified verification required (safety-critical deployment); solver must support get-unsat-core; adds a proof-certificate reconstruction pass after Unsat
IDE/LSP integration, error-localization UI, incremental per-save verification PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT section 25.10") Post-Phase 13 Developer productivity investment after full correctness is established; requires a Language Server Protocol wrapper, incremental re-verification cache, and a VSCode/Emacs plugin

Glossary

Terms are defined as used in this verifier (project 282), not in general. Cross-references in bold. SMT-LIB / Boogie / Dafny / Why3 senses noted only where they diverge.

Term Definition (implementation-relevant)
Hoare triple {P} c {Q} Command c, started in any state satisfying precond P, ends — if it terminates — in a state satisfying Q. Partial-correctness reading (termination separate). The verifier never builds triples directly; it computes wp and discharges.
Precondition Spec.requires: Vec<Expr> (each Bool). Assumed at method entry (Assume), asserted at each call site against the callee's requires. Caller's burden.
Postcondition Spec.ensures: Vec<Expr> (each Bool). Asserted at method exit (Assert, Reason kind PostconditionMightNotHold); assumed after a call against the callee's ensures. The only place old() is legal.
Loop invariant Stmt::While.invariants: Vec<Expr>. A property holding before every iteration. Cuts the loop (Phase 6 / ## 18): proved on entry, preserved by one arbitrary iteration, and with !guard implies the post. Weak/wrong invariant ⇒ inductive Assert fails — a counterexample to the user's invariant, the classic usability pitfall.
Weakest precondition (wp) wp(c: &IvlCmd, post: VcFormula) -> VcFormula. The weakest P with {P} c {Q} valid. Five locked rules: wp(assume p,Q)=p⇒Q; wp(assert p,Q)=p∧Q; wp(havoc x,Q)=∀x.Q; wp(x:=e,Q)=Q[e/x]; wp(c1;c2,Q)=wp(c1,wp(c2,Q)); wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q)∧wp(c2,Q). Method VC = wp(desugar_method(m), True).
Verification condition (VC) VcFormula (first-order logic) produced by wp. Program correct iff VC valid iff ¬VC unsat in SMT. The central artifact; print-vc emits it.
VCGen (verification-condition generator) The spv-vc machinery (wp + passify + simplify) turning an IvlCmd into a VcFormula. Its cardinal duty is soundness: never produce a valid VC for a buggy program.
Guarded command Dijkstra-style primitive command of the IVL. Here the six IvlCmd variants. The verification semantics is defined solely on these, not on source Stmt.
Intermediate verification language (IVL) The Boogie-like guarded-command language in spv-ivl. Source AST is desugared into it (desugar_method) so wp is defined over a tiny core, not the rich surface syntax. print-ivl emits it.
IvlCmd enum IvlCmd { Assume(Expr), Assert(Expr, Reason), Havoc(Vec<Var>), Assign(Var, Expr), Seq(Vec<IvlCmd>), Choice(Box<IvlCmd>, Box<IvlCmd>) }. The complete IVL. Immutable after desugaring.
assume Assume(p) Constrains execution to states where p holds; downstream may rely on p. No proof obligation. wp(assume p,Q)=p⇒Q. Used for entry requires, post-call ensures, loop-entry inv, dead-path assume false.
assert Assert(p, Reason) Emits a proof obligation p must hold here; carries a Reason for error mapping. wp(assert p,Q)=p∧Q. Failure ⇒ a VError.
havoc Havoc(xs) Assigns arbitrary (nondeterministic) values to vars xs, modelling unknown state. wp(havoc x,Q)=∀x.Q. Used for the modifies set after a call and for loop targets at the invariant cut. Mis-framed havoc (too few vars) ⇒ unsound; too many ⇒ incomplete.
Nondeterministic choice Choice(a, b) Executes a or b; both paths must satisfy the post. wp(c1[]c2,Q)=wp(c1,Q)∧wp(c2,Q). if g {s1} else {s2} desugars to Choice(assume g; s1 , assume ¬g; s2).
Passive form / dynamic single assignment (DSA) passify(c: &IvlCmd) -> IvlCmd: rewrite so each Var is assigned at most once (fresh versions per write; Assign ⇒ Assume(x_k == e)); Choice arms reconciled. Prevents the exponential Q[e/x] duplication of naive wp (Flanagan–Saxe 2001) ⇒ VC size near-linear in program size. Names must be invertible for counterexample mapping.
Modular verification Each method verified once against its own contract; every call verified against the callee's contract, never its body (assert callee requires; havoc modifies; assume callee ensures). Makes verification scale and compose.
Frame / modifies clause Spec.modifies: Vec<Expr> — the state a method may change. Frame property: everything outside the modifies set is unchanged. Lowered as Havoc(modifies) after a call plus framing assumes for unmodified locations. VErrorKind::ModifiesViolation when a method writes outside its frame.
decreases / variant Spec.decreases / Stmt::While.decreases: a well-founded measure that strictly decreases each iteration / recursive call, bounded below. Gives total correctness (termination). Failure ⇒ VErrorKind::MightNotTerminate.
Partial correctness {P} c {Q} ignoring termination: if c halts, Q holds. Default obligation.
Total correctness Partial correctness plus termination, discharged via decreases.
old() / two-state expression Expr::Old(e): value of e in the pre state. Legal only inside ensures (TypeError::OldOutsideEnsures otherwise). Encoding keeps a snapshot of pre-state variables/heap; a postcondition is a two-state predicate over pre and post.
SMT (satisfiability modulo theories) First-order satisfiability over background theories (here: linear int arithmetic, Bool, uninterpreted functions, Array/Map). The discharge backend behind the SmtSolver trait.
Satisfiability A formula is sat if some model makes it true. The verifier asks check(¬VC): Sat ⇒ a bug witness.
Validity A formula true in all models. VC valid ⇔ program meets spec ⇔ ¬VC unsat. Not asked directly — reduced to unsat of the negation.
Model struct Model — a satisfying assignment (var → value) returned with SmtResult::Sat. Source of the counterexample; DSA/skolem names must map back to source vars.
Counterexample struct Counterexample { bindings: Vec<(Atom, Value)>, at: Span } — reconstructed (Phase 11 / ## 23) from a Model: the failing program point (at) and concrete source-variable values.
Trigger Vec<Trigger> on a Quant/Forall — syntactic pattern telling the solver when to instantiate a quantifier (Phase 9 / ## 21). Bad triggers ⇒ non-termination (matching loop) or Unknown (no instantiation). Manually annotated on encoding.
Uninterpreted function A function symbol with a declared SmtSort signature but no definition (SmtSort::Uninterp(Atom)); constrained only by axioms. Pure functions are encoded as uninterpreted + defining axioms.
Axiom Decl::Axiom(Expr) — a globally-assumed formula (e.g. a pure function's defining equation, Map/Seq theory). Asserted to the solver unconditionally. Unsound if false: an inconsistent axiom set lets everything "verify".
fuel A bound on recursive unfolding of a pure function's defining axiom (well-founded recursion). Limits quantifier instantiation depth; too little ⇒ incompleteness, too much ⇒ blow-up.
Soundness The cardinal property: the VCGen must never report Verified for a buggy program. A dropped assert, mis-framed havoc, or mis-encoded operator ⇒ false "verified" ⇒ gravest failure. Soundness gate = Phase 13 (## 25).
Completeness Verifying every correct program. Secondary, bounded by SMT (Unknown, timeouts, quantifiers, fuel). Tracked separately from soundness; SmtResult::Unknown is surfaced, never silently treated as Verified.
Reason struct Reason { kind: VErrorKind, span: Span } — attached to every Assert recording why the obligation exists and where, so a failing assert maps to the right VError/counterexample.
VErrorKind enum { AssertionMightNotHold, PostconditionMightNotHold, PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold, LoopInvariantOnEntry, LoopInvariantNotMaintained, DivisionByZero, ModifiesViolation, MightNotTerminate } — classifies a verification failure for diagnostics.
SmtSolver trait SmtSolver { fn check(&mut self, assertions: &[SmtTerm]) -> SmtResult; } — pluggable backend boundary. Implementations wrap #278 or Z3 via SMT-LIB; supports per-method push/pop incremental solving (Phase 10 / ## 22).
SmtResult enum { Unsat, Sat(Model), Unknown } — outcome of check(¬VC). Unsat ⇒ VerifyResult::Verified; Sat ⇒ Failed + counterexample; Unknown ⇒ reported explicitly (timeout/incompleteness).

Core type names (quick index)

Type / fn Crate Role
Program, Decl, Method, Function, Spec spv-syntax/spv-ast surface program + contracts
Stmt, Expr, Type, Param, LValue, Block spv-syntax AST nodes
Value, Atom, Span, Trigger, Quantifier, BinOp, UnOp spv-syntax leaf data
ParseError / ParseErrorKind { UnexpectedToken, BadSpec, BadType } spv-syntax front-end errors
TypeError { Undeclared, Mismatch, NonBoolSpec, OldOutsideEnsures, BadModifies, ArityMismatch } spv-ast type/spec errors
IvlCmd, Reason, Var, desugar_method spv-ivl IVL + desugaring
VcFormula, Pred, wp, passify spv-vc VC generation + DSA
SmtSort { Int, Bool, Uninterp, Array }, SmtTerm, SmtSolver, SmtResult, Model spv-smt SMT encoding
VerifyResult { Verified, Failed }, VError, VErrorKind, Counterexample spv-verify driver + diagnostics

Tool / artifact terms

Term Definition
spv The CLI binary (spv-cli). Subcommands: verify, print-ast, print-ivl, print-vc, print-smt.
source.spv An annotated imperative program: methods with requires/ensures/modifies/decreases, locals, assignment, if/while, calls, assert/assume, pure functions, quantifiers, old().
invariant cut The loop encoding (Phase 6): assert(inv) on entry; havoc(targets); assume(inv); then [assume guard; body; assert(inv); assume false] for the inductive step; exit assumes !guard. Loops are never unrolled.
differential testing Phase 13 cross-check: run the same source.spv corpus through this verifier and a Boogie/Dafny subset; disagreements (esp. a false "verified") are soundness bugs.

Appendix A — File Inventory

Every leaf file from §10 Repository Layout, with a one-line purpose. Paths relative to workspace root. Crate dependency order: spv-syntax → spv-ast → spv-ivl → spv-vc → spv-smt → spv-verify → spv-cli. no_std + alloc for the five core crates (spv-syntax…spv-smt); std for spv-verify, spv-cli, benches, fuzz, tests. One assertion → one Reason{kind,span} → one VError; soundness is the cardinal invariant referenced throughout.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Single-table view of Phases 0–13 (doc headings ## 12–## 25). Durations sum to the Section 1 timeline (28 weeks, single owner: Mujir Qureshi). Each row: the load-bearing deliverable, calendar weeks, the dominant failure modes, and a measurable exit gate. "Exit Criteria" are pass/fail and CI-checkable; a phase is not closed until its gate is green.

Phase Heading Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria (measurable)
0 — Skeleton ## 12 Workspace + 7 spv-* crate shells; Program/IvlCmd/VcFormula/SmtTerm/Type type shells; ParseError/TypeError/VErrorKind; CI + Makefile + Dockerfile; end-to-end smoke path (one hard-coded method). wk 0–1 (1.5 wk) Crate boundaries leak (cyclic dep spv-vc↔spv-ivl); over-design IRs before semantics known; CI flakiness on SMT backend absence. cargo build --workspace + cargo test green in CI & Docker; spv verify on method M(){assert true} → Verified; on assert false → exactly one VError{kind:AssertionMightNotHold}; 7 crates, dep DAG acyclic (cargo-deny/cargo tree checked).
1 — Source front end ## 13 Lexer+Token; recursive-descent Parser → Program AST: methods, VarDecl/Assign/If/While/Call/Assert/Assume/Return, expr grammar, spec clauses requires/ensures/modifies/decreases, Old, Quant+Trigger. no_std+alloc. wk 1.5–4 (2.5 wk) Operator-precedence/associativity bugs; ambiguous old()/quantifier grammar; spec-clause parsing swallows body; poor span fidelity → bad later errors. Round-trip: parse→print-ast→reparse is idempotent on ≥40 fixtures; precedence table has property test (1k random exprs); every Token/AST node carries a correct Span (asserted); ParseErrorKind{UnexpectedToken,BadSpec,BadType} each fired by a fixture.
2 — Resolve + TypeChecker + spec WF ## 14 Name resolution; TypeChecker infer/check Type{Int,Bool,Ref,Map,Seq,Named}; specs must be Bool; old() only in ensures; modifies well-formed; arity checks; precise TypeError. wk 4–6.5 (2.5 wk) Scope/shadowing errors; silent coercion (Int↔Bool) → unsoundness downstream; old() legality missed; modifies of non-lvalue accepted. Every TypeError variant{Undeclared,Mismatch,NonBoolSpec,OldOutsideEnsures,BadModifies,ArityMismatch} has a triggering fixture + a near-miss that passes; 0 untyped nodes after pass (debug assert); no implicit coercions (negative tests); resolver idempotent on re-run.
3 — Logical expression language ## 15 Source Expr → VcFormula atoms/Pred terms: arithmetic, booleans, equality, Quant+triggers, two-state old(), pure-function application. Defines the logical semantics layer wp builds on. wk 6.5–8.5 (2 wk) Two-state old(e) mishandled (wrong pre/post snapshot) → unsoundness; quantifier var capture; partial-op (div/mod) totalization wrong. Translation total on all type-checked exprs (no panics, fuzzed 50k); old() produces a distinct pre-state symbol (asserted, diff-tested); capture-avoidance property test on nested Quant; arithmetic identities (a+0=a, a*1=a) hold via solver on golden set.
4 — IVL core + straight-line desugar ## 16 IvlCmd{Assume,Assert,Havoc,Assign,Seq,Choice} with Reason{kind,span}; lower Assign/Assert/Assume/If(→Choice of assume g/assume ¬g). Loop/call-free body → IvlCmd. wk 8.5–10 (1.5 wk) If desugar drops a branch or mis-pairs guard; every Assert must carry a correct Reason (else error mapping breaks); Seq flattening loses order. print-ivl on loop/call-free fixtures matches golden IVL (≥25); every emitted Assert has non-default Reason.kind+Reason.span (asserted, 100%); If desugar property: both branches reachable, guards complementary (fuzzed).
5 — Contracts + modular verification ## 17 Method body wrapped assume(requires)…assert(ensures); Call lowered to assert(callee.requires); havoc(modifies); assume(callee.ensures); framing of unmodified state. desugar_method. wk 10–12 (2 wk) Call lowered against body, not contract → unsound or non-modular; havoc(modifies) under-/over-approximated (frame bug); pre/post wrapping order swapped. Caller VC depends ONLY on callee signature+Spec, never body (proven by mutation test: change callee body, caller VC byte-identical); framing test: unmodified var provably unchanged across call; wrong-pre fixture → PreconditionOfCallMightNotHold; modifies-leak fixture → ModifiesViolation.
6 — Loops (invariant cut) ## 18 Invariant cut: assert(inv) entry; havoc targets; assume(inv); step [assume g; body; assert(inv); assume false]; exit assume ¬g. decreases variant for total correctness. wk 12–14.5 (2.5 wk) Loop targets mis-computed (havoc set wrong) → unsound; forgetting assume false after step → unsound; weak invariant misread as program bug (usability); non-well-founded decreases. Loop-target set = exactly assigned vars in body (diff vs hand spec, fuzzed); entry-fail fixture→LoopInvariantOnEntry; step-fail→LoopInvariantNotMaintained; non-decreasing variant→MightNotTerminate; soundness probe: a buggy loop with too-weak inv NEVER reports Verified.
7 — wp VC generation ## 19 wp(c:&IvlCmd, post:VcFormula)->VcFormula over the five rules; method VC = wp(desugar_method(m), True). VC valid ⇔ program meets spec. wk 14.5–16.5 (2 wk) Any wp rule wrong (esp. assert p→p∧Q, havoc x→∀x.Q) → unsoundness; substitution Q[e/x] capture; stack depth on deep Seq. Each of 5 wp rules unit-proven against a 2–3 cmd oracle; wp(assert false, _)≡False, wp(assume false,_)≡True (asserted); end-to-end: 20 PASS fixtures → valid VC, 20 FAIL → invalid; no false Verified on the FAIL set.
8 — Passive form (DSA) + simplify ## 20 passify(c:&IvlCmd)->IvlCmd: DSA (each var one version; Assign→Assume of equality); fresh versions across Choice; VcFormula simplification (const-fold, Imp/And flatten). Flanagan–Saxe. wk 16.5–18.5 (2 wk) DSA renaming version-merge bug at Choice join → unsoundness; simplification changes semantics; VC size still exponential if passification skipped on a path. VC size linear-ish: on n sequential Ifs, `
9 — SMT encoding ## 21 VcFormula→SmtTerm over SmtSort{Int,Bool,Uninterp,Array}; trigger annotation; SMT-LIB 2 emission (print-smt); SmtSolver trait + SmtResult{Unsat,Sat(Model),Unknown}. wk 18.5–21 (2.5 wk) Operator mis-encoding (e.g. signed div, Int↔Bool, Map→Array select/store) → unsoundness; bad/absent triggers → matching loops or Unknown; malformed SMT-LIB. print-smt output parses + type-checks in Z3 (CI gate, 0 syntax errors on full corpus); per-operator encoding has a sat/unsat oracle pair; every top-level Forall carries ≥1 Trigger (asserted); trigger-loop guard fixture stays under timeout.
10 — SMT backend integration ## 22 Discharge check(NOT VC): Unsat⇒Verified; Sat⇒Model; Unknown handled explicitly (≠ Verified); per-method push/pop incremental solving; wire #278 or Z3 behind SmtSolver. wk 21–23 (2 wk) Unknown treated as Verified → gravest unsoundness; solver nondeterminism; resource leak across push/pop; backend swap changes results. Unknown/timeout → result is not Verified (explicit MightNot…/diagnostic, negative test); two backends (#278, Z3) agree on 100% of PASS/FAIL corpus; push/pop reuses one process (no per-method spawn, asserted); per-VC solver time recorded, p50 target.
11 — Counterexample reconstruction ## 23 Parse Model; map DSA/skolem names → source vars + failing program point (Span); classify VErrorKind; render Counterexample{bindings,at}. wk 23–25 (2 wk) DSA→source name demap wrong → misleading counterexample; wrong Span/VErrorKind blamed; partial model (no value for a var). On every FAIL fixture, Counterexample.at = the actual failing source span (golden, 100%); bindings use source identifiers (Atom), not DSA versions (asserted); VErrorKind matches the injected bug class on the full FAIL corpus; missing model value rendered as ?, never crashes.
12 — Richer features ## 24 Heap/memory model (objects+fields as Map / dynamic frames); arrays/sequences with axioms; user pure functions (uninterpreted + defining axiom, well-founded recursion with fuel); algebraic datatypes. wk 25–26.5 (1.5 wk) Heap-axiom inconsistency → unsound or trivially-Verified everything; unbounded recursive-function unfolding (no fuel) → Unknown/divergence; ADT selector/constructor axioms unsound. Axiom-set consistency check: solver finds Sat for assume false-free baseline (no accidental False axiom); recursion bounded by fuel (matching-loop test stays under timeout); seq/array select(store(...)) axioms validated against oracle; each new feature adds ≥3 PASS + ≥3 FAIL fixtures.
13 — Validation (done gate) ## 25 Benchmark suite of expected-PASS + expected-FAIL programs; soundness = NEVER Verified on a buggy program; completeness tracked separately; differential vs Boogie/Dafny subset; fuzzing (parser, desugar, wp, SMT emit); VC-size + solver-time benchmarks vs targets. wk 26.5–28 (1.5 wk) A single false Verified = project failure; flaky differential oracle; fuzzers find late-stage unsoundness; perf regressions unnoticed. Soundness gate: 0/0 false Verified across the entire FAIL corpus (hard fail any single one); completeness ≥ target % of PASS corpus reported separately (Unknown ≠ fail of soundness); differential agreement with Boogie/Dafny subset = 100% on shared programs; 24 h fuzzing finds 0 panics + 0 unsoundness; VC-size + p50/p95 solver-time within Section-1 targets.

"Phase-0 done" vs "full done." Phase-0 done (end of ## 12, ~wk 1.5) means the skeleton is real, not the verifier: the seven spv-* crates build as an acyclic workspace under CI and Docker, the type shells (Program, IvlCmd, VcFormula, SmtTerm, Type, the error enums) compile, and the single end-to-end smoke path proves the pipeline is wired — spv verify on a method with assert true returns Verified while assert false returns exactly one VError{kind: AssertionMightNotHold}. No real parsing, type-checking, desugaring, wp, or SMT semantics exist yet; it is the harness, not the prover. Full done (end of ## 25, wk 28) means the validation gate is green and the cardinal property holds: the verifier parses, type-checks, desugars to IvlCmd, generates a passive-form VcFormula via wp, encodes to SMT-LIB, and discharges check(NOT VC) through the SmtSolver trait for the full source language (loops, modular calls, heap/seq/array, pure functions with fuel, datatypes) — and across the entire benchmark suite it never once reports Verified for a buggy program, treats every Unknown as not-verified, reconstructs source-level Counterexamples with the correct Span and VErrorKind for failures, agrees 100% with the Boogie/Dafny differential subset, survives 24 h of fuzzing with zero panics or unsoundness, and meets the Section-1 VC-size and solver-time targets. Soundness is the done-gate; completeness (the PASS-rate) is reported but, bounded by SMT, is not the bar.
